The approve affordance is **gated live on the workout's `delivery.changed`**, so an eased ride that's already been approved drops out of the list rather than showing a stale "Approve" (the structured data stays durable; the UI adapts to live delivery state). `PROMPT_VERSION` → `morning-analysis-v9-2026-07-11`, with a `SYSTEM_PROMPT` instruction that the actions render separately above, so the prose stays the reasoning/"why" and must not duplicate them as a checklist. **Boundaries:** **no migration** (actions ride in `context_packet["verdict"]` + `AnalysisOut`, exactly like `swapSuggestion`); verdict logic, #133, #135, and Red-never-VO2 all untouched (no verdict/executable-coaching code changed — this is a presentation layer over signals the packet already computes). **Verification (local, green):** backend pytest **582 passed / 198 skipped** (6 new pure `build_today_actions` / `_thermal_action` cases run locally — swap-then-ride ordering, Red ride detail, Green-clean degrades to empty, sleep+thermal nudges, completed-ride excluded + `max_actions` truncation, cool-room ignored; plus a `todayActions` assertion folded into the existing DB-backed Amber-swap `assemble_context_packet` case, run in CI), backend ruff check + `ruff format --check` clean, `mypy src` clean (104 files); shared typecheck + **16** tests (new `todayActionSchema` parse + default-`[]`); web `tsc` clean, full web vitest **208 passed** (6 new: `TodayActions` renders all four kinds, approves via the endpoint, applies a swap via the endpoint, drops an already-approved ride, renders nothing when empty; plus a `MorningBriefPage` case that the block leads above the coach read), web lint 0 errors (6 pre-existing Fast-Refresh warnings), build clean under Node 20. The phone eyeball of the action block on a real Amber morning is a closeout/live-use step (an LLM-written brief with a genuine pending eased ride can't be smoke-tested). Tier 🔴 High. **Shipped** (PR #111, squash `7536972`): CI went green across all 7 jobs in both push and PR-context waves plus a successful Vercel preview; squash-merged to `main` as `75369729518d15c97df27d3447642292f413b802`; production verified on the merge SHA (Railway + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both `7536972`, web `/` + `/brief` 200, unauthenticated `GET /api/v1/daily-loop` — now carrying `todayActions` — 401 direct and via Vercel). The action-block phone eyeball remains a live-use verification.

160. **Batch 87 makes every supported post-activity read check-in-first: the hourly Garmin poll syncs and nudges without generating, the generic activity-linked check-in force-dispatches inline to the ride/strength/flexibility/walk reader, and a 20:30 local backstop generates any same-day session Mark ignores.** Implemented on `feat/batch-87-checkin-first-post-workout`. **Kickoff calls:** generation is not hard-gated on subjective input — all fields remain optional and the CTA **"Read my workout"** can be submitted empty for the objective read; pending copy is **"Reading your workout…"**; the nudge is **"How did it feel? / Your {workout} is synced — check in and I will read it."** and deep-links to that activity on Home; the backstop is a same-day sweep at **20:30 Europe/London** with no age threshold. The check-in nudge uses its own `workout-check-in-{activity_id}` idempotency tag, while the existing `analysis-{activity_id}` ready push is reserved for a read generated by the backstop. `pendingPostWorkoutActivities` exposes synced/unread activities on the existing daily-loop payload so Home can render the form before an analysis exists; planned rides keep the rich outcome/adherence logger (saved before the generation-triggering check-in), while strength/flexibility/walk use a minimal RPE/feel/notes form in the existing Today non-ride seam. Every reader prompt now answers a question in the check-in notes from its packet and all four prompt versions are bumped. **Boundaries:** no migration; post-workout remains read-only; no delivery writes; verdict / #133 / #135 / Red-never-VO2 unchanged. **Shipped** through PR #112 (squash `011e342`): both CI waves passed all 7 jobs plus Vercel preview, and production smoke verified Railway + Vercel same-origin health on the merge SHA, web `/` 200, and the daily-loop auth gate 401 direct and proxied.

161. **Batch 88 gives Mark full control of a ride's structure via a free-form segment builder, and scopes a deliberate reversal of the Red-never-VO2 invariant to his explicit manual authoring path only: there the power / ramp / Red-never-VO2 gates become non-blocking warnings, while the coach/automated path keeps all three hard.** Implemented on `feat/batch-88-freeform-workout-editor` (batch row `docs/phase-batches.md`, Batch 88; full spec `docs/designs/full-control-workout-editor.md`; depends on the shipped Batch 77). **Why:** Craig wants Mark to be able to build an arbitrary multi-segment workout on any given day — beyond Batch 77's single interval-pair-or-block shape and the Today-card's whole-workout scaling — warned but never blocked. **Key finding:** the delivery format was never the limit — `expand_structured_steps` already walks an arbitrary ordered `steps` list (ramp / steady / interval-pattern, any count/order) into the IR that both the intervals.icu/Zwift push and the Batch 78 Garmin export consume; the single-pair-or-block ceiling lived only in the builder spec `CustomBikeWorkoutSpec` and its UI. So this is a builder + UI change over the same rail, not a delivery rewrite. **`/batch-start` decisions (settled with Craig):** (1) **mechanism = a `soft_gates` flag on the builder, defaulting to hard**, not a separate validation entrypoint — `build_freeform_bike_workout` is the single manual chokepoint (called only by the two manual endpoints; the coach path via `block_generator`/`vo2_progression`/`weekly_restructure`/`plan_import` never touches it), so threading `soft_gates=True` from `add_workout`/`edit_structured_workout` cleanly scopes the reversal, and the default-hard branch keeps a future non-manual caller safe (proven by test); (2) **warnings contract = `warnings: [{code, detail}]` on the `WorkoutActionEnvelope`, sibling to `errors`** (the advisory analog), codes `power_out_of_band` / `missing_ramp` / `red_vo2`, shared `apiWarningSchema` + `workoutActionResponseSchema`, structured so copy stays swappable (Batch 86 principle); (3) **live power-profile preview is in scope now** (SVG trapezoid/bar profile + running total, built from the expanded IR the engine already produces); (4) **no migration** — segments serialize into the existing `structured_workout` JSON `steps` grammar; (5) **a minimal hard floor stays even under soft warnings** — power `1-300`% FTP and positive per-segment durations and total ≤ `480` min are rejected outright (undeliverable garbage the ZWO/intervals/Garmin payloads can't represent), while sane-but-aggressive choices (power `1-44` or `151-300`, a VO2 set on a Red day, no warm-up/cool-down ramp) only warn. **Implementation (backend):** `CustomBikeWorkoutSpec`/`build_custom_bike_workout` replaced by `FreeformBikeWorkoutSpec` (ordered `WorkoutSegment` tuple, `kind ∈ ramp/steady/interval`) + `build_freeform_bike_workout(...) -> (BuiltCustomBikeWorkout, [WorkoutWarning])`, reusing the exact per-kind step emission and labelling leading/trailing ramps "Warm-up ramp"/"Cool-down ramp" so the expander's phase detection is unchanged; the power (soft-band) and ramp gates warn-or-raise by `soft_gates`. The **Red-VO2** warning is computed in the service (`PlanActionService._append_red_vo2_warning`) via the existing `blocks_red_vo2` predicate against the stored morning verdict — the delivery gates (`send_today` / `approve_adjustment` / `auto_push_due`) keep Red-never-VO2 **hard** for coach adjustments, so the reversal is strictly the workout Mark built for himself. The router body is a discriminated segment union whose pydantic bounds enforce only the absolute floor (power `1-300`, duration `1-480`), never the soft band. **Implementation (frontend):** `StructuredWorkoutSheet` rewritten as a segment-list editor (add/remove/reorder rows, per-row kind selector + fields), live client-side warnings shown as amber "Heads-up… you can still deliver it" hints (never blocking submit), a live power-profile preview + running total, and a round-trip parser from the stored `steps`; `WeekAheadPage` sends `{delivery, segments}` to the unchanged add (`customBike`) + structured-edit endpoints and surfaces server `warnings` as toasts. **Boundaries:** indoor→Zwift and outdoor→Garmin delivery paths unchanged (authoring only); completed workouts still cannot be structurally edited; verdict / #133 / #135 unchanged; Red-never-VO2 stays hard everywhere except the explicit manual authoring path. **Verification (local, green):** backend `test_plan_actions` **15 passed / 17 skipped** (DB-backed cases run in CI) — new: segment→steps mapping, arbitrary order preserved, soft gates warn (power+ramp), hard mode blocks both, absolute floor rejects power>300 + total>480 even soft, the import gate stays hard on a rampless plan, and DB-backed VO2-on-Red warns-but-still-delivers + endurance-on-Red has no warning; backend ruff check + `ruff format --check` clean, `mypy src` clean (105 files); shared typecheck + **16** tests; web `tsc` clean, full web vitest **215 passed** (6 new `StructuredWorkoutSheet` cases: default payload, add interval in order, remove+reorder, out-of-band power warns-but-submits, missing-ramp warning, cleared field blocks submit), web lint 0 errors (6 pre-existing Fast-Refresh warnings), build clean under Node 20. The phone eyeball of the segment editor + a real free-form ride reaching Zwift/Garmin is a closeout/live-use step. Tier 🔴 High. **Shipped** (PR #113, squash `13c64e5`): CI went green across all jobs in both push and PR-context waves plus a Vercel preview; squash-merged to `main` as `13c64e571ff7c356a0fac4b63749a4777dbe4f07` (one `fix(test)` follow-up after CI caught a DB-backed `add_workout` case still reading `.id` off the new `WorkoutActionResult`); production verified on the merge SHA (Railway + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both `13c64e5`, web `/` + `/delivery` 200, unauthenticated `POST /api/v1/plan-actions/days/{date}/workouts` + `.../planned-workouts/{id}/structured` 401 direct and via Vercel). The segment-editor phone eyeball + a real free-form ride reaching Zwift/Garmin remain a live-use verification.

162. **Batch 89 enables Row Level Security on the 18 `coach.*` tables migration 001 left uncovered, as defense-in-depth — no policies, guarded so it only applies on Supabase.** Implemented on `fix/batch-89-coach-rls` (batch row `docs/phase-batches.md`, Batch 89). **Why:** the 2026-07-11 full-app QA review (`QA_REVIEW_2026-07-11.md`, Finding 1) found the Supabase security advisor raising a **critical `rls_disabled`** lint — migration 001 only put the five auth/notification tables (`profiles`, `refresh_tokens`, `push_subscriptions`, `notification_preferences`, `audit_log`) under RLS, so every table added in migrations 002-014 (all of Mark's health data) was left with RLS off. This Postgres is shared with a separate public-facing movie app whose anon key ships in its frontend, which raises the stakes. **Key finding (verified while scoping):** the `anon` and `authenticated` roles currently hold **no grants** on the `coach` schema (`has_schema_privilege('anon','coach','USAGE') = false`, and `false` for SELECT/INSERT on the tables), and the REST Data API is even egress-restricted (402) — so nothing is actually reachable today. The exposure is therefore **latent**, not open: the real fix is a backstop so a future stray `GRANT` or an "Exposed schemas" change can't leak data, plus clearing the advisor. **Design:** enable RLS with **no policies**. The FastAPI backend connects as the `postgres` **owner** role, which bypasses RLS (no `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`) — proven by the fact that `profiles` reads work in prod today despite 001's `auth.uid()`-scoped policies (a non-owner would be denied when `auth.uid()` is NULL). So RLS-with-no-policy is deny-all for `anon`/`authenticated` and a no-op for the app, exactly the posture `audit_log` already has. **Implementation:** migration `015_coach_rls.py` holds an `RLS_TABLES` constant (the 17 remaining coach model tables + `alembic_version`) and emits `ALTER TABLE coach.<t> ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` for each, wrapped in migration 001's exact `IF EXISTS (SELECT FROM information_schema.schemata WHERE schema_name = 'auth')` guard so it runs only on Supabase and is a no-op on plain Postgres — keeping CI's `migration-check` (upgrade head → downgrade base) and the unit-test Postgres green; downgrade disables. A pure regression test (`tests/test_coach_rls_migration.py`) asserts 001's set ∪ 015's set equals the coach model tables on `Base.metadata`, so a future model table shipping without RLS fails the test. **Boundaries:** no schema/data change beyond the RLS flag; no policies (owner-bypass model); verdict / #133 / #135 / Red-never-VO2 untouched; the auth tables' existing policies unchanged. **Verification (local, green):** new test 3 passed; backend pytest **598 passed / 200 skipped**; ruff check + `ruff format --check` clean; `mypy src` clean (106 files); migration chain single-head `015` and the offline-rendered upgrade SQL confirmed as the guarded 18-table `ENABLE` block. Tier 🟢 Mid. **Shipped** (PR #114, squash `cae3888`): Craig made the repo public → the GitHub Actions billing block cleared, both CI waves went green across all 7 jobs (incl. `migration-check` on real Postgres) plus a Vercel preview; squash-merged to `main` as `cae3888adb4a8e72e4869c5c59a79ed0e521cd1b`. **Production verified on the merge SHA:** Railway + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both `cae3888`, web `/` 200, unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` 401 (app up + auth intact — RLS did not break the app); migration 015 applied on Railway startup → **RLS ON for all 23 `coach` tables (0 off)**, and the Supabase **critical `rls_disabled` advisory cleared** (coach tables now report only the benign INFO `rls_enabled_no_policy`, confirming deny-all with the owner-bypass intact). Separately surfaced for Craig (not part of this batch): the shared project's REST Data API is egress-restricted (402, `exceed_egress_quota`) — read-pressure stats point at the co-hosted movie app full-scanning its public tables (`movies`/`movie_credits`/`collection_items` show `idx_scan=0`).

163. **Batch 90 makes the post-mobility read weekday-correct, holiday-aware, and explicit that Mark's daily mobility is his baseline habit — without letting mobility advice become a whole-day recovery verdict.** Implemented on `feat/batch-90-post-mobility-read` (full spec `docs/designs/post-mobility-read-accuracy.md`). **Kickoff decisions:** (1) supply authoritative `subjectWeekday` to all five direct LLM reads (morning, ride, walk, strength, mobility), with a prompt rule never to derive it; (2) give mobility a 14-day actual-plan horizon plus overlapping holiday windows, annotating each planned row `insideHolidayWindow`/`isLive` so skipped holiday VO2 is visibly non-live; (3) express daily mobility through packet/prompt framing over the already-observed consistency signal — no KB re-seed and no rewrite of cycling `weeklyRhythm`; (4) repair the identical empty `analysisRules` lookup systemically in ride/walk/strength/mobility by exposing seeded `data_quality_rules` + `coaching_protocol`, while mobility also receives `training_schedule`. The mobility prompt bans bonus/overshoot/load framing, keeps its one next step within mobility, and preserves `advisoryOnly`/`neverFeedsRecoveryDecision`. Prompt versions bump for every changed reader. **Boundaries:** no migration, KB write, frontend/shared change, verdict/delivery change, or recovery-decision input; #133 / #135 / Red-never-VO2 untouched. **Verification:** backend pytest **598 passed / 200 skipped**, ruff + format clean, mypy clean (105 files); packet tests cover the known Saturday, holiday-suppressed VO2, baseline contract, real schedule/rules, and advisory-only rule. **Shipped** in PR #115 (squash `43d4507`): all seven CI jobs passed in both push and PR-context waves plus Vercel preview; Railway + Vercel same-origin health served the merge SHA, web `/` returned 200, and the daily-loop auth gate returned 401 direct and proxied. The next real post-mobility wording eyeball remains a live-use verification.

164. **Batch 91 makes the morning brief state bed/wake, the date, and Mark's check-in in his own local, human terms — local wall-clock times beside the `*Utc` fields, an authoritative header-date label, and the check-in word — so the read never prints an hour-behind UTC time, re-derives the wrong date, or surfaces the "subjective feel 6" he never recognised giving.** Implemented on `feat/batch-91-brief-read-fidelity` (batch row `docs/phase-batches.md`, Batch 91; surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-12 feedback). **Why:** the packet fed the model naive-UTC bed/wake (`sleepStartUtc`), only `subjectDate`/`subjectWeekday` for the header, and the raw `subjectiveScore` int — so a BST night (`00:17Z`) could read as 00:17 not 01:17, the date could drift to "13 July" when the model re-derived it, and the one-tap "OK" check-in came back as "subjective feel 6". **Kickoff decision (settled with Craig at `/batch-start`):** the check-in score→word map is done **in the packet, not as a prompt instruction** — a deterministic, unit-testable field consistent with Batch 90/91's "supply it, never let the model derive it" philosophy; the raw `subjectiveScore` stays for the deterministic verdict. **Implementation (backend-only, `services/morning_analysis.py`):** (1) `_sleep_packet` now takes `player.timezone` and adds `sleepStartLocal`/`sleepEndLocal` — the naive-UTC bed/wake rendered as the user's local wall-clock `HH:MM` via `_local_clock` (`.replace(tzinfo=UTC).astimezone(ZoneInfo(tz))`, unknown-zone → UTC fallback), **alongside** the untouched `*Utc` fields; (2) a top-level `subjectDateLabel` (`_date_label` → e.g. "Sunday 12 July 2026", built without the platform-specific `%-d` for portability) is supplied beside `subjectWeekday`; (3) `subjective_score_label` (anchors from the frontend one-tap scale `CheckInPage.tsx` `OVERALL_OPTIONS`: 2=Rough, 4=Meh, 6=OK, 8=Good, 10=Great; off-scale legacy values fall to the nearest band; `None`→`None`) adds `subjectiveLabel` to both `manualEntries[]` and the verdict block. **Prompt (91.2/91.3):** `SYSTEM_PROMPT` extends Batch 90's authoritative-`subjectWeekday` rule to `subjectDateLabel` (never re-derive/reformat the date) and time-of-day (state bed/wake from `sleepStartLocal`/`sleepEndLocal`, never print a `*Utc` timestamp or convert one), and instructs the read to refer to the check-in by `subjectiveLabel` (e.g. "you said you felt OK"), never the raw `subjectiveScore`/"6/10" term; three `outputRules` tokens added; `PROMPT_VERSION` → `morning-analysis-v11-2026-07-12` (regenerates stale reads via the existing staleness check). Because the whole `context_packet` is JSON-dumped to the model, the packet keeps both the structured `*Utc`/`subjectiveScore` fields and the human local/label fields, and the prompt rules are the guard against leaking the former into rendered prose. **Boundaries:** **no migration**, no frontend change (the score→word anchors are duplicated from `OVERALL_OPTIONS`, which Batch 94 will unify end-to-end), no shared-schema change (the new fields are internal packet fields the model reads, not surfaced on `AnalysisOut`); the deterministic `_morning_verdict` still reads `subjective_score` exactly as before, so Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, and Red-never-VO2 are untouched. **Verification (local, green):** backend pytest **604 passed / 200 skipped** (6 new pure cases — BST+GMT DST localization with `*Utc` retained, missing-time/bad-zone tolerance, the portable date label, the score→word map incl. off-scale + `None`, the manual-entry packet carrying both score and word, and a prompt-rule regression that the read is told to use local times/label and never print a `*Utc` timestamp or the raw score; the DB-backed main packet test also asserts the GMT-night local clock, `subjectDateLabel`, and both `subjectiveLabel`s, and runs in CI), backend ruff check + `ruff format --check` clean, `mypy src` clean (105 files). The phone eyeball of the next real morning brief (local bed/wake time, correct date, "OK" wording) is a closeout/live-use step — an LLM-written brief can't be smoke-tested. Tier 🔴 High. **Shipped** (PR #116, squash `5fa2f05`): both CI waves passed all 7 jobs in push and PR-context plus a Vercel preview; squash-merged to `main` as `5fa2f05535e90c623826746d4fc6ed7294fa8afb`. **Production verified on the merge SHA:** Railway + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both `5fa2f05`, web `/` + `/check-in` 200, unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` 401 direct and via Vercel (the morning brief rides in that auth-gated payload; no new endpoint, no migration). The next real morning-brief phone eyeball (local bed/wake, correct date, "OK" wording) remains a live-use verification.

165. **Batch 92 makes the morning thermal read describe the room while Mark was actually asleep and credit a genuine pre-cool as separate evidence.** Implemented on `feat/batch-92-overnight-thermal-window` (batch row `docs/phase-batches.md`, Batch 92; surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-12 feedback). **Why:** morning analysis queried a private fixed 18:00→08:00 window, so an 18:1x reading of 24.05°C became the claimed overnight peak even though the sleep-period peak was ~20.2°C; the `/bedroom` chart separately used 21:30→09:00, and the pre-bed 18.64°C low could only produce `precool_target_missed`. **Kickoff decision:** keep the shared 21:30→09:00 chart window as the observation/query envelope and fallback, then expose `preCoolLowC`, `sleepOnsetC`, and `preCoolDropC` as the explicit pre-bed signal beside sleep-only `indoorPeakC`/`indoorLowC`/`indoorLastC`; a material ≥1°C drop or a pre-cool low at/below the 19.5°C disruption threshold earns `precool_credited` and suppresses `precool_target_missed`. This credits effective cooling even when the strict 17°C target was not reached or most of the earlier drop occurred before the chart window. **Implementation:** `MorningAnalysisService._overnight_temperature_rows` now calls `bedroom_overnight.night_window(subject_date - 1, timezone)` with the same inclusive-start/exclusive-end bounds as `/bedroom`; `_thermal_review(..., sleep=...)` partitions those rows at Garmin's `sleep_start_utc`/`sleep_end_utc`, uses only asleep rows for the indoor peak/low/last and thermal-disruption flags, and falls back to the full shared night window if sleep times are absent/invalid. The prompt and `outputRules` require the read to treat the pre-cool fields separately and explicitly credit `precool_credited`; `PROMPT_VERSION` → `morning-analysis-v12-2026-07-12`. **Boundaries:** backend-only, **no migration**, no frontend/shared change; thermal stays advisory/environmental and does not feed `_morning_verdict`, so Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, and Red-never-VO2 are untouched. **Verification (local, green):** backend pytest **607 passed / 200 skipped**; new pure cases prove a 24.05°C pre-bed spike cannot become the sleep peak (20.2°C), the 18.64°C pre-cool is surfaced/credited without a miss, missing-sleep fallback, and shared chart/morning date-window semantics; backend ruff + format-check clean; touched-file mypy clean. Full `mypy src` still has the existing unrelated `pydreo` missing-stub/import issue in `services/dreo_fan.py`. Tier 🔴 High. **Shipped** (PR #117, squash `8ae3736`): both GitHub CI waves passed all seven jobs plus Vercel preview; Railway + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` served the merge SHA, web `/` returned 200, and unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` returned 401 direct and proxied. The next genuinely warm-night generated brief remains a live-use wording check; closeout did not mutate production analysis data to manufacture it.

166. **Batch 93 makes the age-norm sleep surface honest for restless moments: descriptive-only means no fabricated nightly "average".** Implemented on `feat/batch-93-restless-honesty` (batch row `docs/phase-batches.md`, Batch 93; surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-12 feedback). **Why:** `services/age_norms.py` correctly documented `restless_moments_count` as Garmin-proprietary context with "no defensible population band", but the descriptive-only branch still emitted the `_Norm.averages` lookup as `ageAverage=13` for a 50–59 male, which the Sleep page then had to hide with a dash. That left the packet internally dishonest and contradicted the module's own stated rule. **Decision:** keep restless as a context-only row, not a newly-derived per-hour norm for this batch. The honest fix is to emit `ageAverage=null` for descriptive-only metrics, widen the shared/web contract to accept that null, and preserve the explicit descriptor "Shown for context — no age range." *Why:* it is the narrow additive change the batch asked for; it removes a made-up number without introducing a second, more interpretive restless metric. A per-hour Garmin-guidance reference can still be a future batch if Craig wants it. **Implementation:** `AgeComparisonRow.age_average` is now nullable; `_build_rows(...)` sets `age_average=None` for `norm.descriptive_only`; the shared `ageComparisonRowSchema` and the web `AgeComparisonRow` type now accept nullable `ageAverage`, with `MetricComparisonTable` defensively handling null. `SleepStageAgeTable` already used `bandLow`/`bandHigh` to render the dash, so the UI behaviour stays honest by contract rather than by omission. **No-ripple guard:** `restless_moments_count` remains absent from `_SLEEP_BANDS`, `sleep_stage_band("restless_moments_count", ...)` returns `None`, and `classify_sleep_stage(...)` returns `None`, so Batch 61's age-adjusted sleep score keeps reading only REM/Deep/Light/Awake and is unchanged. **Boundaries:** no migration; no prompt/verdict change; #133, #135, and Red-never-VO2 untouched. **Verification (local, green):** targeted backend pytest **24 passed** (`test_age_norms.py`, `test_sleep_scoring.py`), targeted web vitest **6 passed** (`SleepStageAgeTable.test.tsx`), web production build clean, backend ruff clean, touched-file mypy clean. Tier 🟢 Mid.

167. **Batch 94 keeps the morning feel check-in as a one-tap word scale, renames it to the concept Mark recognises, and echoes the same word beside the verdict instead of ever surfacing the hidden 0–10 number.** Implemented on `main` (batch row `docs/phase-batches.md`, Batch 94; surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-12 feedback). **Why:** Batch 91 fixed the brief wording, but the input itself still said "Overall" and Home did not echo the saved feel near the verdict, so Mark could still miss where he had told the app how he felt. Reverting to a visible numeric 1–10 field would undo Batch 63's one-tap speed and reintroduce the exact number-vocabulary confusion the earlier fix removed. **Decision:** keep the stored contract and the chip values exactly as they are (`subjectiveScore` 2/4/6/8/10), but make the *surface vocabulary* explicit and shared: rename the quick label to **How you feel today**, centralise the frontend score→word mapping in one helper, and render the saved word directly under the verdict (`You said: OK ...`) with an inline path back to `/check-in`. *Why:* this is the narrowest additive fix that makes the concept recognisable end-to-end without any backend or schema churn. **Implementation:** new `apps/web/src/lib/subjectiveFeel.ts` exports the canonical Rough/Meh/OK/Good/Great options plus a nearest-band label helper; `CheckInPage.tsx` now consumes that shared list and labels the quick scale `How you feel today`; `DashboardPage.tsx` adds a compact verdict-adjacent recap using the same helper and a `Change` link to `/check-in`; tests lock that the UI never shows the raw number and that the recap round-trips the word vocabulary. **Boundaries:** no API change, no migration, no shared-schema change; the manual-entry write path still posts `subjectiveScore` unchanged; Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, and Red-never-VO2 untouched. **Verification (local, green):** targeted web vitest **44 passed** (`CheckInPage.test.tsx`, `DashboardPage.test.tsx`), web production build clean, web lint clean aside from 6 pre-existing Fast Refresh warnings in shared UI/context files. Tier 🟢 Mid.


168. **Batch 95 gives Home a first-class pre-brief state so the "Say good morning" hero, the Next-action strip, and Sleep's check-in prompt all agree before today's brief exists.** Implemented on `feat/batch-95-pre-checkin-home` (batch row `docs/phase-batches.md`, Batch 95; surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-12 walkthrough). **Why:** since Batch 85 the hero (`GoodMorningCta`) already shows "Say good morning" whenever `morningAnalysis` is null, but the Next-action strip's morning ladder (`homeActions.ts`) required `morningAnalysis != null` for its first rung and fell through everything else to "You're all set" — contradicting the hero directly beneath it. Separately, `rest_day`'s phase-primary section is `lastNight`, so a pre-brief rest morning auto-expanded the raw sleep comparison table before any coached read existed. **Kickoff decision (95.1/95.2 — "new primary override vs. a guard in the existing primary selection"): a guard in the existing selection, not a new override mechanism.** `nextAction`'s existing action-override pattern (`actionSection(action) ?? primarySection(...)`) already covers 95.1 for free — a new `say-good-morning` rung (first check inside `if (isMorning)`, firing on `morningAnalysis == null`) slots into that pattern with no structural change. 95.2 only needed a narrow guard at the `primary` computation in `DashboardPage.tsx` (`analysis == null && phase === 'rest_day' ? 'today' : primarySection(...)`) rather than teaching `primarySection` about brief-state, since `pre_training`'s default is already `today` and only `rest_day` was wrong. **Implementation:** (1) `homeActions.ts` — `nextAction`'s morning ladder gains a `say-good-morning` rung (`to: '/check-in'`) ahead of the sleep-review rung, firing whenever no brief exists; outside the morning phase the ladder is unchanged (the hero still shows regardless of phase, but the day/evening ladders keep their own priorities). (2) `DashboardPage.tsx` — the `primary` section computation guards `rest_day` pre-brief to `today`, so `lastNight` renders collapsed to its one-line summary instead of auto-expanding. (3) `SleepPage.tsx` — reuses `GoodMorningCta` at the top of the page when `manualEntry == null` (pt 4), linking through the same `/check-in` route as Home and the page's existing footer check-in card. **Boundaries:** frontend-only, no API/shared-schema/migration change; Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, Red-never-VO2 untouched. **Verification (local, green):** targeted web vitest **69 passed** (`homeActions.test.ts`, `DashboardPage.test.tsx`, `SleepPage.test.tsx`, incl. new coverage for the pre-brief rung's precedence over a pending eased ride, the rest-day lastNight collapse, and Sleep's check-in CTA presence/absence), full web vitest **222 passed**, `tsc` clean, web lint clean aside from the pre-existing 6 Fast Refresh warnings + 1 pre-existing `tailwind.config.ts` require-import error (untouched by this batch), web production build clean under Node 20. Tier 🟢 Mid. **Shipped** (PR #118, squash `7e9770c`): both CI waves passed all 7 jobs in push and PR-context plus a Vercel preview; squash-merged to `main` as `7e9770c9ffa18d8e3f4df41ae7be2a10823b684c`. **Production verified on the merge SHA:** Railway + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both `7e9770c`, web `/` returned 200, and unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` returned 401 direct and via Vercel. The phone eyeball of the reset pre-check-in Home remains a live-use verification.


169. **Batch 96 gives Home a first-class unviewed-brief state, and stops offering to regenerate a brief that already exists.** Implemented on `feat/batch-96-brief-lifecycle-cta` (batch row `docs/phase-batches.md`, Batch 96; second of the 2026-07-12 walkthrough's Batches 95-102 sequence, builds on Batch 95's brief-state model). **Why:** once today's brief lands it competed for attention with `TodayActions`' thermal/plan nudges (e.g. "Pre-cool the bedroom") rather than leading, and `/check-in`'s primary button kept reading "Get today's brief" even after a brief existed, so tapping it again silently regenerated the read instead of just opening it (pt 8, pt 9). **Implementation:** a new per-day client flag (`apps/web/src/lib/briefReview.ts`, `hasReviewedBrief`/`markBriefReviewed`) mirrors Batch 60's `sleepReview.ts` pattern exactly — keyed by subject date, set when `MorningBriefPage` mounts with a loaded analysis, degrading safely to "not reviewed" on storage failure. `DashboardPage.tsx` renders a new `UnviewedBriefCta` immediately above `TodayActions` whenever `analysis != null && !hasReviewedBrief(subjectDate)`, so the unread brief outranks every action card including the thermal nudge inside it; opening `/brief` clears the flag on the next render. `CheckInPage.tsx`'s primary button now branches on `briefExists` (`brief` local state from a fresh save, or `data.morningAnalysis` already on the loaded snapshot) — once true it renders "View brief" linking to `/brief` instead of the submit button, so there is no path left that re-triggers generation once a brief is present. **Boundaries:** frontend-only, no API/shared-schema/migration change; Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, Red-never-VO2 untouched; the check-in form itself (manual-entry fields) is unchanged — only the button below it branches. **Verification (local, green):** targeted web vitest **51 passed** (`DashboardPage.test.tsx`, `CheckInPage.test.tsx`, `MorningBriefPage.test.tsx`, incl. new coverage for the CTA leading `today-actions` in document order, the CTA clearing once reviewed, the brief page setting the flag on open, and the check-in button reading "View brief" both post-generation and when a brief is already on the loaded snapshot), full web vitest **226 passed**, `tsc` clean, web lint clean on the touched files, web production build clean under Node 20. Tier 🟢 Mid. **Shipped** (PR #119, squash `f7c4b16`): both CI waves passed all 7 jobs in push and PR-context plus a Vercel preview; squash-merged to `main` as `f7c4b1630b89e7af2757f502cabacca948cf8fc3`. **Production verified on the merge SHA:** Railway + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both `f7c4b16`, web `/` returned 200, and unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` returned 401 direct and via Vercel. The phone eyeball of the CTA/button-state change remains a live-use verification.

170. **Batch 97 makes the morning brief generate passively after check-in: the save returns immediately, the server finishes the brief off-request, and the user is pulled back by a ready notification while the page still has an in-app fallback.** Implemented on `main` from Batch 97 (`docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-12 walkthrough). **Why:** Batch 85 had already moved morning generation onto the check-in, but it still held the request open behind "Reading your morning…", making the user wait on the LLM in place. The walkthrough's desired behaviour was closer to Domino's-tracker ergonomics: save, leave, get notified when the read is ready. **Kickoff decision (97.4): use an in-process FastAPI `BackgroundTasks` job, not a new job row or scheduler queue, and use timed staged progress on the client rather than fake backend progress.** The existing seam was already narrow and durable: `DailyLoopService.upsert_manual_entry(...)` commits the check-in, `ExecutableCoachingService.regenerate_after_morning_checkin(...)` already encapsulates the force-regenerate + downgrade-only / never-touch-an-approved-ride guardrails, and `NudgeAlertService` already owns one-per-day push idempotency. A new queue table or polling API would add schema/surface area for a private single-user app without changing the real reliability profile, while "real progress" would be synthetic because the backend has no stage-level job telemetry. **Implementation:** `PUT /api/v1/daily-loop/{date}/manual-entry` now queues `_generate_brief_after_checkin(...)` only for today's local date; that helper opens its own `AsyncSessionLocal`, reloads the active profile, runs the existing regeneration service with `commit=False`, then sends a new one-per-day `brief-ready-{date}` push via `NudgeAlertService.push_brief_ready(...)` before one final commit. The new push deep-links to `/brief` (same transport/idempotency pattern as the verdict/workout pushes) and reuses the brief's first reason as the one-line body when available. On the web, `CheckInPage.tsx` keeps the same write endpoint and daily-loop query but swaps the blocking spinner for an immediate acknowledgement + a three-stage waiting card ("Syncing your overnight data" → "Reading your morning" → "Writing your brief"), with the stages driven by local time windows, not backend job state. **Fallback decision:** when push is denied, missed, or delayed, the page itself polls the normal daily-loop snapshot and offers a manual refresh button — no new API shape, no migration. **Known dependency carried forward:** the ready push now includes `data.url="/brief"`, but focused-client navigation on iOS PWA still needs Batch **99**; until then the cold-start deep-link works and the in-app fallback covers a missed in-place route jump. **Boundaries:** no migration; no verdict logic change; #133, #135, and Red-never-VO2 untouched. **Verification (local, green):** backend pytest **22 passed / 13 skipped** (`test_nudge_alerts.py`, `test_daily_loop.py`), backend `ruff check` clean; web `CheckInPage.test.tsx` **8 passed**; web `tsc --noEmit` clean; web lint clean on touched files aside from 6 pre-existing Fast Refresh warnings in unrelated shared files. **Shipped:** implementation commit `0310cd6ae40c142e301b6c6af8581e48f3ae05ee` was pushed to `main`; GitHub Actions run `29210486906` then passed all 7 jobs; production verification on that SHA showed Railway + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both serving `0310cd6`, web `/check-in` + `/brief` returning 200, and unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` returning 401 via Vercel. The real ready-push-on-device proof remains deferred to live use, and Batch 99 is the focused-client iOS deep-link follow-on. Tier 🟢 Mid.

171. **Batch 98 treats an explicit holiday or a non-empty all-skipped plan as a rest day, while keeping recovery colour and live-workout truth separate.** Implemented on `feat/batch-98-rest-day-verdict` (batch row `docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-12 walkthrough). **Why:** the morning packet had the skipped workout row but no holiday/rest-day concept, and `_plan_adjustments` checked only whether any row existed, so a Green holiday morning could say "Proceed with the planned workout" for an endurance ride already paused by Batch 15. **Kickoff decision:** reuse Batch 90.2's `HolidayPauseService.get_windows(...)` loader **and** derive rest when the day's non-empty active-row set is entirely `status='skipped'`. The holiday signal protects against a stale/unversioned plan row; the all-skipped signal protects against a missing or historical window. An empty plan remains unknown/conservative rather than being silently declared intentional rest. **Implementation:** the morning packet gains `restDay {isRestDay, reason, insideHolidayWindow, allPlannedWorkoutsSkipped, holidayWindows}`; `_morning_verdict` preserves the existing Green/Amber/Red recovery classification but adds `dayType='rest'`, excludes skipped/completed work from live VO2, and emits one rest-day adjustment. Rest days bypass swap-first, eased-ride Today actions, hard-prior-day easing copy, and weekly-mix shortfall/re-patch prose. The prompt bans treating skipped/holiday sessions as live and `PROMPT_VERSION` moves to `morning-analysis-v13-2026-07-12`. **Boundaries:** no migration, frontend/shared change, or change to #133/#135/Red-never-VO2; the colour remains a recovery read, not permission to train. **Verification:** targeted morning/weekly-mix pytest 39 passed / 9 local DB skips; Ruff check + format and touched-service mypy clean. The first two CI runs exposed test-fixture-only profile-order and seeded-version collisions; after correction, both final CI waves passed all seven jobs with 813/813 backend tests plus the Vercel preview. **Shipped:** PR #120 squash-merged as `a0e133dc5b7b7ccc15b7f483da8e47f7911fcf03`; Railway + Vercel same-origin health matched the merge SHA, web `/` + `/brief` returned 200, and unauthenticated daily-loop returned 401 direct/proxied. The genuine rest/holiday wording eyeball remains live-use verification. Tier 🔴 High.

172. **Batch 99 routes warm notification taps through the app shell, not `WindowClient.navigate()`: the service worker posts a normalized same-origin URL to the focused client, the SPA listens on `navigator.serviceWorker.message` and calls `react-router` navigation, and cold-start taps still use `openWindow(...)`.** Implemented on `feat/batch-99-ios-notification-deeplink` (batch row `docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-12 walkthrough). **Why:** the walkthrough confirmed the exact iOS PWA failure mode: tapping the good-morning push while the app was already open just resurfaced the last-viewed screen. `sw.ts` focused an existing `WindowClient` and called `client.navigate(url)`, but on iOS PWAs that call is a no-op, so the route never changed. **Decision:** keep the fix entirely inside the existing frontend + push-payload seam. A focused client should receive a typed `postMessage` with the target URL and let the already-mounted SPA router own the in-app transition; `clients.openWindow(url)` remains the cold-start fallback when no window is open. URLs are normalized to same-origin path/query/hash before either path, and unrelated service-worker messages are ignored. *Why:* this is the narrow additive fix the batch asked for; it solves the focused-client iOS path without inventing a second notification endpoint, changing payload shape, or relying on browser-specific `navigate()` behavior. **Implementation:** new shared web helper `apps/web/src/lib/pushNavigation.ts` centralizes URL normalization, typed message construction, and the focused-client-vs-cold-start branching; `apps/web/src/sw.ts` now delegates `notificationclick` to that helper instead of calling `WindowClient.navigate(...)`; new app-shell `PushNavigationController` listens for service-worker `message` events inside `BrowserRouter` and routes them with `useNavigate`. Existing payload builders already met the batch's deep-link requirement and remain unchanged: good-morning targets `/check-in`, brief-ready targets `/brief`, and verdict push keeps `/`. **Boundaries:** frontend + push-payload only; **no migration**, no backend endpoint change, no verdict / #133 / #135 / Red-never-VO2 change. **Verification (local, green):** backend `test_nudge_alerts.py` **22 passed / 3 skipped** confirms payload `data.url`; new web regression `pushNavigation.test.tsx` **5 passed** covers same-origin normalization, focused-client `postMessage`, cold-start `openWindow`, SPA routing on message, and ignoring unrelated messages; web `tsc --noEmit` and production build both pass. Tier 🟢 Mid.

173. **Batch 100 promotes the fan surface from one anonymous bedroom-fan blob to a stable per-fan list keyed by Dreo identity, with the overnight auto-target staying pinned separately.** Implemented on `main` from Batch 100 (`docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-12 walkthrough). **Kickoff decisions:** (a) **identity = Dreo's own serial + device label/model, not a new user-labelled table or profile JSON.** The app now needs to show and target more than one controllable fan, but adding a rename layer would be a brand-new state model for a private single-user app; Dreo already gives a stable serial and human label, so reuse it directly. (b) **The overnight auto-target remains the configured `DREO_DEVICE_SN` (or the first controllable fan when unset), even if a manual command temporarily targets another fan.** Manual control must not silently move the scheduler's bedroom-fan ownership. (c) **"Next on" is a concrete local clock time (`21:30`), not a vague "until tonight".** That answers Mark's walkthrough question directly and falls straight out of the existing overnight window. (d) **This batch intentionally supersedes Decision #97's "daily-loop fan read stays cloud-free" on this one surface.** Multi-fan identity, oscillation, and per-fan targeting are not available from the old computed-intent-only blob; the daily-loop fan list now does a best-effort live Dreo inventory/state read, then merges the existing overnight intent onto the auto-target fan and falls back to the old single-fan synthetic payload if Dreo is unavailable. **Why:** once Mark has multiple fans, a single `thermalState.fan` object cannot tell him which device is which or safely target commands. Using Dreo's own identity keeps the change additive and migration-free, while a best-effort live read is the smallest way to surface per-fan mode/speed/oscillation honestly.**

174. **Batch 101 gives the room/fan surface its own primary `Climate` tab, while Sleep keeps only the sleep-context summary and the handoff link into it.** Implemented on the current branch from Batch 101 (`docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-12 walkthrough). **Kickoff decisions:** (a) **Name the tab `Climate`, not `Environment`.** The bottom bar needs a short, plain word; `Climate` is clearer in one glance for Mark than the broader `Environment`, and it matches what the surface actually owns: room temperature, fan control, and overnight weather context. (b) **Make it a top-level tab, not a `More` destination.** The multi-fan surface is now a recurring nightly control surface, not an occasional settings page, so burying it under `More` would hide a real day-to-day action area. Four primary tabs plus `More` stays legible on the phone while keeping Sleep focused on the sleep narrative. (c) **Keep Sleep as the hybrid summary surface.** The full `BedroomBody` controls move to the new `/environment` route, but Sleep's Tonight view keeps a concise thermal stats card plus one clear next action (`Open Climate`) so the bedtime story is still in one place. (d) **Retire `/bedroom` to the new tab, not back to Sleep.** Once Climate owns the live room/fan surface, the legacy bedroom route should land on the new home for that functionality. **Why:** Batch 100 made the climate surface materially larger by introducing multiple real fans and richer telemetry; giving it its own primary home avoids overloading Sleep without stripping out the sleep-relevant thermal context. This keeps the UI additive, frontend-only, and easy to understand.

175. **Batch 102 ships the personal-status line and Tonight transparency together as one small frontend polish pass: Home sounds more like a coach, and the evening card says what it knows so far.** Implemented on the current branch from Batch 102 (`docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-12 walkthrough). **Kickoff decision (102.3): ship 102.1 and 102.2 together, not as two tiny branches.** They touch different visible surfaces, but both are additive copy/presentation work on already-stable frontend seams, and 102.2 already has a shared rendering boundary (`SleepPrepBody`) used by both Home and Sleep. Splitting them would add review/closeout overhead without buying risk reduction, while shipping them together keeps the "warmth + honesty" trust theme intact. **Implementation:** `apps/web/src/lib/copy.ts` adds a deterministic `personalStatusLine(...)` helper keyed off the current time of day and verdict, so Home's top lockup now reads as a concise coach-like sentence (`Good morning, Mark. You're good to go this morning.`) instead of a bare greeting. `apps/web/src/components/SleepPrepBody.tsx` now derives a plain-English basis line from the existing projection contract: personalized projections explicitly say they are based on today's training, measured sleep drivers, and the room right now / overnight forecast when those signals are present; fallback projections explicitly say the app is still on the standard routine because it does not yet have enough personal signal to move off the default plan. Because that logic lives inside `SleepPrepBody`, the same transparency line appears consistently on both Home's Tonight card and the Sleep page's Tonight tab. **Boundaries:** frontend-only; no API or shared-schema change, no migration, no change to the Batch 46 sleep-projection model, and no change to Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, or Red-never-VO2. **Verification (local, green):** focused web vitest **47 passed** (`DashboardPage.test.tsx`, `SleepPage.test.tsx`, including the new personal-status assertion and the Tonight basis-line assertions for both the rich personalized and simpler training-only cases), web `tsc --noEmit` clean, and web production build clean under Node 20. Tier 🟢 Mid.

176. **Batch 103 fully gates the pre-check-in sleep surfaces: before a check-in or a generated brief exists, Home shows one `GoodMorningCta` and no raw sleep section, and `/sleep` shows only that same hero.** Implemented on `feat/batch-103-pre-checkin-gating` (batch row `docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-13 walkthrough). **Kickoff decision (103.3): choose the stricter gate, not a headline-only compromise.** Batch 95 had already fixed discoverability, but the walkthrough showed the remaining pre-brief problem was *competing sleep content*: Home still carried `lastNight` under "More detail", the Next strip repeated the hero CTA, and `/sleep` exposed the whole last-night/tonight/climate body while still asking him to check in. Showing even a minimal sleep headline on those surfaces keeps splitting attention between "say good morning" and "read this sleep data", so the tighter interpretation better matches the batch goal of one clear morning action. **Implementation:** `DashboardPage.tsx` now suppresses the `NextActionStrip` while `morningAnalysis == null` and removes `lastNight` from the visible Home sections until a brief exists, leaving the hero as the sole CTA while `today` still leads. `SleepPage.tsx` now derives `hasSleepAccess = manualEntry != null || morningAnalysis != null`; until that unlocks, it renders only `PageHeader` + `GoodMorningCta`, with the tabs, last-night cards, tonight/climate cards, and the optional footer check-in card all withheld. Once a check-in or brief exists, the existing Sleep experience is unchanged. **Boundaries:** frontend-only; no API/shared-schema/migration change; no verdict / #133 / #135 / Red-never-VO2 change; Batch 60's "check-in is optional" still holds after the morning surface is unlocked because the footer check-in card remains available in the unlocked Sleep view. **Verification (local, green):** targeted web vitest **68 passed** (`DashboardPage.test.tsx`, `SleepPage.test.tsx`, `homeSections.test.ts`), web lint clean aside from the 6 pre-existing Fast Refresh warnings in unrelated files, and web production build clean under Node 20. Tier 🟢 Mid.

177. **Batch 104 completes the Sleep/Climate split by moving each retrospective or habit surface to the page that matches its job: the overnight chart stays on Sleep, Climate stays live-only, breathwork belongs with Tonight, and walking base belongs with Trends.** Implemented on `feat/batch-104-right-surface-placement` (batch row `docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-13 walkthrough). **Kickoff decisions (104.2/104.3):** (a) **Walking base moves to Trends, not Home or Sleep.** It is a rolling baseline-credit read, not a same-day workout action or a sleep-specific lever, so the long-range page is the cleanest place to keep it visible without polluting the Today card. The Trends page can safely reuse today's `walkingBrief` snapshot as a compact 4-week strip beside the broader historical charts; no new endpoint is needed. (b) **Breathwork rhythm moves to Sleep's Tonight view.** Breathwork is a wind-down lever, so it should sit next to the sleep projection and bedroom context rather than inside the workout card. (c) **Climate keeps a simple "review last night in Sleep" link after losing the chart.** Once Climate is the live room/fan control surface (#174), it should not duplicate the retrospective overnight chart, but it still needs an obvious handoff back to Sleep for yesterday's room story. **Implementation:** `EnvironmentPage.tsx` drops `OvernightChartCard` and replaces it with a lightweight last-night card linking to `/sleep`; `SleepPage.tsx` keeps the overnight chart on the Last night tab and adds the shared `BreathworkRhythmCard` under Tonight's sleep-prep body; `DashboardPage.tsx` removes both `walkingBrief` and `breathworkBrief` from `DayPlanBody`, leaving deliberate-walk reads on Today but moving the rolling summaries off the workout surface; `TrendsPage.tsx` now renders a compact `WalkingBaseCard` above the long-range charts using the existing daily-loop payload. Shared UI for the two moved summaries lives in new reusable `WalkingBaseCard` and `BreathworkRhythmCard` components. **Boundaries:** frontend-only; no API/shared-schema/migration change; no change to Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, or Red-never-VO2. **Verification (local, green):** focused web vitest **53 passed** (`EnvironmentPage.test.tsx`, `SleepPage.test.tsx`, `DashboardPage.test.tsx`, `TrendsPage.test.tsx`), web lint clean aside from the 6 pre-existing Fast Refresh warnings in unrelated files, and web production build clean under Node 20. Tier 🟢 Mid.

178. **Batch 105 treats an active holiday window as an away signal for the bedroom environment subsystem: no evening sleep/cooling nudge, no thermal alert, and no fan-autopilot tick while the profile-local date is inside that unresumed window.** Implemented on `feat/batch-105-holiday-environment` (batch row `docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-13 walkthrough). **Kickoff decision (105.4): use the existing holiday as the away flag for this private app, not a second parallel away-state model.** Batch 15 already stores the explicit date window Mark controls, and Batch 98 established `HolidayPauseService.get_windows(...)` as the authoritative holiday truth. Adding another flag would create two states that could disagree while solving no current use case. **Date semantics:** `holiday_windows_covering_date(...)` is now the shared coverage helper used by Batch 98's retrospective morning packet and Batch 105's scheduler guard; retrospective reads still include resumed historical windows, but scheduled environment work uses `active_holiday_window_for_date(...)`, so only an unresumed covering window means away and an early resume re-enables the subsystem immediately. **Implementation:** `run_evening_sleep_nudge` skips the profile outright; `run_evening_monitoring_alerts` calls `NudgeAlertService.run_monitoring_alerts(..., include_thermal=False)` so temperature/fan evaluation is dormant while the independent Garmin/Hive/weather stale-source guardian remains intact; and `run_fan_control` returns after the overnight phase check but before the latest-temperature read, Dreo cloud call, or `fan_state_readings` write. Outside the window every call keeps the previous path. **Boundaries:** backend-only; no migration/API/shared/frontend change; no threshold, fan-decision, notification-copy, Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, or Red-never-VO2 change. **Verification (local):** relevant holiday/scheduler/fan/morning/nudge suites **133 passed / 27 skipped**; backend Ruff check + format clean across all source/tests; full mypy clean (105 source files). The full local pytest run remains at the known pytest-9 baseline (581 passed / 203 skipped / 8 existing unmarked-async failures / 28 async-fixture errors), with the new marked holiday fan case passing and no baseline increase. Tier 🔴 High.

179. **Batch 106 uses the browser's built-in `SpeechSynthesis` for read-aloud morning briefs, not a hosted TTS service, and keeps the spoken output ephemeral by deriving clean prose from the existing markdown on the client.** Implemented on `feat/batch-106-read-aloud-brief` (batch row `docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-13 walkthrough). **Kickoff decision (106.3): default to built-in browser TTS first.** The brief already exists in the web app, so sending it to a hosted voice service would add cost, privacy surface, latency, and another dependency without proving the user need first. Browser `SpeechSynthesis` is free, offline-capable, and good enough for a first pass; if voice quality becomes a real issue later, that can be a separate batch with an explicit tradeoff. **Implementation:** `/brief` now renders `BriefListenControls` inside the existing coach-read card, exposing Listen / Pause / Resume / Stop states over one transient `SpeechSynthesisUtterance`; playback is cancelled on unmount or when the brief text changes so the UI cannot drift from the spoken state. The new frontend helper `markdownToSpeechText(...)` strips markdown syntax into readable prose before speaking it, including link text and table rows, so the spoken version does not read raw asterisks or pipe characters. **Boundaries:** frontend-only; no API/shared-schema/migration change; no persistence of audio or transcripts; no prompt/verdict change; Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, and Red-never-VO2 untouched. **Verification (local):** focused web vitest **6 passed** (`MorningBriefPage.test.tsx`, `markdownSpeech.test.ts`), web lint clean aside from the 6 pre-existing Fast Refresh warnings in unrelated files, and web production build clean. Tier 🟢 Mid.

180. **Batch 107 stays a Sleep/climate calendar on the existing `/sleep` surface, not a whole-app planner calendar, and it reuses the app's existing date-addressable reads rather than adding a new history API.** Implemented on `feat/batch-107-sleep-climate-calendar` (batch row `docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-13 walkthrough). **Kickoff decision (107.3): pin scope to the sleep/climate split that Batch 104 established.** The request came from the Sleep/Climate walkthrough and the ledger explicitly pairs 104 + 107 around that split, so folding workouts/Week into the same batch would blur the surface boundary and turn a Mid frontend browse feature into a larger information-architecture change. **Existing-read decision:** keep the batch frontend-only and build on the reads already in production: `/api/v1/daily-loop` already accepts `subject_date`, and `/api/v1/bedroom/overnight` already accepts `date`, so the page can browse history honestly without a new backend seam or migration. **Implementation:** `/sleep` now adds a month-grid `SleepDateCalendar` on the Last night tab, threads an optional `subject_date` through `useDailyLoop(...)`, and drives `OvernightChartCard` from the selected night so the sleep snapshot and room/fan chart stay in lockstep. The current-day pre-check-in gate remains unchanged; once unlocked, selecting a past date reloads that day's stored morning packet when it exists, or falls back to a compact raw-sleep summary while still showing the overnight climate history. The Tonight tab remains today's forward look-ahead, not a browseable future calendar. **Boundaries:** frontend-only; no API/shared-schema/migration change; no change to Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, or Red-never-VO2. **Verification (local):** focused web vitest **9 passed** (`SleepPage.test.tsx`, including the historical-date browse path), web `typecheck` clean, web lint clean aside from the 6 pre-existing Fast Refresh warnings in unrelated files, and web production build clean. Tier 🟢 Mid.

181. **Batch 108 fixes the 103×107 regression by separating "today is still gated" from "history is still browseable", and it keeps the whole-day context inside Sleep rather than inventing a cross-surface day viewer.** Implemented on `main` from Batch 108 (`docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-14 walkthrough). **Kickoff decisions:** (a) **Today-scoped gate, not page-scoped gate.** Batch 103's stricter pre-check-in rule was right about *today's* sleep surface, but it accidentally swallowed Batch 107's history browser too by tying access to the whole page. The fix keeps the Good Morning gate for today's current-date detail while letting the calendar and any other stored date load immediately. (b) **Collapse the calendar by default.** Once history is always available again, the month grid should not dominate the page on every open; it now sits behind an explicit Show/Hide toggle with the selected date still visible at a glance. (c) **Extend the Sleep history view in place, not a shared whole-day viewer reused by Week.** The walkthrough issue is specifically "when I pick a sleep date, show me that whole day too"; turning that into a new shared day-summary surface would broaden a Mid frontend follow-through into an information-architecture batch. A lightweight in-place "The whole day" card on `/sleep` is enough to carry the verdict + planned sessions beside the existing sleep and room history. **Implementation:** `SleepPage.tsx` now treats history access as "the daily-loop payload loaded" while `hasTodaySleepAccess` continues to gate only the current-date sleep detail and Tonight tab. Pre-check-in `/sleep` therefore shows `GoodMorningCta`, the collapsed `SleepDateCalendar`, and a locked-today card with the current-date check-in CTA; selecting a past date loads that stored sleep snapshot plus a new in-place whole-day summary card (verdict badge/reason + planned sessions) while the overnight chart still follows the selected night. `SleepDateCalendar.tsx` now defaults collapsed behind a Show/Hide toggle. **Boundaries:** frontend-only; no API/shared-schema/migration change; no change to Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, Red-never-VO2, or the Batch 103 current-day gate itself. **Verification (local, green):** targeted web vitest **10 passed** (`SleepPage.test.tsx`, including the new pre-check-in browse regression and whole-day historical view), web `tsc --noEmit` clean, web lint clean aside from the 6 pre-existing Fast Refresh warnings in unrelated files, and web production build clean. Tier 🟢 Mid.

182. **Batch 109 completes Batch 105's holiday-awareness on the frontend by keeping the evening/bedroom surfaces *dormant*, not hidden, and by extending the existing holiday seam into `/api/v1/daily-loop` rather than inventing a second away-state model.** Implemented on `feat/batch-109-holiday-home-sleep` from Batch 109 (`docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-14 walkthrough). **Kickoff decisions:** (a) **Choose dormant placeholders over full hide.** Batch 105 already proved that an active holiday means "Mark is away", so the frontend should stop acting as if tonight's room and wind-down are live; but making the sections vanish would make Home/Sleep look inconsistent with the rest of the app's object-permanence direction. Dormant cards keep the information architecture intact while honestly saying the system is paused until he is back. (b) **Use the existing active holiday window as the one away flag.** This matches Batch 105's private-app rule: the app already has an explicit, user-controlled holiday window and that is sufficient truth for away-ness; no parallel boolean or settings model is needed. (c) **Carry only the minimal frontend truth on the daily-loop payload:** `holiday { isActive, activeWindow { startDate, endDate } }`. The frontend needs to know whether the holiday is active and when the away state ends so it can render the dormant copy; it does not need the full holiday-history envelope. **Implementation:** `DailyLoopService` now loads `HolidayPauseService.get_active_window_for_date(...)` for the subject date and the router serializes that into the new daily-loop `holiday` object; Home's `tonight` and `bedroom` sections swap from live `SleepPrepBody` / `BedroomBody` content to quiet away-state placeholders with a Holiday handoff and resume date when `holiday.isActive`; Sleep's Tonight tab collapses to one holiday-away card instead of rendering the prep + climate cards; and the Home `nextAction(...)` ladder suppresses the evening `protect-sleep` CTA during an active holiday so the action strip cannot contradict the dormant surfaces. **Boundaries:** no migration; no scheduler/fan/verdict/threshold change; the existing backend holiday behavior from Batch 105 stays the source of truth. **Verification (local):** a focused daily-loop API test covers the new holiday payload, shared-schema tests parse both active and inactive shapes, and web tests cover Home/Sleep dormant holiday rendering plus the suppressed protect-sleep action. Tier 🟢 Mid.

183. **Batch 110 de-dups the brief flow by replacing UI in place rather than adding new state, and by trusting Batch 96's Home surfacing instead of duplicating the brief on Check-in.** Implemented on `feat/batch-110-brief-flow-dedup` (`docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-14 walkthrough). **Kickoff decisions:** (a) **In-place progress, not a second card.** The staged generation progress (`BRIEF_STAGES`) already existed from Batch 97; the only change needed was moving it to render where the button/entry area was, instead of alongside it, so the check-in doesn't show two competing "what's happening" surfaces at once. (b) **Drop the inline brief render entirely, don't gate it further.** Once Batch 96 shipped `UnviewedBriefCta` on Home, the Check-in page's own `VerdictHero` + Markdown render became a second place the same brief showed up; the simplest fix is deleting it and leaving Check-in's job as "save, then hand off to `/brief`" via the existing View brief button. (c) **On Home, drop the duplicated date rather than rewrite the verdict copy.** The greeting lockup's date (`friendlyDate(daily.subjectDate)`) was a literal duplicate of `VerdictHero`'s/`GoodMorningCta`'s own `dateLabel` immediately below; `personalStatusLine`'s prose framing ("You're good to go this morning") and `VerdictHero`'s badge ("Good to go") are different enough in register (personal narration vs. status label) that rewriting the greeting risked losing tone for no real de-dup gain, so only the redundant date was removed. **Boundaries:** frontend-only, no API/shared-schema/migration change; Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, Red-never-VO2 untouched. **Verification (local, green):** full web vitest **243 passed**, `tsc --noEmit` clean, web lint clean aside from the 6 pre-existing Fast Refresh warnings in unrelated files, web production build clean. Tier 🟢 Mid.

184. **Batch 111 stays on-device for the read-aloud voice — it improves `SpeechSynthesis` voice *selection*, not a hosted TTS swap — and explicitly excludes remote-service voices to hold the line Decision #179 drew.** Implemented on `feat/batch-111-natural-voice` (`docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-14 walkthrough). **Kickoff decision (111.2): on-device over hosted.** #179 chose the browser's `SpeechSynthesis` API specifically to avoid sending the health-data brief text to a third party; better voice quality alone doesn't outweigh that, so this batch stays inside the same API and fixes the actual complaint (the default voice sounds robotic) by picking a better voice, not a better backend. **The sharper finding:** some `SpeechSynthesisVoice` entries reported by `getVoices()` — e.g. Chrome's default "Google UK English" voices — are not actually on-device; `voice.localService === false` for them because the audio is synthesized on Google's servers, meaning naively picking "the best-sounding voice by name" could silently reintroduce the exact privacy leak #179 avoided. **Implementation:** new pure `selectBestVoice(voices, targetLang)` (`apps/web/src/lib/speechVoice.ts`) filters to `localService === true` only, scores by locale match (exact `lang` match beats same-language-different-region beats no match) then a small bonus for quality-signal names (`natural`/`neural`/`premium`/`enhanced`), and returns `null` when nothing local matches — in which case `BriefListenControls` leaves `utterance.voice` unset and the platform's own default takes over, exactly as it did before this batch. `BriefListenControls` now loads `speechSynthesis.getVoices()` (with the async `onvoiceschanged` load handled) and applies the selected voice per-utterance. **Boundaries:** frontend-only; no API/shared-schema/migration change; no hosted TTS integration; #179's privacy posture unchanged; Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, Red-never-VO2 untouched. **Verification (local, green):** full web vitest **249 passed** (incl. new `speechVoice.test.ts` and a `MorningBriefPage.test.tsx` case proving a remote "Natural"-named voice loses to a plainer local one), `tsc --noEmit` clean, web lint clean aside from the 6 pre-existing Fast Refresh warnings in unrelated files, web production build clean. Tier 🟢 Mid.

185. **Batch 112 converges the 09:30 backstop onto the same `brief_ready_push` notification the check-in path already sends, and points every thermal severity at `/environment` instead of the stale redirect-only `/bedroom` route.** Implemented on `feat/batch-112-notification-cleanup` (`docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-14 walkthrough). **Kickoff decision (112.2): retire `verdict_push`, don't keep a distinct backstop notification.** `verdict_push` (`/`) and `brief_ready_push` (`/brief`) both announced "your brief is ready" under different copy and different URLs depending on which path (check-in vs. the 09:30 backstop) generated the analysis first — a Mark-facing inconsistency with no upside, since both paths land on the same `/brief` destination he actually wants. `run_morning_weather_sync` now calls `NudgeAlertService.push_brief_ready` in place of the removed `push_morning_verdict`; the existing `brief-ready-{date}` idempotency tag means whichever path (check-in's `_generate_brief_after_checkin` or the backstop) generates and pushes first wins, and the other is a no-op — no double-push, no separate verdict copy. `build_verdict_push_plan`, `ANALYSIS_TYPE_VERDICT_PUSH`, and `push_morning_verdict` are deleted rather than left dead. **112.1/112.3 — one thermal deep-link.** The fan-can't-cope critical alert pointed at `/bedroom`, a route `App.tsx` only ever redirects to `/environment` (never renders); the other four thermal branches (`peak_20c`, `peak_19_5c`, `seal_22`, `pre_cool_17c`) pointed at `/`, the app root, rather than the environment/thermal detail page. All five now share one `THERMAL_URL = "/environment"` constant, so every thermal severity opens the same live room/fan detail instead of a mix of the home screen and a route that immediately bounces elsewhere. **Boundaries:** backend-only, no migration, no scheduler/verdict/threshold change; Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, Red-never-VO2 untouched; evening/stale-source/good-morning/workout-checkin notification URLs unchanged. **Verification (local, green):** backend pytest **615 passed** (204 skipped, DB-backed), `ruff check` clean, `mypy src` clean on all 105 source files. Tier 🟢 Mid.

186. **Batch 113 suppresses the morning brief's thermal/environment review and pre-cool action only inside an active holiday window (`restDay.insideHolidayWindow`), not for every rest day, and fixes the pre-cool link to `/environment`.** Implemented on `feat/batch-113-holiday-thermal-suppression` (`docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-14 walkthrough). **Kickoff decision (113.4): holiday-window-only, not any rest day.** `restDay.isRestDay` also covers an all-skipped local rest day where Mark is still sleeping at home — the bedroom is genuinely occupied and the thermal read stays relevant there; only an active holiday window means the room isn't being slept in, so that's the narrower, already-named `insideHolidayWindow` flag the batch row itself specified. **Implementation:** `assemble_context_packet` now derives `thermal_review_for_output = None if rest_day["insideHolidayWindow"] else thermal_review`, used for both `environment.thermalReview` (null on holiday) and the `build_today_actions(...)` call (so `_thermal_action` sees no flags and never emits the "Pre-cool the bedroom" action); the `include_thermal_environment_review` output rule is filtered out of `prompt.outputRules` under the same condition, and `SYSTEM_PROMPT` gains a line telling the model not to fabricate a thermal review when the field is null. `_thermal_action`'s `href` moves from the stale `/sleep` to `/environment` unconditionally (post-Batch-101 route), independent of the holiday gate. On the frontend, `SleepPage.tsx`'s Last-night tab now checks `holiday.isActive` and swaps `OvernightChartCard` for a dormant "Holiday away" card mirroring the Tonight tab's existing holiday placeholder (Batch 109), rather than always rendering the fan chart. **Boundaries:** no migration; no scheduler/verdict/threshold change; Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, Red-never-VO2, and Batch 105/109's holiday-dormant pattern for Tonight/bedroom untouched — this batch only extends the same pattern to the brief and Last-night. **Verification (local, green):** targeted + full backend pytest (615 passed, 204 skipped DB-backed), `ruff check` clean, `mypy src` clean on all 105 source files; targeted web vitest (12 passed, `SleepPage.test.tsx`), full web vitest (249 passed, 1 pre-existing unrelated `WeekAheadPage` timeout confirmed flaky in isolation), `tsc --noEmit` clean, web lint clean. Tier 🟢 Mid.

187. **Batch 114 gates both check-in CTAs on `manualEntry`, not on whether an analysis/brief exists yet, so the async window between "checked in" and "brief generated" (Batch 97) stops looking like "not checked in".** Implemented on `feat/batch-114-check-in-cta-clears` (`docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-14 walkthrough, Issues 8 and 11). **Kickoff decision: gate on `manualEntry`, not `analysis`.** Both surfaces the batch row named (`DashboardPage.tsx`'s hero, `SleepPage.tsx`'s check-in card) were keyed off whether `morningAnalysis` existed, but Batch 97 made brief generation asynchronous — a checked-in Mark sits in a `manualEntry != null, morningAnalysis == null` window while the background job runs. Gating the hero and the card off `manualEntry` instead means "have you checked in" and "should I invite a check-in" track the thing that's actually true, independent of how far along the brief is. **Implementation:** Home's hero (`DashboardPage.tsx`) gains a third state — new `BriefGeneratingCta` component (mirrors `GoodMorningCta`'s layout, no CTA button, "Writing your brief…" copy) renders when `analysis == null && daily.manualEntry != null`, between `VerdictHero` (analysis present) and the original `GoodMorningCta` (neither present). Sleep's "Add today's check-in" card now only renders when `data.manualEntry == null` — previously it rendered whenever `hasTodaySleepAccess` was true (i.e. also once checked in), so it kept dangling a redundant check-in link even after Mark had already checked in for the day. It still offers the optional check-in when the sleep surface unlocked via the 09:30 backstop's auto-generated brief without a manual check-in. **Boundaries:** frontend-only, no API/shared-schema/migration change; Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, Red-never-VO2 untouched. **Verification (local, green):** targeted web vitest **56 passed** (`DashboardPage.test.tsx`, `SleepPage.test.tsx`), `tsc --noEmit` clean, web lint clean aside from the 6 pre-existing Fast Refresh warnings in unrelated files, full web vitest **249 passed** (3 unrelated `WeekAheadPage.test.tsx` timeouts under full-suite parallel load, confirmed a pre-existing flake — all 12 pass in isolation), web production build clean under Node 20. Tier 🟢 Mid.

188. **Batch 115 folds the personable status line into `VerdictHero`'s own `line` prop instead of dropping it, and suppresses the "You're all set" strip only when the day itself already says nothing's planned (`dayState.isRest || holiday.isActive`), not on every all-clear.** Implemented on `feat/batch-115-home-dedup` (`docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-14 walkthrough, Issues 4 and 5). **Kickoff decision (115.1): fold, don't drop.** `personalStatusLine(verdict, name)` carries a personal, named greeting (`"Good morning, Mark. You're good to go this morning."`) that `VerdictHero`'s own generic `line` (`"Train as planned today."`) doesn't; dropping the paragraph outright would have lost that personal register entirely (Batch 110's Decision #183 explicitly declined to touch it for the same reason). Passing `personalStatusLine(...)` into `VerdictHero`'s existing `line` prop keeps the personal wording but renders it exactly once, inside the hero, instead of twice — once above it restating the verdict, once inside the hero's own badge. The plain `greeting` line (no verdict restated) still renders above the pre-verdict CTAs (`GoodMorningCta`/`BriefGeneratingCta`), since neither of those carries a greeting of its own. **Kickoff decision (115.2): gate the suppression on the day, not on the all-set state generally.** The existing all-clear `NextActionStrip` test (Batch 50) uses a normal workout day, where "You're all set" is the *only* place that says nothing's pending — suppressing it unconditionally would leave a genuinely all-clear cycling day with no next-step signal at all. The redundancy the walkthrough actually flagged is specific to rest/holiday days, where the Today card's own summary (`"Rest is the plan today."` or the holiday-dormant state) already conveys it; `dayState.isRest || holiday.isActive` is the existing, already-computed signal for exactly that case, so no new day-type field was added. **Boundaries:** frontend-only, no API/shared-schema/migration change; Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, Red-never-VO2 untouched. **Verification (local, green):** targeted web vitest **45 passed** (`DashboardPage.test.tsx`, 3 new), full web vitest **254 passed**, `tsc --noEmit` clean, web lint clean aside from the 6 pre-existing Fast Refresh warnings in unrelated files, web production build clean. **CI:** both branch-push and PR-context waves passed all 7 jobs plus the Vercel preview. **Production on merge SHA `b97e90b9dc286ff932ab71c33c7cb8b744cbc958`:** Railway + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both served that SHA; web `/` returned `200`; unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` returned `401` both direct and via Vercel. Tier 🟢 Mid.

189. **Batch 116 escalates the read-aloud voice to a hosted/neural option via OpenAI's TTS API, but only behind an explicit per-user opt-in that defaults off, with the on-device voice (Batch 111) as the automatic fallback — re-opening, not overturning, #179/#184's on-device-first stance.** Implemented on `feat/batch-116-hosted-tts` (`docs/phase-batches.md`, surfaced by Craig's 2026-07-14 walkthrough). **Kickoff decision (116.2, decided with Craig at `/batch-start`): consent-gated hosted TTS, on-device fallback.** #179 chose the browser's `SpeechSynthesis` API specifically to keep brief text off third-party servers; #184 held that line while improving on-device voice selection.
