(138.3) a second bug in the same class — `WeekAheadPage.tsx`'s `todayIso = new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)` used the **UTC** date, which during BST rolls a day late from ~23:00 UTC and could highlight the wrong day at the boundary — now uses `localTodayIso(player?.timezone)`. **(138.4) The backstop retune (Craig's call: keep the guaranteed-verdict safety net, just move the deadline out):** `wake_detection.py`'s `BACKSTOP` **09:30 → 11:00** and `WINDOW_END` **10:00 → 11:30** (kept 30 min past the backstop so `run_wake_check` is still polling when it fires), and the `morning_backstop` APScheduler cron **09:30 → 11:00**; cadence, `settle_min` (20), duration floor (180), and the back-to-sleep stability guard are all unchanged. The original 09:30 (Decision #87) hugged Mark's latest-observed wake (09:24, from the 363-night backfill: median 08:22, p90 08:50), so a genuine lie-in got force-read (`past_backstop → fire`) on stale/unfinalized data before he was up; 11:00 sits well clear. **This only affects edge days:** a normal morning wake-detects ~08:22 via the stability guard and never reaches the backstop, so lie-in / watch-not-worn days are the only ones moved — and they still get a guaranteed verdict, just later. **Boundaries:** frontend + the backstop constant/cron-time change only; no API/shared-schema/migration; the Green/Amber/Red verdict, advisory/no-load, #133/#135, and Red-never-VO2 are untouched; the design doc `docs/designs/wake-triggered-morning.md` carries an as-built update note (the historical 09:30/10:00 calibration reasoning is preserved, operative values flagged as 11:00/11:30). **Verification (local, green):** new tests — `dailyFlow.test.ts` (5: `localTodayIso` across a BST/GMT/behind-UTC boundary), `DashboardPage.test.tsx` (+3: stale notice shows for an earlier day, hidden when today, Refresh triggers a `cache: 'reload'` refetch that clears the notice once today lands), `WeekAheadPage.test.tsx` (+1: the Today badge lands on the local day, not the UTC day, at a 23:30-UTC BST boundary), `test_wake_detection.py` (+1 lie-in-before-new-backstop-still-waits, constants + backstop-time cases retuned), `test_scheduler.py` (backstop cron asserts `hour='11', minute='0'`, the unfinalized-backstop DB case moved to 11:05); backend pytest **634 passed / 227 skipped**, `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean, `mypy src` clean (113 files); web `tsc --noEmit` clean, eslint clean on all touched files, production build clean (Node 20). The full local web vitest suite (run concurrently with the build, 521s) reported a **mix** of failures: genuine #122/#214 cold-start `userEvent` timeouts in untouched files (`HandoverPage`, `WeekAheadPage` move-picker) **plus one real regression** — `SleepPage.test.tsx`'s single-arg `apiFetch('/api/v1/daily-loop?subject_date=…')` assertion broke because adding `fetchDailyLoop`'s `init` param made the *normal* fetch pass a second `undefined` arg; the local run's contention noise hid it among the timeouts, and CI's clean environment isolated it (only `SleepPage` failed there). **Fix (amend `e3869e4`):** only the forced refresh passes an init (`cache: 'reload'`), so the normal path stays `apiFetch(path)` single-arg. **Shipped** (PR #165, squash `f23078b`): after the fix both branch-push and PR-context CI waves passed all 7 jobs plus the Vercel preview. Production verified on merge SHA `f23078b723bb9e20960ef0616c5130eff4c9ad41`: Railway + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both served that SHA, web `/` `200`, unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` `401` both direct and via the Vercel proxy. The on-device eyeball of the stale banner + refresh and the 11:00 backstop firing remain deferred to live use. Tier 🔴 High.

218. **Check-in details were lost to a warm-resume refetch, not a save bug: `CheckInPage` re-seeded its form from the server on every `['daily-loop']` refetch, so a focus / `pageshow` refetch landing mid-check-in blanked Mark's unsaved edits and the next tap saved an empty entry — fixed by only mirroring the server into the form while it is pristine.** Implemented on `fix/batch-139-checkin-refetch-clobber` (`docs/phase-batches.md`, Batch 139); triggered by Mark reporting his check-in details "aren't being saved." **Root cause (not a backend bug — `DailyLoopService.upsert_manual_entry` and the read-back `_manual_entry` in `services/daily_loop.py` are symmetric on `entry_date` and commit correctly; prod confirms the write works absent an interfering refetch):** `CheckInPage.tsx`'s `useEffect([query.data])` (`:132`) copies `data.manualEntry` into local `manualForm` state on **every** change to the loaded snapshot, with no guard for unsaved edits. `refetchOnWindowFocus` (`lib/queryClient.ts:22`) is enabled and deliberately widened to fire on iOS-PWA warm resume (`pageshow`) via `lib/resumeRefetch.ts` (installed at `App.tsx:77`), so a warm resume mid-check-in refetches `['daily-loop']`; because a fresh envelope differs (new `meta.generatedAtUtc`), React Query's structural sharing returns a **new** `query.data` reference, the effect re-runs, and the form resets to the server's still-empty `manualEntry` — then "Get today's brief" PUTs an empty entry. **Prod evidence:** 2026-07-18's morning `manual_entry` was created 09:05:49 all-null with the brief generated 47 s later (the PUT fired, empty), versus 11/12 Jul rows created-then-updated with his real values and 7 Jul's empty+full pair 8 s apart (the same race from the other side). **Fix (frontend-only, no API/shared-schema/migration):** a `dirtyRef` — set by `setManual` and `toggleChip`, which between them cover the subjective-feel buttons, the quick chips, and every free-text/BP input — makes the seed effect return early once he has edited, so a background refetch can no longer clobber unsaved input; a successful save clears `dirtyRef` in `saveMutation.onSuccess` so the post-save invalidation re-seeds cleanly from the stored values, and pristine-form seeding (first load, and a later backstop-generated entry) is unchanged. This restores the "dirty-state-safe across a focus refetch" contract that `resumeRefetch.ts:18` already assumed every editor honoured — `CheckInPage` was the sole exception. **Boundaries:** frontend-only; verdict / Green-Amber-Red / #133 / #135 / Red-never-VO2 untouched; the empty-payload overwrite in `upsert_manual_entry` (an all-empty PUT still replaces a populated row) and the missing morning-entry uniqueness (the 7 Jul duplicate rows) are recorded as **separate follow-ups**, not bundled into this fix. **Verification (local, green):** new `CheckInPage.test.tsx` cases — a mid-edit refetch returning a distinct envelope (new `meta.generatedAtUtc`, `manualEntry` null — the real structural-sharing condition) preserves the typed score + chip and the save PUTs them, not empties (verified to fail without the guard); a pristine form still adopts a server entry arriving on a later refetch — full `CheckInPage.test.tsx` **10 passed**, web `tsc --noEmit` clean, eslint clean on touched files. **Shipped** via PR #166 (squash `a6dc4ee`); both CI waves (branch-push + PR-context) passed all jobs plus the Vercel preview, and production verified on merge SHA `a6dc4ee34626376037cc0d9b361cd09393479a6e` — Railway + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both served that SHA, the Vercel production deployment is READY at the merge commit, web `/` + `/check-in` `200`, unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` `401` both direct and via the Vercel proxy. No `ARCHITECTURE.md` change (no roadmap / spec / data-model impact). The on-device eyeball of a real iOS warm-resume mid-check-in is deferred to Mark's live use (live-browser blocked on this Mac — [[ui-walkthrough-method]]); the discriminating unit test stands in. Tier 🔴 High.

219. **A post-activity re-read *inserts* a new `Analysis` row rather than superseding the prior one, so the daily-loop snapshot must reduce to the latest analysis per activity — otherwise a corrected ride surfaces both its stale and fresh reads and the frontend renders the *stale* one (Mark's "changed RPE 7→4 but the read still says 7").** Implemented on `claude/dads-feedback-mii8u3` from Batch 140 (`docs/phase-batches.md`), traced from Mark reporting that after mistyping his post-ride RPE as 7, correcting it to 4, and tapping "Read my workout" again, the analysis still read RPE 7. **Root cause (backend read-path, not a save or generation bug):** the correction *does* save and the coach *does* re-read at RPE 4 — `upsert_post_ride_checkin` writes rpe=4 and `generate_post_activity_read(..., force=True)` regenerates through `PostWorkoutAnalysisService.generate_and_store`, which reads the latest check-in and produces a fresh RPE-4 read. But `generate_and_store` **never supersedes** the prior analysis — every re-read (this check-in-first path *and* the hourly poll after a `PROMPT_VERSION` bump) `session.add`s a *new* `Analysis` row, and there is no unique constraint on `analyses (user_id, activity_id, analysis_type)` to collapse them. Everywhere else that needs the current read uses `latest_analysis_for_activity` (order by `generated_at_utc desc`) to pick it — but `DailyLoopService._post_activity_analyses` did **not**: it returned *every* analysis for the day (`order_by Activity.start_utc desc, Analysis.generated_at_utc desc`), so a corrected ride's two rows [RPE 4 (newer), RPE 7 (older)] both flowed to the envelope. **The frontend then rendered the stale one:** `DashboardPage.tsx` built `rideByWorkoutId` with `.set(plannedWorkoutId, ride)` in a loop over that newest-first list, so last-write-wins left the *oldest* (RPE 7) row in the map for a planned ride; an unplanned ride showed both as duplicate cards. **Fix (primary, backend):** `_post_activity_analyses` now reduces to the newest analysis per activity per type (drop-after-first over the already-newest-first ordering — the same latest-wins rule as `latest_analysis_for_activity`), fixing both the planned-ride map and the unplanned-ride duplicate cards at the payload boundary. **Fix (defense-in-depth, frontend):** `DashboardPage` dedupes `postWorkouts` to the newest read per `activityId` (`latestRides`) *before* the planned/unplanned split, so a stray duplicate can never resurface a stale read again even if the contract is violated. **Why reduce-at-read rather than delete-on-regenerate or add a unique constraint:** the historical `Analysis` rows are intentionally retained (feedback rows link to a specific analysis id, `PROMPT_VERSION` bumps deliberately regenerate through a new row, and reviews/history query prior reads) — deleting or upserting them would break that lineage, whereas reducing to latest at the daily-loop read leaves the audit trail intact and matches how every other reader already resolves "the current read". **Boundaries:** backend read-path + a small frontend client-side guard only; **no API/shared-schema/migration**; the analyses table, `generate_and_store` insert semantics, verdict / Green-Amber-Red / #133 / #135 / Red-never-VO2 all untouched; no `ARCHITECTURE.md` change (no roadmap / spec / data-model impact — the stored shape is unchanged, only which rows the daily-loop surfaces). **Verification (local, green):** new `test_daily_loop.py::test_post_activity_analyses_keep_latest_per_activity` (two post_workout analyses for one activity — RPE 7 then RPE 4 — collapse to only the RPE-4 row; **verified to fail without the fix**, 2≠1) alongside the existing collapse test; new `DashboardPage.test.tsx` "renders only the latest read when a corrected ride has two analyses (Batch 140)" (a planned ride with a stale RPE-7 + fresh RPE-4 pair renders only the RPE-4 read behind one "View analysis"; **verified to fail without the frontend guard**). Full backend pytest **862 passed** (Postgres-backed), `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean, `mypy` clean (113 files); web `tsc --noEmit` clean, `DashboardPage.test.tsx` **57 passed**, eslint clean on touched files, production build clean (Node 22). The on-device eyeball of a real corrected-RPE re-read is deferred to Mark's live use; the two discriminating tests stand in. **Shipped** via PR #167 (squash `c0fb5817bad4b70cfa8a366cd3c7beb9f108dfea`); both CI waves (branch-push run #836 + PR-context) passed all 7 jobs plus the Vercel preview, and production was verified on the merge SHA — Railway + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both served `c0fb581`, web `/` `200`, unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` `401` both direct and via the Vercel proxy. Tier 🔴 High.

220. **A failed brief generation must resolve to an honest, retryable state instead of an endless "Writing your brief", and an Anthropic billing/credit outage must alert the operator — settled at `/batch-start` as a backend-recorded failure signal on the daily-loop envelope + both alert channels (a structured `error`-log event *and* an admin push decoupled from the app `admin` role).** Implemented on `feat/batch-141-brief-failure-resilience` from Batch 141 (`docs/phase-batches.md`); surfaced by the **2026-07-21 Anthropic credit outage** — the account ran out of credits, so every `POST https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` returned a **400** `invalid_request_error` ("Your credit balance is too low…"); `_generate_brief_after_checkin` caught the `HTTPStatusError`, logged, and returned, leaving **no `morningAnalysis`** for the day, and the frontend polled "Writing your brief" indefinitely (the spinner only ever cleared when an analysis appeared) — silent to Mark *and* to Craig, with the *reason* invisible in the logs because `generate_anthropic_text`'s `raise_for_status()` discards the response body (recovering it on the day needed a manual out-of-band API call). **The design fork settled with Craig at `/batch-start` (2026-07-21):** 141.1 → a **backend-recorded failure signal** (a persisted per-`(user, subject_date)` marker on the daily-loop envelope) over a client-side timeout/poll-cap — honest and able to tell failed from genuinely-slow, at the cost of a table + envelope field (this lifted the batch to 🔴 High and added migration `020`); 141.3 → **both** channels, triggered on the **first billing-classed failure** (deduped once/day), not an N-failure threshold. **Built:** (141.1) `brief_generation_status` (migration `020`, RLS-guarded like 015/019) + `BriefGenerationStatusService` upsert one row per `(user, subject_date)` — `generating` when the check-in schedules generation, `ready` on success, `failed` (+ classified `reason`) on error; `DailyLoopData.briefGeneration = {status, reason}` exposes it, and `_serialize_brief_generation` makes a real analysis on the day authoritative (a stale `generating`/`failed` row never overrides an existing brief). `CheckInPage` stops polling and shows a retryable `ErrorState` on a `failed` signal (Retry = `saveMutation.mutate()`, re-running the check-in generate — the app's only generate trigger) and Home shows a `BriefFailedCta`; both read the envelope, so a failure shows even on a cold reopen with no in-flight `queuedAtMs`. The shared Zod field is `.nullable().optional()` so a pre-141 cached payload still parses. (141.2) `generate_anthropic_text` reads the error body and logs `error.type`/`error.message`, then raises a classified `AnthropicApiError` (`classify_anthropic_error` → `billing`/`rate_limit`/`auth`/`overloaded`/`prompt_too_long`/`server_error`/`invalid_request`/`other`; the credit case is a 400 whose *message* names the balance, so the message is checked first). The eight analysis callers all catch `Exception`, so the new distinct type on the HTTP path changes no existing handler. **The `x-api-key` is never logged** — it travels only in the request header and is never echoed in a response body. (141.3) `NudgeAlertService.notify_admin_generation_failure` always emits an `error`-level `brief_generation_admin_alert` event and additionally web-pushes the profile named by `settings.admin_alert_user_id`, **deliberately decoupled from the app `admin` role** because the primary user (Mark) holds that role and an ops/billing alert must never land on his phone — so the push is a no-op until Craig seeds his *own* profile id into that env var; deduped once per (operator, day) via `_send_once`, best-effort (never raises, so it can't perturb the failing generation path). The check-in failure branch calls `mark_failed`, then on `reason == "billing"` fires the alert. **Boundaries:** generation logic + the verdict + Green/Amber/Red + advisory/no-load + #133/#135/Red-never-VO2 untouched; one new table (migration `020`) + one optional envelope field; `ARCHITECTURE.md` §5 gains the `brief_generation_status` row. **Verification (local, green):** backend pytest **646 passed / 229 skipped** (DB-backed skips local-only; new coverage: a billing-classed 400 logged with its message + no key material and raising the admin alert, a non-billing 4xx not alerting, the status-service upsert), `ruff`/`mypy` clean (114 files); web vitest **341 passed** (54 files; new: a `failed` envelope resolves `CheckInPage` to the retryable card and Retry re-triggers generation — verified to fail without 141.1 — plus the Home `BriefFailedCta`), `tsc`/eslint clean, production build clean under Node 20. **Shipped** via PR #168 (squash `f4215067091ac9a3df49aa7064d8d7faa219952f`); both CI waves (branch-push + PR-context) passed all 16 checks plus the Vercel preview. Production verified on the merge SHA — Railway `/api/v1/health` served it (so migration `020` applied, the container running `alembic upgrade head` before uvicorn; a read-only `information_schema` check confirmed `coach.brief_generation_status` exists with the expected columns), Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` served the same SHA, web `/` + `/check-in` `200`, unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` `401` both direct and via the Vercel proxy. **`admin_alert_user_id` is unset in prod**, so the push channel is dormant (the `error`-log channel is live) until Craig seeds his profile id — an env-only change, no code redeploy. Enabling **auto-reload** in the Anthropic console is the complementary ops action (prevents the outage class; not a code phase). The on-device eyeball of the real failure card + Retry is deferred to live use (needs an actual generation failure to trigger; [[ui-walkthrough-method]]); the discriminating tests stand in. Tier 🔴 High.

221. **The morning read now states time-in-bed vs time-asleep as two explicitly-labelled packet figures rather than leaving the model to infer them from a single `durationMin`, and the prompt forbids re-deriving either — fixing Mark's 2026-07-19 read that mislabelled his 6h26 asleep as "in bed" and re-subtracted the 62-min awake window to invent a fictitious "5h5 actual sleep".** Implemented on `feat/batch-142-sleep-time-in-bed` from Batch 142 (`docs/phase-batches.md`); surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-23 feedback (2026-07-19 note). **Root cause (backend packet — not a data or verdict bug):** `_sleep_packet` (`services/morning_analysis.py`) gave the model `durationMin` = Garmin `sleepTimeSeconds` — time *asleep*, already excluding the awake window — with **no explicit in-bed total**, so the model had to guess which figure was which and guessed wrong. **Decision settled at `/batch-start`: additive, not rename** — kept `durationMin` after confirming it has **zero Python consumers** (every other `durationMin` in the repo is a workout/activity schema), so no stored `context_packet` shape or historical read breaks. **Built (backend-only, no migration, no shared-schema change):** (142.1) the sleep packet carries two explicitly-named totals beside the unchanged `durationMin` — `timeAsleepMin` (= `duration_sec`, asleep) and `timeInBedMin` (new `_time_in_bed_min(row)` helper = the `sleep_end_utc − sleep_start_utc` bed→wake window, falling back to `duration + awake + unmeasurable` when a bed/wake timestamp is missing, and `None` only when neither a valid window nor any component total exists). (142.2) `SYSTEM_PROMPT` gains a sleep-arithmetic rule (state in-bed from `timeInBedMin`, asleep from `timeAsleepMin`; the asleep total already excludes awake so **never subtract it**; in-bed = asleep + awake + brief unmeasurable — state each as given, never re-derive), and `PROMPT_VERSION` bumps `v13 → v14-2026-07-24` so stale reads regenerate. **Boundaries — proved, not assumed:** verdict + Green/Amber/Red + #133/#135/Red-never-VO2 untouched — `age_adjusted_sleep_score` reads stage seconds (deep/light/rem/awake), not `duration_sec`, and `_morning_verdict` never reads `duration_sec`, so adding `duration_sec` to the assembled-packet test fixture left its `ageAdjustedScore==71` / `−3.0` delta / `Amber` assertions intact (verified in isolation — age score identical at 71 with/without — and re-confirmed by CI's DB-backed pytest job on the real Postgres). **Out-of-scope follow-up flagged:** `garmin_sync.py:478` synthesizes `duration_sec = deep+light+rem+awake+unmeasurable` (*including* awake) when Garmin omits `sleepTimeSeconds` — in that rare fallback-populated case `timeAsleepMin` isn't strictly asleep and the summed `timeInBedMin` fallback would double-count; left untouched because it doesn't affect Mark's real nights (his `sleepTimeSeconds` is present, distinct from the window) and fixing the sync's duration provenance is a separate change from this read-path labelling. **Verification (local, green):** backend pytest **649 passed / 229 skipped** (+3 new non-DB tests — the 2026-07-19 regression, the missing-timestamp fallback, the prompt guard; the extended assembled-packet test is DB-backed and runs in CI), `ruff check` + `format --check` clean, `mypy src` clean (114 files); no web/shared gates (backend-only); no `ARCHITECTURE.md` change (no roadmap/spec/data-model impact). **Shipped** via PR #169 (squash `a18b35b7e15a1d15ec1fe9c0b6f8d1e46eec70cd`); both CI waves (branch-push + PR-context) passed all 7 jobs plus the Vercel preview. Production verified on the merge SHA — Railway `/api/v1/health` + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both served it, web `/` `200`, unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` `401` both direct and via the Vercel proxy. The on-device eyeball of a real brief rendering both figures is deferred to Mark's next generated brief; the discriminating tests + the `v14` bump (which regenerates his stale reads) stand in ([[ui-walkthrough-method]]). Tier 🔴 High.

222. **Two in-request Anthropic boundaries — the brief follow-up chat and the post-ride check-in read — now fail as honest, retryable errors instead of a bare FastAPI 500 whose plain-text body the web client threw a JSON `SyntaxError` on; settled at `/batch-start` as inline-guarded (no background/status table).** Implemented on `feat/batch-143-daytime-llm-failures` from Batch 143 (`docs/phase-batches.md`); surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-23 feedback (the 2026-07-20/21 `Unexpected token 'I', "Internal S"… is not valid JSON` errors on "Ask about the brief" and the post-ride check-in). **Root cause:** Batch 141 hardened only the *background* morning-brief task; `routers/brief_chat.py::ask_brief_followup` and `routers/daily_loop.py::upsert_post_ride_checkin` call Anthropic synchronously and let `AnthropicApiError` propagate → FastAPI's default 500 with a non-JSON `Internal Server Error` body → `apiFetch`'s `resp.json()` threw a `SyntaxError` it re-threw verbatim. **Built:** (143.1) `ask_brief_followup` catches `AnthropicApiError` and raises `HTTPException(status=anthropic_http_status(reason), detail=anthropic_user_message(reason))` — new helpers in `services/anthropic_text.py` map the 141 classifier's reason to **503** (retryable: billing/rate_limit/overloaded) or **502** (hard upstream) and to a generic, provider-prose-free message; no half-written turn persists because the model call precedes every DB write in `BriefChatService.ask`. (143.2) **Decision (inline-guarded, not the 141 background+status pattern):** `upsert_post_ride_checkin` generates the read inside a **SAVEPOINT** — `async with db.begin_nested(): generate_post_activity_read(..., force=True, commit=False)` then `await db.commit()` on success — so an `AnthropicApiError` rolls back only the half-written analysis + its `complete_matched_planned_workout` flip, never the check-in the service already committed; the endpoint returns 200 with a non-fatal `errors[]` note (`code=post_workout_read_failed`) and the ride re-surfaces as a `pendingPostActivity` carrying the saved check-in, so re-submitting is the retry. **Why a savepoint, not a full `db.rollback()`:** the first CI wave failed the post-ride test with `relation "analyses" does not exist` — the `db_conn` fixture issues `SET search_path TO coach, public` inside its transaction, so a full request-level rollback reverted it (and, in the single-connection harness, risks unwinding the earlier-committed check-in); a `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT` touches nothing established before the savepoint (the search_path and the check-in both precede it), so the same code is faithful in prod *and* the harness. **Prod was never at risk** either way: `database.py` sets `search_path` connection-level via asyncpg `server_settings`, which a transaction rollback does not revert. (143.3) billing-classed failures on both paths route through 141's `NudgeAlertService.notify_admin_generation_failure` (log always + dormant admin push — `admin_alert_user_id` still unset in prod). (143.4) `apps/web/src/lib/api.ts::apiFetch`'s `!resp.ok` branch now parses the body in a `try`, falling back to `API error {status}` on a non-JSON body instead of re-throwing the parse `SyntaxError`, while still surfacing a JSON `detail`; new `lib/postWorkoutRead.ts::postWorkoutReadFailure` lets both post-ride mutations show a retry toast rather than a false "Workout read ready" when the envelope carries the read-failed note. **Boundaries:** verdict + Green/Amber/Red + #133/#135/Red-never-VO2 untouched; **no migration, no shared-schema change** (`errors[]`/`apiErrorSchema` already existed). **Verification:** two new discriminating DB-backed tests — `test_post_message_anthropic_billing_failure_returns_clean_error_and_alerts` (503 + JSON `detail` + one admin alert + zero persisted turns) and `test_post_ride_checkin_persists_when_read_generation_fails` (200 + `errors[]` note + one admin alert + RPE/feel/notes persisted) — run in CI (skip locally: no Postgres, brew broken Tier-3). Local: backend ruff/format/mypy clean (114 files), non-DB pytest green; web vitest **346** + shared **19**, tsc/eslint/build clean (Node 20). **Shipped** via PR #170 (squash `ab905df4bcc718e44692c0d8ea797ac6b0c6286b`); the first branch-push wave caught the fixture artifact above, and after the savepoint fix both CI waves passed all 7 jobs (pytest **880 passed**) plus the Vercel preview. Production verified on the merge SHA — Railway + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both served it, web `/` + `/check-in` + `/brief` `200`, unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` and `GET /api/v1/briefs/{id}/messages` `401` both direct and via the Vercel proxy. The on-device eyeball of a real day-time outage is deferred to live use (needs an actual failure to trigger; [[ui-walkthrough-method]]); the discriminating tests stand in. Tier 🔴 High.

223. **An orphaned "generating" brief now resolves to a retryable failure within minutes — settled at `/batch-start` (Craig) as a read-time staleness derivation (no writer, no migration, no scheduler) plus a mirrored client max-wait cap, both at 12 min.** Implemented on `feat/batch-144-stale-brief-generation` from Batch 144 (`docs/phase-batches.md`); surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-23 feedback (2026-07-21 note). **Gap:** Batch 141 flips a *cleanly-failed* generation to a retryable card, but a background task orphaned by a process restart or a hung Anthropic call never runs `mark_ready`/`mark_failed`, so the `brief_generation_status` row stays `generating` forever — no reaper, no client max-wait — the 2026-07-21 90-minute-spinner class. **Decision fork (144.1): read-time derivation vs a persisted transition.** Chose **read-time** — `_serialize_brief_generation` (`routers/daily_loop.py`) now treats a `generating` row whose `updated_at` is older than `settings.brief_generation_stale_after_minutes` (default **12 min**, new config field) as `failed`/`reason="stale"` on the daily-loop envelope, off the existing `UpdatedAtMixin.updated_at` — no new column, no writer on the read path, no scheduler. **No admin alert for the stale case** (unlike 141's billing path): the 141 push is dormant in prod (`admin_alert_user_id` unset) and firing an alert from the frequently-hit `GET /daily-loop` read path has no natural dedup; the ops fix for orphaned generations is the same env seed as 141. A real analysis on the day still outranks any status row (`has_analysis` short-circuits before the staleness check). **144.2 client max-wait cap:** `CheckInPage.tsx` arms a single `window.setTimeout(BRIEF_MAX_WAIT_MS = 12 min)` when the wait begins (keyed on `queuedAtMs`), cleared the moment the wait ends (brief lands or a `failed` signal nulls `queuedAtMs`); on elapse it self-resolves to the same retryable `ErrorState`, folded into `generationFailed` via a new `waitTimedOut` flag (reset in the save mutation's `onMutate` so a retry re-submits cleanly). This is the belt-and-suspenders for when the envelope is slow to report `failed` (e.g. a stale service-worker `NetworkFirst` cache, Batch 138). **Home needs no separate client timer** — `DashboardPage` is purely envelope-driven, so 144.1 alone flips its `BriefGeneratingCta` → `BriefFailedCta` on a cold reopen. **Boundaries:** verdict + Green/Amber/Red + #133/#135/Red-never-VO2 untouched; **no migration, no shared-schema change** (the `briefGeneration` envelope field + its `reason: string | null` already accept `"stale"`); the 141 clean-failure + ready paths unchanged; no `ARCHITECTURE.md` data-model change beyond a §5 as-built note on the existing `brief_generation_status` row. **Verification (local, green):** backend +3 non-DB tests in `test_brief_generation_status.py` (a `generating` row past the threshold reads `failed`/`stale`; a fresh one stays `generating`; a real analysis still outranks a stale row — all pure `_serialize_brief_generation` calls with an injected `now`), pytest **652 passed / 231 skipped**, `ruff check` + `format --check` + `mypy src` clean (114 files); web +1 `CheckInPage.test.tsx` case (a wait past the cap resolves to the retry card, not an endless spinner — driven by spying `window.setTimeout`, capturing the one >10-min backstop callback, and invoking it, so no fake clock fights react-query/userEvent), vitest **347 passed / 55 files**, `tsc --noEmit` (web + shared)/eslint/production build clean (Node 20). Ops (not a code phase, carried from 141): seed `admin_alert_user_id` in prod to light the push channel. **Shipped** via PR #171 (squash `dbc47b8c61d3ce496ba5390777ae7c86e9430826`); both CI waves (branch-push + PR-context) passed all 7 jobs (Build web, Lint ruff, Migration check, Security audit, mypy, pytest, web + shared) plus the Vercel preview. **Production verified on the merge SHA** — Railway `/api/v1/health` + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both served it (no migration → fast redeploy), the Vercel production deployment READY at the merge commit, web `/` + `/check-in` `200`, unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` `401` both direct and via the Vercel proxy. The on-device eyeball of a real orphaned/stuck generation resolving (Home CTA flip + check-in retry card) is deferred to live use — needs an actual stuck generation to trigger; the two discriminating tests stand in ([[ui-walkthrough-method]]). Tier 🔴 High.

224. **Batch 145 grades a structured ride on credible executed boundaries in the order Garmin laps → validated target-power trace → planned clock; this supersedes the batch plan's assumption that a Zwift workout would expose one Garmin lap per step.** Implemented on `feat/batch-145-actual-lap-grading` from Batch 145 (`docs/phase-batches.md`); surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-23 feedback after two true ~250 W sweet-spot efforts were reported as 226–232 W because the planned 2×25-minute clock swept the 170 W recovery into work he had shortened to 2×15. **Evidence/decision at `/batch-start`: capture splits, but do not pretend they solve the affected ride.** The canonical `~/garmin-spike/out/activity_summary.json` contains only `lapCount`; the separate `activity_splits.json` has timestamped `lapDTOs`, so `GarminConnectClient.fetch_activity_payloads` now fetches splits for cycling activities and stores the raw response under existing `activities.raw_summary.activitySplits` (optional endpoint failure preserves prior captured splits and never fails the established summary/time-series sync). No migration. But both the canonical controlled workout and a read-only check of Mark's affected Garmin activity expose exactly **one whole-ride lap** (`hasSplits=false`), not step boundaries. Shipping only the lap fetch would leave the bug intact. **Boundary policy:** `segment_ride_intervals` first accepts Garmin laps only when count, total duration, and target-power fit make them credible matches for the planned steps. When Garmin supplies the common whole-ride lap, a 5-second dynamic-time-warp alignment may map the executed power trace to the known structured IR target sequence; it is fail-closed — requires FTP and power, ≤8 %FTP RMSE, and at least a 1 %FTP-point fit improvement over the planned clock. A noisy/non-matching trace retains Batch 44's planned-duration windows, while a planless/free ride still returns no intervals and uses the whole-ride read. Every interval and the execution summary records `boundarySource` (`actual_laps` / `actual_trace` / `planned_durations`) plus an honest note. **Real regression check:** run read-only against the affected Garmin details at the production sync's `maxchart=2000`, the one-lap path recovers the two long work blocks as **900 s at 249.5 W and 900 s at 249.6 W**, with the ~170 W recovery isolated and all four work efforts (including the two 30-second primers) on target. `PROMPT_VERSION` → `post-workout-analysis-v8-2026-07-24`, so old reads are stale; the existing prompt rule (grade work intervals, whole-ride average is context only), role classifier, grade math, recovery decision, verdict + Green/Amber/Red, #133/#135, and Red-never-VO2 are unchanged. Tier 🔴 High.

225. **Batch 146 restores Mark's requested literal 0–10 morning feel input as a slider plus number field, with the existing words kept as anchors; the finer number feeds the unchanged verdict gates while the read keeps speaking the word.** Implemented on `feat/batch-146-numeric-feel` from Batch 146 (`docs/phase-batches.md`); surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-23 feedback. **UI choice at `/batch-start`: slider/number over 11 chips.** Eleven buttons would have made every possible value one tap, but on Mark's phone it would either wrap awkwardly or shrink the tap targets; a range slider with a visible numeric input gives exact 0–10 entry, keeps the card compact, and preserves the old Rough/Meh/OK/Good/Great anchors as quick buttons at 2/4/6/8/10. **Contract:** `subjectiveScore` is explicitly integer 0–10 in shared Zod and the FastAPI request models; serializers now preserve `"0"` instead of treating it as empty/null. **Narrative + verdict boundary:** `subjective_score_label` / `subjectiveFeelLabel` map the full scale to the nearest word anchor, so the morning packet still carries `subjectiveLabel` and the prompt's "never raw subjectiveScore / 6/10" rule remains sufficient; no prompt bump is needed because the packet and instruction shape did not change. The deterministic verdict threshold remains exactly the existing one: `<5` is Amber, `>=5` does not itself downgrade. **Boundaries:** no migration, no analysis prompt-version bump, no Green/Amber/Red, #133/#135, or Red-never-VO2 change. Tier 🟢 Mid.

226. **Batch 147 adds a narrow primary-interval-block editor to Home, using an additive source grammar and the existing proposal/delivery rail; unlike Batch 88's explicit free-form authoring path, edits here keep Red-never-VO2 hard.** Implemented on `feat/batch-147-interval-editor` from Batch 147 (`docs/phase-batches.md`), surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-23 request to control the exact rep count and independent work/rest duration, power, and cadence of an app-suggested ride. **Decisions settled at `/batch-start`:** (1) source/API durations are integer `durationSec` and power is absolute integer `powerPct`; (2) v1 edits only the primary interval block (the longest repeated legacy interval set, so a short primer cannot win), with every other step deep-copied unchanged; (3) ship all deterministic presets — Keep current, Scale down (75% duration / 90% power), Sweet Spot (3×10 min @90%, 5-min recovery), and Z2 (1×45 min @65%, zero recovery); (4) bounds are reps 1–20, work 30 sec–120 min, rest 0–60 min, power 40–150% FTP, cadence 40–130 rpm; (5) use numeric phone controls on the Home Today card; (6) this coached edit path has no Red-day bypass. **Implementation:** `workout_delivery` accepts a new `{"label","block":{"repeat","work":{durationSec,powerPct,cadenceRpm},"rest":{...}}}` step and expands independent cadence per leg; zero rest emits no recovery step, while the legacy `pattern`/`repeats` form is unchanged. A pure mapper reads native or legacy sessions, surfaces fixed warm-up/cool-down/primer context, and writes only the chosen block into a fresh source document. Approval validates the stored morning verdict before any new version is written, versions the `PlannedWorkout`, rebuilds its exact IR, and reuses proposal approve/push or in-place live-event replacement; the Today-card table then offers editable Current/Change-to values and one approval button. **Boundaries:** additive JSON/shared-schema/API only, no migration; existing free-form authoring, verdict logic, Green/Amber/Red, #133/#135, and prompt versions are untouched. **Verification:** backend pytest 661 passed / 234 skipped, Ruff/format/mypy clean; web vitest 349 passed, typecheck/lint/build clean; shared 21 passed + typecheck; 390px browser check verified the Z2 edge case and no console errors. Tier 🔴 High.

227. **Batch 148 grounds calendar-week training history in a new factual planned → changed → executed packet and demotes the nominal routine in both morning and weekly-review prompts.** Implemented on `feat/batch-148-training-week-grounding` from Batch 148 (`docs/phase-batches.md`), surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-24 report that a Tuesday VO2 moved to Saturday was still narrated as completed Tuesday. **Decisions settled at `/batch-start`:** (1) the window is calendar week-to-date (Monday through the analysis subject date), matching `weekly_mix`; (2) `workout_moved`/`workout_skipped`/`workout_removed`/`workout_replaced` audit rows are authoritative change events, while linked `PlannedWorkout` versions supply the session identity/date and active rows supply the final schedule; (3) v1 does not say “you followed the app's suggestion” because the audit does not durably link the action to the exact `verdict.swapSuggestion` that prompted it; (4) the weekly review reuses the same factual builder, while a monthly review continues to use its deterministic rollup and is equally forbidden from reconstructing history from `trainingSchedule`. **Execution boundary:** only Garmin `Activity` rows populate `days[].executed`; planned and changed entries explicitly say they are not completion evidence. Activity packets carry load, power, aerobic/anaerobic TE, and the matched planned session when a post-session `Analysis.planned_workout_id` proves one. A moved audit is represented on both its origin (`direction=out`) and destination (`direction=in`), so Tuesday can say VO2 moved out + easy Z2 executed while Saturday holds the moved-in VO2. Day status distinguishes executed, skipped, removed, planned/no-activity, changed/no-activity, and true rest rather than guessing that an absent activity was skipped. `morning-analysis-v15-2026-07-24` and `reviews-v4-2026-07-24` mark older narratives stale; both generation services now short-circuit only when the newest stored row already has the current prompt version, so a bump actually regenerates rather than only relabelling new rows. **Boundaries:** read-only existing tables; backend-only; no migration/shared-schema/verdict/Green-Amber-Red/#133/#135/Red-never-VO2 change. **Verification:** backend pytest 665 passed / 235 skipped; Ruff/format clean across 190 files; mypy clean across 116 source files. Tier 🔴 High.

### 2026-07-24 — Requirement (pre-scoping) — Learn from conversations with Mark

228. **Confirmed requirement (accepted product direction, now specced as Planned Batches 150–151): the coach should learn *durably* from its conversations with Mark — distilling standing facts, preferences, and recurring themes out of the follow-up brief chat, check-in notes/questions, post-workout notes, and corrections, and folding them into the persistent, editable knowledge base so context compounds instead of each exchange being forgotten.** Raised by Craig 2026-07-24; recorded here as accepted direction and decomposed (Planned) into **Batch 150** (post-workout follow-up chat) then **Batch 151** (learn from any chat) in `docs/phase-batches.md`; the scope decisions below carry into each batch's own `/batch-start`. **Why it's on-thesis:** the core thesis is to "hold that knowledge as living, editable state so he never writes a handover doc again" (ARCHITECTURE §Product thesis); today the app only *ingests* that knowledge when Mark or Craig types it into the KB, so conversation is a large, unused source of exactly the context the handover doc used to carry. **Why it's genuinely new (not already built):** the two adjacent loops both deliberately stop short of durable learning — the brief follow-up chat (Batch 119 / #194) is grounded only in one brief's `context_packet` and is barred from any claim beyond it, and the rate-&-correct loop (#137 lineage, `recent_corrections` / `RecentCorrection.to_packet()`) feeds a correction *forward into the next generation's packet only*, per-analysis, never distilled into standing state. **Hard guardrail (the central constraint):** conversational learning must never become a lever on the verdict. The 2026-07-10 coaching-integrity audit named sycophancy/gaming the top acute risk, and a naive "learn from what he says" loop *is* that failure mode (learn to always say Green because he likes Green; drift cautious→aggressive because he pushes back). So this is scoped to durable *facts / preferences / terminology / recurring context* (equipment names, foods that disagree with him, schedule constraints, stated goals, chronic niggles) — **not** verdict thresholds, the data-quality rules, or anything that overrides the data-grounded Green/Amber/Red framework (#133/#135, Red-never-VO2 and the data-quality rules stay exactly as they are). Learned items surface as **editable KB entries Mark/Craig can see and correct (extract-and-confirm), not silent auto-application**, consistent with the "living, editable state" thesis and the Mark-facing Coach-memory read (#202). **Open scope questions for the design decision / `/batch-start`:** (1) which surfaces feed it (brief chat, check-in notes, post-workout notes, corrections — likely all); (2) extract-and-confirm vs auto-apply (thesis + integrity lean confirm); (3) the durable-vs-transient taxonomy (keep facts/preferences/patterns; never persist today's mood/fatigue); (4) where learned knowledge lands (new `knowledge_base` sections vs structured profile fields vs the experiment/hypothesis tracker for recurring themes); (5) trigger + cost (a batched periodic distillation pass over recent conversations vs per-turn); (6) privacy (same paid-API / no-third-party stance, RLS on any new store). No code, migration, or verdict change accompanies this entry — it records the requirement only.

229. **Batch 149 refines the Week tab from Decision #103's rolling 14-day plan window into two profile-local Monday-start calendar weeks, while keeping elapsed days read-only in the admin organiser.** Implemented on `feat/batch-149-monday-week-view` from Batch 149 (`docs/phase-batches.md`), surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-24 feedback that Week should begin on a set day rather than on whatever day he opens it. The frontend derives `today` with `localTodayIso(player.timezone)`, computes the date-only Monday via `weekStartForDate`, includes that Monday in the React Query key, and calls the existing generic schedule endpoint as `/api/v1/plan-actions/schedule?start_date={monday}&days=14`; the API default remains unchanged for other callers. The first seven returned days remain "This week" and the next seven remain "Coming up", but they are now real Mon-Sun calendar weeks, aligned with `weekly_mix`, weekly reviews, and Batch 148's factual training-week window. **Admin write boundary:** elapsed dates earlier than profile-local today stay visible and tappable for read-only workout/activity detail, but day/workout add/build/move/edit/skip/remove controls are hidden; the move sheet keeps past dates visible as disabled "Past day" targets with an unavailable note. Today/future controls remain active, completed planned work from elapsed days still counts toward the week totals, Batch 133 activity chips stay read-only and no-double-counted, and current-week banner actions (`Rearrange week`, `Light reset`/`Restore week`) remain available because they target the containing week, not a past date. **Boundaries:** frontend-only; no API/shared-schema/migration/plan-engine change; verdict logic, Green/Amber/Red, #133/#135, and Red-never-VO2 untouched. **Verification:** `WeekAheadPage.test.tsx` 22 passed, full web vitest 352 passed, web `tsc --noEmit` clean, eslint clean apart from six known Fast Refresh warnings, and production build clean. Tier 🟢 Mid.

230. **Batch 150 extends the existing brief follow-up chat rail to post-workout reads as advisory-only chat, mounted only inside the Home post-workout read disclosure.** Implemented on `feat/batch-150-post-workout-chat` from Batch 150 (`docs/phase-batches.md`) as the first conversational-learning prerequisite from #228. **Kickoff decisions:** (1) post-workout chat is **advisory-only** — it never offers a proposal affordance for tomorrow or any completed-session adjustment; (2) v1 mounts the chat on the existing Home post-workout disclosure only, not a new dedicated read view. **Why:** `brief_messages` and `/api/v1/briefs/{analysis_id}/messages` are already keyed to any `analyses.id`, and post-workout/strength/flexibility/walk reads already store the same `context_packet` + `output_markdown` grounding material, so adding another table, endpoint, or migration would duplicate a working ownership/turn-cap/history model. The only morning-specific behavior is the deterministic "propose today's ride" affordance from #194; that stays explicitly gated to `analysis_type == "morning"` so completed-session reads remain advice and explanation, not plan writes. The prompt now speaks about a generic "read" and includes `Read type` beside the stored packet/text, while preserving the same floors: Red-never-VO2, no L/R balance, local times, and no skipped/holiday workout narrated as live. **Boundaries:** no new table/endpoint/migration/shared-schema change; Green/Amber/Red, #133/#135, Red-never-VO2, and the morning brief chat's existing proposal path are unchanged. Tier 🟢 Mid.

231. **Batch 151 uses an explicit, batched Coach-memory distillation pass; candidates live in a dedicated RLS-backed proposal table and can write only the versioned `learned_context` KB section after confirmation.** Implemented on `feat/batch-151-conversational-learning` from Batch 151 (`docs/phase-batches.md`) as the substantive learning half of #228. **Kickoff decisions:** (1) **storage** — migration `021` adds `conversation_learning_proposals` rather than overloading KB rows: each user-scoped row keeps kind, statement, evidence, fingerprint, pending/accepted/rejected status, reviewer, and timestamps, giving review lifecycle, deduplication, and RLS a real home; (2) **destination** — all accepted facts/preferences/terminology/recurring themes append to a new version of one `knowledge_base.section='learned_context'`, not profile fields, `coaching_protocol`, `data_quality_rules`, or the experiment tracker; (3) **trigger** — Coach memory exposes an explicit "Look for new memories" action that batches the last 30 days of user-authored chat across every analysis type plus check-in/post-workout notes and corrections. It does not spend an Anthropic call per turn and adds no cron; repeated passes deduplicate; (4) **review** — Mark or Craig can edit then accept, or reject. Pending/rejected proposals never enter KB or packets. **Integrity boundary:** Anthropic receives only user-authored sources (assistant turns cannot be evidence), must return a strict typed JSON destination and verbatim source IDs/quotes, and code independently verifies the quote, destination, durable-vs-transient taxonomy, duplicates, and forbidden verdict/data-quality/sycophancy language. Acceptance re-validates edited text and has exactly one write destination. Morning and ride/strength/flexibility/walk packets expose confirmed items through `learnedContext` with `classificationImpact='none'`; deterministic Green/Amber/Red, #133/#135, Red-never-VO2, thresholds, data-quality rules, and objective metrics are unchanged and unreachable from this write path. **Privacy/cost:** same existing Anthropic boundary and private backend; no new provider; source evidence/proposals are user-scoped and the new table is RLS-enabled. ### 2026-07-25 — Batch 152 — Completed-workout read on the Week view + ERG-always profile

232. **Batch 152 surfaces the persisted post-workout read on the Week-view detail sheet and records Mark's ERG-always indoor setup as durable profile context.** Implemented on `feat/batch-152-week-read-erg` from Batch 152 (`docs/phase-batches.md`), surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-25 feedback. **Feedback #1 — look back at how a session went.** Tapping a completed workout previously showed only the planned structure; the post-workout read already persists in `analyses` keyed to `planned_workout_id` (and `activity_id`) and Home shows it the day of, but the Week rail carried no link to it. A new read-only `GET /api/v1/plan-actions/workouts/{planned_workout_id}/analysis` returns the latest read for that workout (user-scoped; a foreign/unknown id yields `read: null`, the same honest empty state as "no read yet") — a retrieval, never a regeneration. `WorkoutDetailSheet` fetches it lazily when a completed session opens and renders verdict + Markdown alongside the retained planned structure, reusing Home's read stack — `FeedbackControl` (rate/correct) and `BriefFollowUpChat` (advisory follow-up) — so an old session is as interactive as a fresh one. **Kickoff decision:** include the full feedback + chat stack, not read-only. **Feedback #2 — ERG-always.** Mark rides every Zwift session in ERG and chose ERG-on always, accepting that it softens his one weekly short-sharp-sprint session, because toggling ERG off mid-session is impractical and can cause trainer issues. The post-workout packet now also carries the session's `prescribedErgMode` (VO2 micro-interval protocols prescribe ERG off — surge lag), and the prompt (bumped `post-workout-analysis-v9-2026-07-25`) trusts ERG-held power as delivered, never frames it as under-performance, and never suggests switching ERG off. **Kickoff decision (honest-note, not silent):** when an ERG-off surge protocol was ridden in ERG, the read adds one plain line that the surges were smoothed so the neuromuscular hit was lighter — without suggesting he change anything, consistent with the app's coaching-integrity stance and distinct from the "switch it off" advice Mark rejected. **Boundaries:** new read-only endpoint + KB content update only; no migration, no shared-schema change beyond the read DTO; verdict logic, Green/Amber/Red, #133/#135, and Red-never-VO2 untouched. **Ops:** Mark's live `profile` KB row predates this fact and the seed builder only fills missing sections, so the ERG fact is applied to prod at closeout via the existing versioned `PUT /coaching-state/knowledge-base/profile` admin path. **Verification:** backend ruff/format/mypy clean; targeted pytest 34 passed / 33 skipped (the Postgres-backed endpoint + packet tests run in CI); web tsc/eslint/build clean and `WorkoutDetailSheet.test.tsx` covers the read, the honest empty state, and the feedback + chat stack. ### 2026-07-26 — Batch 158 — Delivery-rail atomicity & observability

238. **Batch 158 treats the persisted delivered IR as the cloud-content fingerprint, advances delivery pointers only after intervals.icu confirms success, and reuses proposal rows for durable structured failures rather than adding a sync-state migration.** Implemented on `feat/batch-158-delivery-atomicity` from CR153-01/CR153-08. **Atomicity:** every rail method with `commit=False` now leaves transaction ownership to its caller on failure; interval approval can also flush without committing. `_deliver_one`, `_resync_event`, and interval-edit approval call the cloud first and only then repoint `planned_workout_id` / `planned_workout_version`. Reconciliation's idempotency check now requires those columns *and* the `structured_workout_ir` snapshot's planned-workout id/version to match, so a false pointer left by the pre-fix path self-heals instead of suppressing the retry. Legitimate move/swap re-slotting updates that same IR fingerprint after the cloud move, preserving idempotency without weakening the stale-pointer check. **Failure/retry contract:** an interval edit whose cloud update fails deliberately persists the edited plan as desired state, a failed proposal, and the error while leaving the old live event pointer/content honest; the next reconciliation updates the same cloud event and resolves the failed proposal to pushed. Generic re-sync and baseline reconciliation persist only their owned failure state and retry without a duplicate cloud create. **Observability:** existing `workout_delivery_proposals.last_error` now carries compact JSON with code, stage, retryability, detail, user id, workout id, and version; malformed/missing-FTP workouts get a deduplicated failed proposal plus a structured warning, while intentional non-bike and exact-version no-ops remain row-free. Bike-typed rows with malformed structure are allowed through the indoor filter specifically so they cannot disappear silently. The stronger failure path surfaced four legacy generated bike sessions whose prose targets could not become Zwift steps; their easy portions now carry explicit `% FTP` targets, with a pure generator-to-delivery regression test. **Boundaries:** no migration or shared/frontend contract change; no prompt, verdict, threshold, plan-rule, #133/#135, or Red-never-VO2 change. **Verification:** Ruff/format clean, mypy clean across 118 source files, and backend pytest **668 passed / 250 expected PostgreSQL skips** locally; new DB-backed cases cover `commit=False`, generic replace failure/retry, legacy false-pointer healing, interval-edit failure→reconciliation, malformed IR, missing FTP, and intentional no-op. Branch CI run **30207939387** passed all seven jobs, including **918 PostgreSQL-backed tests** and the Alembic round trip. **Closeout:** shipped via PR #187, squash `bf7474b`; branch, PR-context, and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs. Vercel production reached READY, Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health served exact SHA `bf7474b`, web `/` returned 200, unauthenticated workout-delivery proposals returned 401 direct and proxied, and Vercel's one-hour runtime-error scan was clean. Tier 🔴 High.

### 2026-07-26 — Batch 161 — Concurrency, idempotency & workload budgets

241. **Batch 161 keeps the three related concurrency fixes in one release, with activation fixed first inside the same branch rather than split into a standalone batch.** **Activation:** `POST /auth/activate` consumes an eligible code with one conditional `UPDATE … RETURNING`; only the transaction that receives the user id can mint a device credential. The PWA also coalesces an in-flight activation by code so React StrictMode/remount cannot make a second exchange. **Paid-generation identity:** migration `024` adds RLS-enabled `generation_requests` with a unique stable identity, `running`/`completed`/`failed`, a three-minute reclaimable lease, and an optional `analyses.id`. A transaction-scoped PostgreSQL advisory lock is keyed to the artifact scope (user/date morning; user/activity post-session), while the identity includes the substantive check-in input and prompt version. Post-session status/linking is committed before the claim so Batch 159's `generating` state remains externally visible; no later commit can release the lock before the completed/failed request state is atomic. Identical tabs/retries reuse the completed analysis and one paid call; failed/expired work is reclaimable; genuinely changed input/prompt makes a new request and `analyses` row, so Decision #219's intentional history remains intact and `analyses` receives no uniqueness constraint. **Workload boundary:** every Anthropic call and Piper synthesis runs through a zero-queue fail-fast slot: at most one active call per user, four Anthropic calls globally, and one Piper call globally per API process. Excess same-user work is 429; exhausted global capacity is 503; `finally` releases slots after errors/timeouts. User-triggered Anthropic routes share `30/hour`, TTS shares `60/hour`, and the limiter key is the verified profile id placed on request state, so switching among a user's device tokens cannot multiply the allowance. **Coverage:** separate-session PostgreSQL tests exercise activation winner/loser and morning/post-session coalescing/history/reclamation; TTS tests exercise unique-input cache bypass, revoked tokens, timeout recovery, and no extra subprocess. **Boundaries:** no prompt, verdict, threshold, plan-rule, #133/#135, Red-never-VO2, hosting, or production change. Local gates pass (backend 663 passed / 271 expected PostgreSQL skips; full web 359 passed; Ruff/format/mypy/TypeScript/lint/build clean; Alembic single head `024`); branch CI run `30214334873` passed all seven jobs, including 934 PostgreSQL-backed tests and the Alembic round trip. **Closeout:** shipped via PR #190, squash `bc057ea`; final branch, PR-context, and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs. Vercel preview/production reached READY; Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health served exact SHA `bc057ea`; web `/` and `/activate` returned 200; unauthenticated daily-loop and TTS returned 401 direct and proxied; Railway/Vercel runtime-error scans were clean. Read-only production verification confirmed Alembic head `024`, RLS enabled, the expected ten-column `generation_requests` shape and one unique request-identity constraint, with zero rows. Tier 🔴 High.

242. **The installed home-screen PWA authenticates by entering the activation code/link inside the app itself, not only by opening the emailed `/activate?code=` link — because iOS gives a standalone home-screen web app a storage container isolated from Safari, so a device token minted through the Safari link never reaches the installed app.** Surfaced 2026-07-27: Mark's (and Craig's) home-screen icon kept showing "Set up this device" after a successful Safari activation. **Root cause:** Batch 160 (DS154-01) removed the last in-app credential entry — the display-name + PIN `LoginPage` — leaving the activation link as the sole path. The link stores the device token in `localStorage` (`apps/web/src/lib/tokens.ts`), which on iOS lives in Safari's container, not the standalone app's; the `start_url=/activate` + `coach_pending_activation_code` bridge (`apps/web/src/pages/ActivatePage.tsx`) can't cross that boundary (and is cleared on success), and activation codes are single-use so a Safari activation can't be replayed in the app. **Decision (Batch 172, 🟢 Mid, frontend-only):** `AccessPage` gains an activation field that accepts a full `/activate?code=` URL or a bare code, extracts the code, and calls the existing `activateDevice(code)` from `useAuth()` — provisioning the standalone app's own storage; on success it routes to `/`. The Safari/desktop link flow is unchanged. iOS home-screen setup is now: add to Home Screen → open the app → paste the link/code (do not tap it in Safari first, which consumes the single-use code). No backend change, no migration (Alembic head stays `024`). Number assigned at `/batch-start`. **Shipped** via PR #191, squash `3449c7e`; both the branch-push and PR-event CI runs passed all seven jobs plus Vercel preview, and production verification confirmed Railway health at exact SHA `3449c7e`, web `/` + `/access` 200, the new activation field rendering on prod `/access` with a clean console, and unauthenticated daily-loop 401 direct and proxied.

### 2026-07-28 — Batch 162 — Learning-memory integrity

243. **Conversation-memory confirmation is exact-wording only, revalidates current user-owned evidence, and every downstream prompt receives learned statements solely as untrusted quoted data.** Implemented on `fix/batch-162-learning-memory-integrity` for DS154-02/DS154-07. **Acceptance design:** Batch 151's edit-before-accept path is retired rather than relying on an approximate semantic checker to bless caller-authored rewrites. Extraction may still produce a concise supported third-person paraphrase, but candidate filtering now requires deterministic lexical support from the cited verbatim quotes and rejects instruction-shaped statements; confirmation can accept only the stored proposal wording. Before the KB write, the service re-resolves every `chat:` / `checkin:` / `correction:` source under the authenticated profile, confirms it is still user-authored and still contains the cited quote, and re-runs the candidate filter. Missing, changed, foreign, malformed, or unsupported evidence leaves the proposal pending and writes no KB version. The Coach-memory UI and shared request contract therefore expose confirm/reject, not editing; stale cached clients that send changed text receive 422. **Prompt boundary:** `learned_context_packet` no longer mixes a natural-language rule with raw items. It emits only `classificationImpact='none'`, `contentRole='untrusted_user_data'`, and `untrustedQuotedData[{kind,quote}]`; morning plus ride/strength/mobility/walk system prompts all carry one fixed higher-priority guardrail that treats those quotes as data, never instructions, and ignores any quote that conflicts with deterministic verdicts, measurements, data-quality rules, or safety gates. All five prompt versions advance so old artifacts regenerate. `_morning_verdict` remains structurally unable to consume learned memory. **Ownership posture:** DB-backed endpoint coverage proves a second profile lists no owner proposals and gets 404 for accept/edit/reject, with the proposal still pending and no KB version written. A route-inventory lint walks every registered API route and fails unless it is one of four explicit public routes or resolves `CurrentUser`/`AdminUser`. **Boundaries:** no migration, provider, cron, deterministic verdict, threshold, #133/#135, Red-never-VO2, plan, or delivery change; Batch 169's memory cap/decay stays separate. Local full gates pass (backend 666 passed / 273 expected PostgreSQL skips; shared 24; full web suite; Ruff/format/mypy/TypeScript/lint/build clean). **Shipped** via PR #192, squash `3a58debe`: branch-push, PR-context, and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs; Vercel preview/production reached READY; Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health served the exact merge SHA; web `/` + `/coach-state` returned 200; unauthenticated learning-route access returned 401 direct and proxied; and the Vercel runtime-error scan was clean. Tier 🔴 High.

245. **Batch 164 hardens dependency, operator-artifact, backup, and RLS-drift posture without bundling the larger React Router 7 or least-privilege-backend-role migrations.** Implemented on `chore/batch-164-security-ops` for DS154-03/04/06. **Supply chain:** PostCSS is updated to 8.5.24, clearing the audited High advisory. The Railway Dockerfile now uses digest-pinned `python:3.12.11-slim-bookworm` and installs that locked Python runtime with hash enforcement. **Operator artifacts/backups:** Garmin and Hive bootstrap scripts no longer print bearer blobs by default; operators must choose explicit `--stdout` or a secure `--env-output` file written atomically at 0600 under a 0700 directory. The scheduled `pg_dump` is retained but narrowed to `--schema=coach`, writes through a hidden partial file, chmods the backup dir/file to 0700/0600, removes failed partials, and prunes to the latest seven dumps. **RLS posture:** migration `025` hardens the four legacy policy-bearing tables with explicit `TO authenticated`, `(select auth.uid())`, and `WITH CHECK` on update policies, and fixes `coach.set_updated_at()` with `search_path=coach, pg_temp` plus public execute revoked. The larger FastAPI least-privilege role plus `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` change remains deferred because it changes the backend ownership model and needs a separate role/policy design. A read-only `scripts/check_rls_posture.py` can fail CI/deploy when `RLS_POSTURE_DATABASE_URL` is present if any `coach` table loses RLS, Data API roles gain `coach` privileges, exposed schemas drift, legacy policies lose the authenticated role/checks, or functions lose their safe search path. **Boundaries:** no prompt, deterministic verdict, threshold, #133/#135, Red-never-VO2, plan, delivery, or production config mutation.
