### 2026-07-28 — Batch 167 — Deterministic training-load verdict cap

248. **Batch 167 closes coaching-integrity finding F2 / R155-A with a one-way Amber ceiling driven by either computed ACWR ≥1.50 or Garmin recovery time >24 hours; the existing `load_driven` interpretation remains descriptive but can no longer relax a high-load day to Green.** Implemented on `fix/batch-167-training-load-cap` after the explicit `/batch-start 167` request. **Threshold/source decision:** use both already-captured signals with OR semantics and no migration. The acute:chronic ratio is computed from Garmin's captured acute/chronic training-load values; 1.50 keeps the audit's proposed fast-ramp boundary but is treated as a conservative workload flag, not an individual injury prediction, because ACWR threshold research has material methodological limitations. Garmin defines recovery time as the time remaining until the next hard workout; more than 24 hours therefore means the athlete will not be ready for hard work within the coming day. Missing or sub-threshold load data is neutral. **Verdict boundary:** `_morning_verdict` receives an explicit load packet, evaluates the ordinary acute ladder first, then changes only Green→Amber when either threshold fires. Amber remains Amber and Red remains Red; the acute sleep/HRV/Poor-readiness gates, subjective downgrade-only rule, #133/#135, and Red-never-VO2 are unchanged. A Low readiness may still be labelled `readinessInterpretation="load_driven"` when clean recovery signals justify that reading, but `verdict.trainingLoadCap` is authoritative and sets the Amber ceiling. The structured cap packet records values, thresholds, trigger sources, pre-cap status, reasons, and whether the cap changed the colour; `safetyRulesApplied` records `training_load_amber_cap`. The prompt is bumped to `morning-analysis-v17-2026-07-28` so prose explains the deterministic cap and can never soften or model-set it. **Regression:** the 2026-07-24 audit shape (Low readiness + clean recovery + hard yesterday + >24h recovery time) now resolves Amber rather than the previous load-driven Green, and a severity matrix proves the new input never makes any existing light greener. **Boundaries:** backend + architecture/decision documentation only; no migration, shared schema, plan, delivery, auth, or hosting change. **Shipped via PR #197, squash `596b2b9`:** branch-push, PR-context, and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs; Vercel preview/production and Railway production passed exact-SHA verification; web/readiness/protected daily-loop smokes passed. Tier 🔴 High.

### 2026-07-29 — Batch 168 — Absolute readiness anchor + baseline-drift alarm

249. **Batch 168 closes coaching-integrity finding F1 / R155-B by anchoring the soft-sleep readiness floor at 60 and surfacing a deterministic warning when the trailing 84-day readiness distribution materially declines; the warning does not itself set the verdict.** Implemented on `fix/batch-168-baseline-drift-anchor` after the explicit `/batch-start 168` request. **Absolute-floor decision:** use `max(personal readiness median, 60)`. Sixty is not a new speculative physiological threshold — it is the already-shipped no-personal-baseline fallback. Making it the lower bound means accumulated history can make the override stricter but never more permissive than the fallback, closes the audit's readiness-52 / drifted-median-50 Probe 4, preserves Mark's real readiness-66 soft-sleep Green case, and remains well below the old generic 70 gate that Decision #129 deliberately removed. **Trend decision:** compare calendar-based medians of the first and second 42-day halves of the trailing 84-day window, require at least 21 readiness samples per half, and trigger on a second-half decline of at least 5 points. The 21/half coverage requires half of each period to be observed; the 5-point threshold follows the existing review rule that treats a four-point readiness move as stable noise. Calendar halves prevent missing days from shifting old samples into the recent period. The structured `verdict.readinessBaselineTrend` packet records coverage, medians, delta, thresholds, dates, and `verdictImpact="warning_only"`; a triggered alarm is appended to verdict reasons and prompt v18 must state it plainly. **Warning rather than cap:** Batch 168's absolute floor closes the verdict backdoor. Turning the chronic trend into a colour cap would pre-empt Batch 171's explicit choice between a deload action and a verdict cap, so this batch makes the slow decline visible without silently inventing that later action. The alarm cannot soften any Green/Amber/Red result. **Boundaries:** backend + architecture/decision documentation only; no migration, shared schema, plan, delivery, auth, hosting, acute sleep/HRV/Poor-readiness gate, subjective rule, #133/#135, or Red-never-VO2 change. Deterministic tests cover Probe 4, preserved healthy behavior, sustained decline, noise/coverage, prompt ownership, and a Green/Amber/Red no-softening matrix. Tier 🔴 High.

### 2026-07-29 — Batch 169 — Memory cap and correction decay

250. **Batch 169 closes coaching-integrity finding F9 / R155-C, plus F7's decay half, with read-time bounds on confirmed learned context and fed-forward corrections rather than a schema migration or a background pruning job.** Implemented on `feat/batch-169-memory-decay` after the explicit `/batch-start 169` request. **Learned-context policy:** `learned_context_packet` emits at most the latest 12 eligible confirmed items. Dated items older than 365 days decay out of the prompt; undated legacy items are still eligible but sort oldest, so they survive only when the cap has room. Twelve is deliberately small enough to keep prompt growth bounded while leaving space for the current handful of durable preferences/facts; one year is long enough for stable profile-like context to remain useful, while preventing old conversational residue from quietly warming tone forever. The packet remains `classificationImpact='none'`, `contentRole='untrusted_user_data'`, and quoted-only. **Correction policy:** `FeedbackService.recent_corrections` keeps the existing five-row cap but now filters to corrections/reason-tags created in the last 45 days. Corrections are per-read repair signals, not permanent facts; 45 days keeps genuinely recent "you misread this" context alive across several reads without letting a repeated correction steer narrative tone indefinitely. Morning and post-workout prompt versions advance to `morning-analysis-v19-2026-07-29` and `post-workout-analysis-v11-2026-07-29`, and both prompts now say a correction can be weighed or acknowledged but can never restate an objective metric, completed workout, or deterministic verdict as better than the packet measures it. **Boundary:** no migration, cron, provider, shared schema, deterministic verdict, thresholds, #133/#135, Red-never-VO2, plan, delivery, auth, or hosting change. `_morning_verdict` still has no learned-memory parameter, and tests re-assert that invariant. Local gates pass: backend pytest 690 passed / 279 expected PostgreSQL skips; targeted memory/correction/prompt tests 74 passed / 31 expected skips; Ruff/format clean; mypy clean across 123 source files. Tier 🟢 Mid.

### 2026-07-29 — Batch 170 — Verdict-ladder hardening

251. **Batch 170 closes coaching-integrity findings F3/F4/F5 with three deterministic one-way hardenings of `_morning_verdict`: credited-sleep Green needs complete corroboration, Poor readiness stacks with a second negative to Red, and missing evidence cannot satisfy a discretionary Green exception.** Implemented on `fix/batch-170-verdict-ladder-hardening` after the explicit `/batch-start 170` request. **Sleep-credit decision:** do not invent another raw-score threshold. When Garmin raw sleep is below 74 but the age-adjusted recompute reaches 74+, Green is allowed only when the already-settled #133 recovery bundle is complete — an actual HRV measurement with a clean status, resting HR inside its personal band, a measured readiness score at or above `max(personal median, 60)` and outside Low/Poor — plus a current subjective check-in ≥5. This lets age credit still lift a night within Red/Amber and preserves a genuinely corroborated age-appropriate Green, but the credit alone cannot cross the Green line. **Stacking decision:** Garmin Poor readiness plus any one of age-adjusted soft sleep 60–73, subjective <5, yesterday marked hard, or resting HR proved above its personal upper quartile escalates the existing Amber caution to Red. An RHR value with no usable baseline is neutral, not guessed elevated; acute sleep/HRV Red still wins independently. **Missing-data decision:** absence is not itself a negative and does not downgrade an otherwise raw-Green night, but absent HRV/readiness/check-in data cannot count as the positive evidence required by a soft-sleep exception, a credited-score crossing, or the Low-readiness `load_driven` relaxation. `verdict.sleepCreditCeiling`, `verdict.cumulativeEscalation`, explicit evidence booleans, and `safetyRulesApplied` make each decision inspectable; prompt v20 only explains the already-final Python light. **Boundaries:** backend + architecture/decision/status docs only; no migration, shared schema, plan, delivery, auth, hosting, age-credit math, load thresholds, or chronic-action change. The #133 personal/absolute readiness floor, #135 age-scoring model, acute Red/Amber gates, and Red-never-VO2 remain intact. The combined audit matrix proves credited Green→Amber, Poor-stack Amber→Red, missing HRV/check-in Green→Amber, clean soft-sleep Green preserved, and acute Red unchanged; separate regressions cover all four stacking signals, within-band sleep credit, missing readiness, missing-RHR-baseline neutrality, and honest neutral wording on a raw-Green night with absent HRV/check-in. Local gates pass: backend pytest 708 passed / 279 expected PostgreSQL skips; focused verdict/breathwork tests 79 passed / 7 expected skips; Ruff/format clean; mypy clean across 123 source files. Tier 🔴 High.

### 2026-07-29 — Batch 171 — Chronic decline drives an approval-gated deload

252. **Batch 171 closes coaching-integrity finding F6 by turning sustained recovery strain into a structural deload proposal on the existing delivery rail, while leaving the daily deterministic Green/Amber/Red verdict unchanged.** Implemented on `feat/batch-171-chronic-deload-action` after the explicit `/batch-start 171` request. **Action choice:** choose a proposal rather than a chronic verdict cap. The daily light remains the acute answer to “what is safe today”; a separate `verdict.chronicAction` makes the slower structural response explicit and keeps the change in this batch's 🟢 Mid tier. **Trigger:** fire when either (a) an already-qualified Batch 59 personal-baseline recovery marker (`readiness_score`, `hrv_7_day_avg_ms`, or `resting_heart_rate_bpm`) misses on at least 70% of its observed nights in the 28-day chronic window, preserving the existing 21-night and marker-history floors, or (b) at least two distinct latest-per-day morning verdicts are Red inside the rolling seven-day window. Sparse history, missing markers, one bad marker day, and one Red are neutral rather than guessed negative. **Action/delivery contract:** when triggered, create persistent approval-gated proposals for active indoor-bike workouts from the subject date through day six using the established safe adjustment shape — 75% duration, one-zone drop, explicit power cap, and no HIT/VO2 — then reuse propose→approve→push unchanged. Completed sessions are excluded. An existing acute pending adjustment wins, already-acted adjusted proposals are not replaced, and a stable per-workout/version audit tag makes reruns idempotent. The structured evidence packet records marker and Red-cluster sources, thresholds, reasons, `deliveryContract="propose_approve_push"`, and `verdictImpact="none"`; prompt v21 explains and surfaces the action but cannot recolour the daily verdict. The existing Batch 59 advisory packet and exact caution copy remain unchanged, so chronic advice and structural action coexist without a frontend/schema regression. **Boundaries:** backend + architecture/decision/status docs only; no migration, shared schema, new endpoint, provider, plan mutation, automatic Garmin push, auth, hosting, or frontend change. Local gates pass: backend pytest 712 passed / 282 expected PostgreSQL skips; focused chronic/morning/executable/scheduler tests 114 passed / 59 expected skips; Ruff/format clean; mypy clean across 123 source files. **Shipped via PR #201 / squash `d6cd890`:** branch-push, PR-context, and implementation-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs; Vercel preview/production reached Ready; exact-SHA Railway + Vercel health, web `/`, and protected daily-loop smokes passed. Tier 🟢 Mid.

### 2026-07-29 — Batch 173 — One coherent morning adjustment (timing + one scaling rule)

253. **Batch 173 makes the morning Amber/Red adjustment appear when the verdict does and describes it with one set of numbers everywhere.** Implemented on `feat/batch-173-adjustment-coherence` after the explicit `/batch-start 173` request; closes Mark's 2026-07-25/28/29 adjustment-coherence feedback (prod-confirmed root cause: proposal `bf964ccc` created at the 11:00 backstop, ~85 min after the verdict). **173.1 acted-on-today predicate (decided at `/batch-start`):** a ride is "acted on today" — and so never silently re-adjusted (Decision #29) — only when a delivery proposal for it carries an explicit approver (`approved_by_profile_id`, set solely by `approve`/`send_today`/interval-edit approve) **or** is `pushed` with an `adjustment.changed` IR (a manual `edit_today`). The push-on-plan-set baseline (`origin=as_planned`, `changed=False`, no approver — Decision #99) is deliberately **not** "acted on", so a pre-delivered ride still receives a *proposed* (never auto-pushed) morning adjustment that flips the Home card into approve-and-upload. The guard moved into `regenerate_for_verdict` (`_deliberately_acted`), so the check-in path (`regenerate_after_morning_checkin`, which dropped its old `ride_still_pending` gate) and the ungated 11:00 backstop now apply exactly the same rule; the backstop keeps proposing on pre-delivered rides. Not split into 173a/b. **173.2 one scaling rule + Zone-2 floor (decided at `/batch-start`):** a new leaf module `services/verdict_scaling.py` owns the canonical constants (`AMBER_DURATION_SCALE=0.75`, `ZONE_DROP_PCT=13`, `AMBER_POWER_CAP_PCT=98`, `RECOVERY_CAP_PCT=60`, `RED_DURATION_SCALE=0.5`, `MIN_POWER_PCT=45`, new `ENDURANCE_CEILING_PCT=75`) and one function `ease_amber_power_pct(p)`: a working step at or below the Zone-2 ceiling **keeps its intensity** (duration is cut only — so Mark's 67% Z2 ride stays 67%, not the delivery transform's 54% or the editor's 60%), while a harder step drops one zone floored at the Z2 ceiling and capped at threshold (HIT removed). The delivery transform (`adjust_ir_for_verdict`, moved here with `blocks_red_vo2`/`ir_has_vo2`/`_normalize_verdict`/`adjust_ir_for_chronic_deload` and re-exported from `executable_coaching` for compatibility), the interval-editor "Scale down" preset (`scale_block`), and the morning narrative all route through it, so a given ride yields identical numbers everywhere. Red is unchanged (recovery deliberately drops below Zone 2). The `adjustment.zoneDropPct` annotation now records the *actual* points shed (0 on a held Z2 ride), keeping it honest. **173.3 numbers in the packet:** `verdict.verdictAdjustment` carries planned-vs-adjusted duration and primary working %FTP plus `intensityHeldAtEndurance`/`removedHit`, computed via the *same* transform (`summarize_verdict_adjustment`); it is `classificationImpact="none"`, explanatory only, and absent on Green / no ride. The eased-ride Today action and prompt v22 (`morning-analysis-v22-2026-07-29`) quote those exact figures instead of guessing (unblocking Batch 175's "explain the app's own 60%/54%" half). **Boundaries:** backend + docs only; no migration, shared schema, new endpoint, provider, auth, hosting, or frontend change; delivery stays propose→approve→push (Decision #29) and push-on-plan-set (#99), the deterministic Green/Amber/Red ladder, #133/#135, and Red-never-VO2 are untouched. Local gates pass: backend pytest 721 passed / 284 expected PostgreSQL skips; new pure tests cover the Z2 hold, cross-path number equality (transform = editor = summary = `ease_amber_power_pct`), and packet exposure; new DB tests (CI) cover the pre-delivered-ride proposal at check-in and the acted-on guard; Ruff/format clean; mypy clean across 124 source files. Tier 🔴 High. **Shipped via PR #202 / squash `04efcb3`:** branch-push and PR-context CI passed all seven jobs; production verified on the merge SHA — Railway direct + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both returned `04efcb3`, web `/` 200, and protected `GET /api/v1/daily-loop` 401 direct and proxied.

### 2026-07-29 — Batch 174 — Honest analysis packets

254. **Batch 174 treats real scheduled rows and whole-day Garmin cost as analysis ground truth, while keeping both additions outside the deterministic verdict ladder.** Implemented on `feat/batch-174-analysis-packet-honesty` after the explicit `/batch-start 174` request. **174.1 upcoming-session precedence:** post-workout assembly queries all active, `status="planned"` `PlannedWorkout` rows whose dates are subject day +1 or +2, orders them by date/version, and serializes them through the existing planned-workout packet shape as `upcomingWorkouts`; skipped rows are excluded rather than narrated as live sessions. Split-session days remain representable because the query returns every qualifying row. Prompt v12 requires tomorrow impact to name the real row's date/title/duration and ranks it above `trainingPlan.weeklyRhythm`; the prose rhythm is a fallback only for a date with no actual row, so it can no longer rename or contradict a planned Long Z2 session. **174.2 kickoff decision — narrative-only, not verdict-hardening:** morning assembly now queries yesterday's `DailyMetric` regardless of whether an activity exists and adds `yesterdayLoad.wholeDayCost` with `allDayStressAvg`, `bodyBatteryDrained`, `bodyBatteryEnd`, and `classificationImpact="none"`. The pre-existing activity-derived `status` is labelled `statusScope="exercise_only"` so a zero-training day cannot be described as a low-cost whole day. Prompt v23 must discuss the whole-day signal independently of exercise totals. `_morning_verdict` still sees only the pre-existing activity status, `_training_load_signal` still exposes only ACWR and recovery time, and no stress/Body-Battery threshold is introduced; this is the conservative choice because current-day HRV/readiness/Body Battery already overlap with the signal and the reported defect is missing explanatory context, not proven ladder under-caution. **Boundaries:** backend + decision/status docs only; no migration, shared schema, endpoint, frontend, provider, auth, hosting, plan/delivery, threshold, #133/#135, or Red-never-VO2 change. Local gates pass: backend pytest 721 passed / 285 expected PostgreSQL skips; focused morning/post-workout tests 94 passed / 20 expected skips; Ruff/format clean; mypy clean across 124 source files. The DB-backed regressions run with Postgres in CI.
