Production verification returned exact SHA `b051b7c` from Railway and `garmin-coach-one.vercel.app`, with DB readiness and web `/` 200 and protected daily-loop 401 direct/proxied; the bare `garmin-coach.vercel.app` remains the known stale 404 alias.

### 2026-07-29 — Batch 175 — Brief-chat general science lane

255. **Batch 175 lets brief-chat answer general endurance-training science when clearly labelled, while keeping Mark-specific answers and prescription changes packet-bound.** Implemented on `feat/batch-175-brief-chat-science` after the explicit `/batch-start 175` request, closing Mark's 2026-07-28 chat refusal around "what %FTP could I drop VO2 intervals to and keep similar benefit?" **Kickoff decision — lane width and label:** use a broad but explicit established-endurance-science lane rather than a tiny curated whitelist. The prompt may answer non-personalized principles such as minimum-effective VO2 work, intensity/duration trade-offs, why an endurance zone matters, or recovery adaptation, but every such answer must start with the exact label `General principle:` and must not turn itself into advice for Mark unless the packet supports that personalization. This avoids another refusal for legitimate physiology questions while making provenance obvious. **Packet boundary:** Mark-specific metrics, plan details, history, readiness, and prescriptions remain packet-bound; when the packet lacks the fact, the answer must say so rather than guessing. **Action boundary:** actual workout changes still go through the existing confirm-before-apply/propose rail; the model cannot mutate the plan, and when Batch 173's `verdict.verdictAdjustment` figures are present in the packet the chat should quote those app-calculated numbers instead of inventing alternatives. Red-never-VO2 and all original read floors remain in force. **Boundaries:** single prompt-policy change in `services/brief_chat.py` plus tests/docs; no migration, backend-service rewrite, shared schema, frontend, provider, auth, hosting, deterministic verdict, plan/delivery rail, #133/#135, or Red-never-VO2 change. Local gates pass: backend pytest 722 passed / 288 expected skips; Ruff clean; mypy clean across 124 source files. Tier 🟢 Mid. **Shipped via PR #204 / squash `4aeb5c1`:** the first PR pytest run found newline-sensitive assertions in the new DB-backed tests, amended commit `9750d22` fixed them, and branch, PR-context, plus post-merge `main` CI then passed all seven jobs. Production verified exact-SHA Railway + Vercel health, web `/` 200, and protected brief-chat POST 401 direct/proxied.


### 2026-07-29 — Batch 176 — Bodyweight from Garmin → watts-per-kg in the ride read

256. **Batch 176 carries Mark's most recent Garmin weigh-in forward and computes watts-per-kg into the post-workout packet, closing his 2026-07-29 question-thread finding that the ride read could not give a W/kg figure.** Implemented on `feat/batch-176-weight-power-to-weight` after the explicit `/batch-start 176` request. **176.1 carry-forward, resolved at packet-build:** `weight_kg` on `daily_metrics` is only ever set on an exact same-day weigh-in (`_extract_weight_kg`), so it is null on most days. New `services/body_metrics.resolve_effective_weight_kg` queries the most recent non-null `weight_kg` within a 7-day lookback of the ride's local date (matching the window `fetch_daily_payloads` already uses for `get_weigh_ins`) and returns it with its own calendar date. No new column or migration: the value is resolved at read time rather than persisted, keeping this additive and consistent with Batch 177's packet-build overlay pattern; a stale weight is never presented as today's because `weightAsOfDate` travels with it. **176.2 powerToWeight packet:** the post-workout packet's `profile` block gains `weightKg`, `weightAsOfDate`, and `weightOnFile`; a new top-level `powerToWeight` block carries `ftpWattsPerKg` (headline), `wholeRideAvgWattsPerKg`, and `workIntervalAvgWattsPerKg` (averaged across `role="work"` intervals, reusing the existing interval-segmentation power data). Prompt v13 (`post-workout-analysis-v13-2026-07-29`) leads the performance read with W/kg when present; brief-chat inherits the figures for free since it prompts over the stored `context_packet` verbatim. **176.3 honest degrade:** when no weigh-in falls in the window, `powerToWeight` is `None` and `weightOnFile` is `false` — the prompt must say plainly that no recent weight is on file rather than estimating or dividing by a fabricated value. **Scope decided at `/batch-start`:** ride-only; morning-packet and trend-series surfacing (the acceptance criteria's optional scope) are deferred, not built, to keep this batch's Mid tier and because the reported gap was specifically the ride read. **Boundaries:** backend-only; no migration, shared-schema break, new endpoint, frontend, provider, auth, hosting, or verdict change — W/kg is explanatory-only and cannot move the deterministic Green/Amber/Red ladder or any floor. Local gates pass: backend pytest 722 passed / 293 expected PostgreSQL skips (new carry-forward and packet tests included; DB-backed cases run in CI); Ruff clean; mypy clean across 126 source files. Tier 🟢 Mid. **Shipped via PR #205 / squash `c14d154`:** branch-push, PR-context, and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs; production verified exact-SHA Railway + Vercel health, web `/` 200, and protected daily-loop 401 direct/proxied.

### 2026-07-30 — Batch 177 — VO2 max read live from Garmin, not the static profile 54

257. **Batch 177 overlays the live daily Garmin VO2max onto the packet profile at packet-build, so the coach narrates the real current value instead of the KB's hardcoded 54.** Implemented on `feat/batch-177-live-vo2max` after the explicit `/batch-start 177` request, closing Mark's 2026-07-29 question-thread finding that the coach admitted a fixed 54 while Garmin's live value is 55. **177.1 overlay, not write-through:** new `services/body_metrics.resolve_effective_vo2max` mirrors Batch 176's `resolve_effective_weight_kg` — most recent non-null `daily_metrics.vo2max` within a lookback window of the packet's subject date, returned with its own calendar date. **Kickoff decision — lookback window:** VO2max is recomputed far less often than a weigh-in (Garmin derives it from qualifying runs/rides, not daily), so the window is 90 days (`DEFAULT_VO2MAX_LOOKBACK_DAYS`), not the 7-day weight window. **Kickoff decision — overlay-only, no write-through:** the effective value is merged into `profile.athleteProfile.vo2max` at read time in both the morning packet (`_profile_packet`) and the post-workout packet (`_athlete_profile_packet`) whenever a reading exists in the window; the static `_profile_content()` seed value stays untouched and serves only as the fallback for Mark's existing prod `profile` row when no live reading is on file (documented in-line, not dropped, matching Batch 176's `ftpWatts` precedent and avoiding a wholesale-replace PUT against prod state). A new `profile.vo2maxAsOfDate` sibling field states which day the effective figure is from. **177.2 prompt guidance:** both prompts (`morning-analysis-v24-2026-07-29`, `post-workout-analysis-v14-2026-07-29`) state the value is live and dated, permit noting a real difference from a previously-stated figure, and explicitly forbid inventing a trend from memory or a single reading — no new trend-series plumbing was added to either packet; the existing `trends.py` VO2max series remains the mechanism for that surface. **177.3 verdict boundary:** `_age_comparison`/`_morning_verdict` are untouched — VO2max was already read live (not hardcoded) for the age-norm and never touches the Green/Amber/Red ladder; only the packet's `athleteProfile` mirror was stale. **Boundaries:** backend-only; no migration (packet overlay, no new column); no shared-schema/endpoint/frontend/provider/auth/hosting/verdict change. Local gates pass: backend pytest 722 passed / 300 expected PostgreSQL skips (new resolver + packet-overlay tests included; DB-backed cases run in CI); Ruff clean; mypy clean across 125 source files. Tier 🟢 Mid.

### 2026-07-30 — Batch 178 — Chat answers from live app state, and stops narrating its own plumbing

258. **Batch 178 assembles the coach conversation's context when the question is asked, widens it to what the app has already computed, and stops the app's internal vocabulary reaching Mark.** Implemented on `feat/batch-178-chat-live-context` after the explicit `/batch-start 178` request, closing Mark's 2026-07-30 report that the conversations feel *almost disconnected* from the app — a structural gap, not a wording defect: brief chat's entire world was one stored `context_packet` plus the read's markdown, so a ride completed after the morning brief was invisible and any question whose answer is rendered one tab away got an honest refusal. **178.1 register, not honesty:** `brief_chat.SYSTEM_PROMPT` said "packet" eight times and instructed the model to say plainly when the packet could not answer, which is how "that isn't in the packet" reached him. The prompt (`brief-chat-v5-2026-07-30`) now describes what the coach has in front of him, gives the genuinely-unknown case a coach's sentence, and names the internal nouns exactly once — inside `NO_PLUMBING_RULE`, the single sentence that forbids them; the raw `analysis_type` no longer travels as "Read type: post_workout" either (`_READ_LABELS`). The grounding requirement, the Batch 175 labelled general-science lane, Batch 165's read-type capability wording and every read floor are unchanged. A tuple of banned nouns plus `internal_vocabulary_hits()` makes the contract checkable over the instruction text, over every string the module can put in front of Mark, and over a stored answer; there is deliberately **no post-hoc rewrite of a coach answer** — rewriting model output would mangle sentences, so the prompt is the lever and the detector is the test. **178.2 kickoff decision — layer, do not rebuild:** the stored packet stays the read's own record (its markdown was written *from* that packet, so rebuilding at ask-time would let the chat contradict the text it is explaining, and would re-run the whole morning assembly per question). New `services/chat_context.py` layers an `appState` block alongside it, labelled as true-now, carrying a `sinceThisRead` delta from `analysis.generated_at_utc` — activities, check-ins, plan-action audits, newer reads, and subject-date workouts closed since. Staleness reuses Batch 159's `manual_entry_input_version` compared against the check-in version the packet itself recorded, via one `_packet_check_in_versions` reader that handles both the morning `manualEntries` list and the single post-session check-in node — one notion of "current", not a second. **178.3 kickoff decision — deterministic pre-assembly, not tool-use:** `generate_anthropic_text` has no tool support, so retrieval would mean extending the client and paying a round-trip per lookup; the existing builders are deterministic, cheap and unit-tested, so the block is pre-assembled from them — `TrainingWeekService.build_window` (new windowed entry point; `build()` delegates, and `_day_status` now takes an explicit subject-date anchor so a day still *ahead* of Mark reads `planned`, never `no_activity_recorded`), `TrendsService.windows` (new public loader so the series and the year-on-year comparison come from one sample load) with `window_json`/`compute_year_on_year`, plus today's plan, recent activities and sleep history. **Latest review = the conclusions Mark was already given:** the stored `weekly_review`/`monthly_review` rows are read rather than re-running `ReviewService.preview`, which would recompute a rollup, strength brief and insights on every question to reproduce a narrative the app has already shown him. **Token budget:** `APP_STATE_CHAR_BUDGET = 30_000` (~7.5k tokens) against a measured ~22k-character full block, so trimming is a safety valve rather than routine; sections drop in a fixed order (recent sessions → review prose → sleep history) then the trend series oldest-first, while the week ahead, today and the since-read delta are never dropped, and everything dropped is *named* in `omittedForLength` because an omission that read as an absence would recreate the exact defect this batch removes. **Proposal gate untouched:** `_analysis_allows_adjustment_proposal` still keys on `analysis_type == "morning"` (re-keying it to live plan state is Batch 179.3's job); ask-time state is used only to *retire* an affordance the frozen packet would still offer for a ride Mark has since completed or skipped — it can never add one. **Boundaries:** backend-only; no migration (context assembly, not storage), no shared-schema, endpoint, frontend, provider, auth or hosting change; explanatory-only, so the deterministic Green/Amber/Red ladder, Red-never-VO2 and every floor are untouched and plan changes stay propose/confirm (#29). Local gates pass: backend pytest 733 passed / 310 expected PostgreSQL skips (the new DB-backed assembly tests run in CI; the pure budget/truncation, check-in-version, forward-window and prompt-policy tests run locally, and prompt assertions are whitespace-normalized after Batch 175's newline-sensitive CI failure); Ruff clean; mypy clean across 126 source files. **Branch CI passed all seven jobs on `11b69c8` (1043 pytest passed against Postgres, including all ten new `test_chat_context.py` cases).** The first push (`4b8ca71`) failed the pytest job alone on a test-fixture bug the no-Postgres local run cannot surface: two rows on one `workout_date` need incrementing versions, because `plan_import` writes a split day that way and `(user_id, workout_date, version)` is unique. Fixed in `11b69c8`, which also settles the ordering that exposed: same-day rows are *siblings* rather than revisions of each other, so `_planned_workouts_on` orders by ascending version — the order Mark's plan reads in — and the packet lists a split day cycle-then-strength. Tier 🔴 High. **Shipped via PR #207 / squash `5d5abee`:** PR-context and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs. **Deploy note — auto-deploy is not guaranteed:** a Railway platform incident (2026-07-30 16:32 UTC, "builds and deployments are delayed") paused Hobby-plan deployments and the merge webhook was *dropped, not queued* — Railway kept serving the pre-merge SHA after the incident cleared, with no failed deployment to signal it. `railway redeploy --service api --from-source --yes` deploys `main` HEAD from the configured GitHub source (as distinct from `railway up`, which uploads the local tree) and is the right recovery: it ships exactly what auto-deploy would have shipped. Worth checking the served SHA at every closeout rather than assuming the push deployed. Production verified on `5d5abee`: Railway direct + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both returned the SHA, `/api/v1/health/ready` reported `db: ok`, web `/` 200, and the Batch 178 surface itself (`POST /api/v1/briefs/{id}/messages`) plus daily-loop returned 401 direct and proxied.

### 2026-07-31 — Batch 179 — One coach conversation, reachable from anywhere

259. **Batch 179 makes the coach one rolling conversation Mark can open from any surface, with the document a question came from demoted from a fence to a context seed.** Implemented on `feat/batch-179-one-coach-conversation` after the explicit `/batch-start 179` request, closing the structural half of his 2026-07-30 "almost disconnected" report that Batch 178 (#258) could not: the schema itself asserted that a conversation cannot exist without a document — `BriefMessage.analysis_id` was `NOT NULL` with an FK — which is why there was no way to *just ask the coach something*, and why Sleep (which borrows `morningAnalysis` and has no `Analysis` of its own) and the breathwork/strength/walking briefs (not `analyses` rows at all) could not host a chat without inventing a row purely as an anchor. **179.1 kickoff decision — one rolling thread with a nullable anchor, not a `conversations`/`conversation_messages` pair.** The rows already *were* the thread — ordered, user-scoped, RLS-enabled — so migration `026` simply drops the `NOT NULL`, adds `origin_kind`/`origin_date` for the surfaces with no read, and adds `ix_brief_messages_user_created` for the new per-user read path. That buys the continuity Mark asked for without a second table, a backfill, or a rewrite of the learning-source queries, and existing per-read history stays readable **in place** because filtering by `analysis_id` still selects exactly the rows it always did. RLS needed no new work (019 already enabled it; altering a column does not change that). The `downgrade` deletes unanchored rows before restoring `NOT NULL` — they are conversation turns that never had a document, so that is the only honest reversal. **179.2 the regression this batch could most easily have caused:** `conversation_learning` inner-joined `BriefMessage` to `Analysis` in both its source query and its single-record evidence lookup, so a nullable anchor without touching them would have dropped every unanchored message out of Batch 151's learning pipeline **with no error at all** — the conversations Mark starts by just opening the coach would silently stop teaching it anything. Both are now outer joins; `_chat_source_date` falls back to the origin date, then the message date, when there is no read to take a subject date from; and a regression test asserts an unanchored message is both a learning candidate and still re-verifiable evidence. The single-record lookup also moved `Analysis.user_id == user_id` into the join condition, so a foreign analysis yields a null anchor rather than hiding the user's own message. **179.3 the propose gate is keyed on the plan, not the paperwork.** `_analysis_allows_adjustment_proposal`'s `analysis_type == "morning"` test was a proxy for "there is a live adjustable ride", and the candidate was read out of a frozen packet; `ChatContext.adjustable_workout_id` now answers that question directly from live plan rows (active, not completed/skipped, has a structured workout, is a bike type, and — checked against the window rather than inferred from statuses, mirroring `morning_analysis._rest_day_context` — not inside a holiday). **Kickoff decision on the acceptance criteria's ambiguous "never on a retrospective read":** the gate is *purely* today's live plan state. The retrospective case is then handled by the plan itself — a completed or skipped ride yields no affordance, which is the realistic post-session shape — while blocking a genuine "make today easier" merely because it was asked from yesterday's read would recreate the document-fencing this batch exists to remove. The deterministic keyword check on **Mark's own question** is unchanged, the model still triggers nothing, and the propose→approve→push gate (#29) is untouched. This supersedes Batch 178's subject-date liveness set, which could only ever *retire* an affordance the packet had already offered, so `ChatContext.live_workout_ids`/`workout_is_live` were removed rather than left as a second, wrong answer to the same question. **179.4 one entry point, context seeded by origin.** New `CoachLauncher` (a persistent button plus the existing bottom sheet) is mounted in `Layout`, so the coach is reachable from every authenticated page including the ones with no analysis row; `originForPath` maps the route to a **controlled vocabulary** of origin kinds, and `normalize_origin_kind` degrades an unrecognised value to `general` so the client can never put free text into the prompt (Decision #243's "Mark's words are data" applied to the app's own controls). **Kickoff decision — the inline read chats stay.** `BriefFollowUpChat` keeps its three mounts and keeps listing only that read's turns via the unchanged `/api/v1/briefs/{id}/messages`, because a brief should show its own exchange rather than everything Mark has ever asked; both surfaces now render one extracted `CoachConversation` and write into the same thread, so they are two views, not two chats. New `GET|POST /api/v1/coach/messages` serves the rolling thread. **The caps moved with the model:** a per-document cap made no sense once the document stopped bounding the conversation — Mark could have reset it by opening a different read — so `MAX_USER_TURNS_PER_DAY = 20` bounds the paid calls over his local day (the same order of spend two per-read chats already allowed, spent on one conversation that continues), and `MAX_HISTORY_TURNS_IN_PROMPT` is now the tail of the *thread*. `ask()` takes an injectable `now`, because a gate and a cap both anchored to "today" cannot be tested by a suite that has no way to name today. **179.5 kickoff decision — one policy surface as a registry, not a rewrite of five prompts.** New `services/coach_policy.py` holds the floors, the grounding/no-plumbing rules, the Batch 175 science lane, the propose/confirm rule and the anti-sycophancy rule; the conversation composes its prompt from them, so all of it is identical from the morning brief, the Week sheet, Sleep, or a cold open — asserted directly by a test that assembles the prompt from three different entry points and checks every floor sentence. The deterministic read prompts are **audited** against the same registry (`missing_floors`, `READ_PROMPT_FLOORS`) rather than rewritten to import from it: they are not interchangeable — the morning read states Red-never-VO2 and the power-balance floor because it prescribes a ride, while the walk/strength/mobility reads state neither because neither applies to a session with no power meter — so making them recite inapplicable rules would bump every prompt version and change narrative behaviour on the deterministic reads inside a batch about the conversation surface. The audit still fails CI if a floor a read *does* state is later dropped. Prompt → `coach-chat-v6-2026-07-31`. **Boundaries:** one migration (`026`, anchor optionality); shared-schema additive (`analysisId` nullable, `originKind`/`originDate`, `coachMessageInputSchema`); one new router; frontend surface change; no provider, auth or hosting change; explanatory-only, so the deterministic Green/Amber/Red ladder, Red-never-VO2 and every floor are untouched and plan changes stay propose/confirm (#29). Local gates pass: backend pytest 738 passed / 321 expected PostgreSQL skips (the pure policy, floor-audit, origin-normalisation and budget tests run locally; the DB-backed propose-gate, holiday-guard, unanchored-assembly, coach-endpoint and learning outer-join cases run in CI); Ruff clean; mypy clean across 128 source files; web 380 tests / 62 files, `tsc --noEmit` clean, ESLint 0 errors. **Branch CI passed all seven jobs on `baed03b` (1059 pytest passed against Postgres).** The first push (`a9ad36f`) failed the pytest job alone on the *same fixture class that failed Batch 178's first push*: two `PlannedWorkout` rows on one `workout_date` are siblings rather than revisions, so they need ascending versions under the `(user_id, workout_date, version)` unique constraint. Fixed in `baed03b` by making `version` an explicit fixture parameter. Worth carrying forward — a no-Postgres local run cannot surface it, so this failure mode recurs once per batch that fixtures a split day. Tier 🔴 High. **Shipped via PR #208 / squash `94f10b8`:** PR-context and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs. Production verified on `94f10b8`: Railway direct + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both returned the SHA, `/api/v1/health/ready` reported `db: ok`, web `/` 200, and the new `GET|POST /api/v1/coach/messages` plus the unchanged per-read `briefs/{id}/messages` and daily-loop all returned 401 direct and proxied. **The migration itself was verified live rather than assumed** (read-only `railway run`): alembic head `026`, `brief_messages.analysis_id` nullable, `origin_kind`/`origin_date` and `ix_brief_messages_user_created` present, and all 64 pre-existing messages preserved — the check that matters for a migration whose downgrade deletes rows. Railway auto-deploy fired normally this time, but the served SHA was still checked first, per #258's dropped-webhook lesson.

### 2026-08-02 — Batch 180 — Daily rows finish the day, not the morning

260. **Batch 180 treats Garmin stress and Body Battery as closed-day aggregates only when their own raw local-time windows prove that the day is closed, and it continuously re-reads a short rolling window rather than trusting the waking snapshot forever.** Implemented on `feat/batch-180-complete-daily-metrics` after `/batch-start 180`, closing Mark's 2026-07-30/08-01 report that the brief's “all-day” stress disagreed with Garmin. **Kickoff sync decision:** the morning daily sync reads today first, then D-1…D-3; every date independently uses the existing three-attempt exponential `_retry_sync` path and failure isolation. Three closed days, rather than D-1 alone, self-heal up to two missed mornings; no evening pass is added because the consuming brief needs yesterday only after it has closed, and a second scheduler path would add calls without buying correctness. This is roughly 30 extra Garmin endpoint calls per profile each morning inside the existing 429 posture, acceptable for this private 1–2-user app. The existing `(user_id, calendar_date)` upsert corrects rows in place; no migration or endpoint. **Kickoff completeness decision:** derive coverage at read time from `raw_payload`, not a persisted column. `services/daily_metric_coverage.py` follows the same source preference as the parser (dedicated stress/Body Battery payload, then daily-stats fallback), requires the source to start by local midnight and end within the final five-minute sample interval of the following midnight, and reports complete/incomplete/unknown. The five-minute tolerance is evidence-based: a read-only production audit found one genuine historical closed day ending 23:55; every defective live row ends hours early. `yesterdayLoad.wholeDayCost` now nulls each figure unless its source is complete and carries explicit coverage metadata; morning prompt v25 forbids reconstructing or narrating missing partials as completed totals. The chronic `daytime_stress_avg` input uses the same stress guard. Driver-cache version `insights:v2-2026-08-02` makes older cached correlations fail open to a live recomputation and permits the next morning to store a corrected version alongside the dated old audit artifact, so repaired source rows are not hidden by v1 cache state. **Kickoff repair decision:** the production boundary was verified read-only with this exact predicate, not assumed from the approximate planning note: 2026-06-20 is the last complete row, and the 42 closed rows from **2026-06-21 through 2026-08-01 are one contiguous incomplete window**. Closeout must deploy the guard first, dry-run and then run `garmin_history_backfill --start 2026-06-21 --end <day-before-repair> --no-skip-existing --no-activities --require-complete-daily`, and rebuild the `db_history` metric baselines because Body Battery charge participates there; chronic suggestions/driver correlations then regenerate through the versioned live/cache path. The repair runner refuses to write a day whose fetched aggregate sources are not complete. **Regression boundary:** fixtures reproduce 31 July's stored waking snapshot 12 stress / 1 drained / 92 end and prove a completed re-sync overwrites that one row with 28 / 70 / 16; partial packets omit the totals, a completed packet exposes them, incomplete stress is absent from driver records, and the deterministic verdict is byte-for-byte identical across the two DailyMetric variants. Green/Amber/Red consumes none of these fields and is untouched. Backend-only; no migration/shared-schema/frontend/provider/auth/hosting/verdict change. Local gates: backend pytest 745 passed / 325 expected Postgres skips, Ruff/format clean (216 files), mypy clean (129 source files). Tier 🔴 High. **Closeout:** shipped via PR #209, squash `70443e7`; branch, PR-context, and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs. Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health served the exact SHA, DB readiness was `ok`, web `/` returned 200, and protected daily-loop returned 401 direct/proxied. The guarded dry-run and committed repair each completed 42/42 dates with zero failures and no activity writes; all 404 closed rows now prove complete. Garmin's live historical 31 July response now reports 28 stress / 70 drained / 24 end in both dedicated and stats sources over a complete window, so production retains that current upstream truth while the original 16 remains the locked regression fixture. All eight `db_history` baselines were rebuilt and a follow-up dry-run found them unchanged.

### 2026-08-02 — Batch 181 — Recorded data is not ground truth

261. **Batch 181 makes observed-data humility a shared CheckMark floor while preserving anti-sycophancy on coaching judgement.** Implemented on `fix/batch-181-coach-data-honesty` after `/batch-start 181`, closing Mark's report that the coach defended the bad stored stress figure as “correct data.” **The boundary is observed data vs coaching decisions:** every figure is described as what the app recorded, not independently verified truth about Mark; when he says his own device displayed a different value, the coach acknowledges the discrepancy, treats his reading as the better evidence for what the device showed, and calls the mismatch a data-quality problem. That deference cannot change a deterministic verdict, Red-never-VO2, any safety floor, recorded plan/completion state, or the propose/confirm rail. This narrowly supersedes Decision #250's over-broad “packet measurement always wins” wording for explicit own-device observations; it does not let transient corrections rewrite stored metrics or turn confirmed learned memory into a verdict lever. **One policy surface:** `coach_policy.FLOORS` owns the canonical rule, the rolling conversation composes it from every origin, and `READ_PROMPT_FLOORS` now audits the same universal floor across morning, ride, strength, mobility, walk, review, trend, and handover prompts. The chat's current-vs-read wording now names both as app records rather than physical truth. Prompt versions advance to chat v7, morning v26, ride v15, strength/mobility/walk v5, reviews v5, trends v4, and handover v2 so pre-policy narratives are not treated as current. **Kickoff storage decision:** the existing conversation rows retain the dispute, but this batch adds no separate data-quality flag/admin record; the learning store is for durable personal context, while an operational metric discrepancy needs a future lifecycle/review design rather than being smuggled into it. **Regression boundary:** a 12-vs-28 Garmin dispute is acknowledged without internal vocabulary or a claim that 12 is correct, while the same answer holds a Red verdict and rejects VO2; the labelled `General principle:` lane, Mark-specific no-invention rule, no-plumbing rule, anti-sycophancy, and propose/confirm floor remain intact. Backend prompt-policy/tests/docs only; no migration, shared schema, endpoint, frontend, provider, auth, hosting, plan, delivery, or verdict change. Local gates: 746 passed / 326 expected PostgreSQL skips; Ruff/format clean (216 files); mypy clean (129 source files). Tier 🔴 High. **Shipped via PR #210 / squash `65f7e87`:** branch-push, PR-context, and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs; production Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health served the exact SHA, database readiness was `ok`, web `/` returned 200, and protected coach POST returned 401 direct and proxied.

### 2026-08-04 — Egress incident — Nightly backup repair and time-series write churn

262. **The in-container `pg_dump` is the only backup this project has on the free plan, so it is repaired and narrowed rather than retired; and the hourly activity poll stops restating a sample stream that has already settled.** Unplanned incident work on `fix/backup-and-timeseries-churn` — not a batch, so `docs/phase-batches.md` gains no row. **Trigger:** Supabase reported the org over its 5.5 GB egress quota (15.19 GB used, requests dropped until 21 Aug). Production was checked first and was still serving — `/api/v1/health/ready` returned `db: ok` and web `/` 200 — because the restriction lands on the platform APIs, not the direct pooler connection, so this was a cost incident and not an outage. **Diagnosis** came from `pg_stat_statements`, with the two obvious alternatives ruled out rather than assumed: no replication slots existed (so nothing was streaming WAL out) and PostgREST had no traffic. **Cause A — the backup was dumping everything, then silently stopped working.** The nightly 03:00 job had dumped the *entire* database 36 nights running (2026-06-23 → 07-28): every schema, including the co-tenant movie app's `public` tables (2.5M `movie_credits` rows a night) and the 300 MB `coach.activity_timeseries`. Batch 164.1's supply-chain pin then moved the base image from a floating `python:3.12-slim` tag — which had drifted to trixie, whose `postgresql-client` is 17 — back to `slim-bookworm`, whose client is **15**. `pg_dump` refuses to dump a newer server, so the job has failed every night since 2026-07-29 (five `backup_failed` audit rows) and nothing alerted. The quota was therefore spent producing backups that no longer existed. **Decision — keep the in-container backup.** Batch 164.2 explicitly left open "whether the in-container backup is still useful beside Supabase managed backups"; it is, because managed backups and point-in-time restore are **paid-plan features** and this org is on the free plan, so these dumps are the only backup that exists. It is repaired by installing `postgresql-client-17` from PGDG rather than by unpinning the image, so 164.1's digest pin survives intact. **Decision — exclude `coach.activity_timeseries` row data, and only that.** It is ~85% of the database and the one table replayable from an upstream source (`garmin_history_backfill` refetches it from Garmin); the table definition still restores, empty. Everything authored — analyses, check-ins, plans, profile, chat — has no upstream and stays in, guarded by a test asserting the exclusion list never widens beyond that single table. The archive becomes compressed custom format (`.dump`, restored with `pg_restore`), since compression happens server-side and it is the compressed bytes that bill. **Cause B — the poll rewrote settled data.** The activity poll re-reads a four-day window hourly and deleted and re-inserted every sample for every activity on each pass: ~96x write amplification on the largest table (9.6M inserts and 8.5M deletes against 587k live rows, and the 30–50 MB-per-five-minute WAL churn visible in the checkpoint log). **Decision — the staleness test pairs row count with the latest sample timestamp, never count alone.** Details are fetched with `maxchart=2000`, so a long ride polled mid-session and again once finished returns *exactly* 2000 points both times; a count-only guard would call those equal and freeze the partial stream in place, which is precisely the in-progress case the re-import exists to serve. The latest sample timestamp always advances when a session progresses. **Latent defect fixed in passing:** the delete ran *before* the parse, so a thin or partial details payload wiped stored samples and replaced them with nothing; an empty parse is now read as "this payload carried no samples", not as a deletion. **Regression boundary:** a repeat poll leaves the stored rows' primary keys untouched (identity, not merely row count — a delete-and-reinsert would mint fresh ids at the same count); a mid-session ride and its finished form at equal sample count still replaces; a sample-free payload does not delete; the dump argv carries `--format=custom` and the exclusion while keeping the password out of argv (P3-6). Backend + Docker + docs only; no migration, shared schema, endpoint, frontend, provider, auth, hosting, plan, delivery or verdict change. Local gates: 749 passed / 329 expected PostgreSQL skips; Ruff/format clean (216 files); mypy clean (129 source files). **Shipped via PR #211 / squash `18d3af5`.** **Closeout deviation, recorded deliberately:** branch and PR-context CI passed six of seven jobs, `Unit tests (pytest)` green against PostgreSQL in both waves (the gate that actually exercises the three new time-series tests, which skip on a machine without Postgres). `Security audit (deps)` failed on `cryptography 49.0.0` / CVE-2026-69247. That failure is **pre-existing and repo-wide** — `main` pins the same version and last ran green on 2026-08-03, before the advisory published — so it is unrelated to this change and merging does not worsen exposure. The closeout guardrail "do not close out with failing CI" was overridden on explicit instruction rather than silently: the alternative was to hold a live egress fix and a six-night-broken backup behind a major dependency bump that touches the auth stack and `garth`, under a `--require-hashes` lock needing regenerated hashes. **That deviation was closed the same day** by PR #212 / squash `1eeba0a`: the lock was regenerated with the same recorded `uv` command plus `--upgrade-package cryptography`, moving exactly one package (49.0.0 → 50.0.0) with the header and every other pin untouched. The compatibility risk was low and is now evidenced rather than asserted — `cryptography` is transitive via the web-push stack (`pywebpush`/`py-vapid`/`http_ece`), not the Garmin or auth path; uv resolving the whole graph at 50.0.0 proves no dependent declares an upper bound; and CI's pytest job, which installs the *unpinned* `requirements-dev.txt`, had already been resolving to 50.0.0 and passing, so the lock bump aligned the hashed image pin with what was under test. All seven jobs are green on `main`, and the running container reports `cryptography 50.0.0` alongside `pg_dump 17.10`. One consequence worth knowing: from 50.0.0 the macOS wheels are **arm64-only**, so installing the lock on an Intel Mac falls back to a source build needing OpenSSL and Rust; Linux is unaffected (31 manylinux wheels, `cp39-abi3-manylinux_2_28_x86_64` covering Python 3.12 on both CI and the bookworm image). Recorded in `docs/runbooks/sync-and-analysis.md`. **Durability was closed the same day:** Railway volume `api-volume` (5 GB) is attached to service `api` at `/data/backups` with `BACKUP_DIR` set to match, replacing the ephemeral `/tmp` default that had been discarding every archive at each redeploy. Verified end-to-end in production rather than waiting for the 03:00 run — the app's own `create_backup` produced a 7.4 MB archive, `pg_restore --list` reads it with 27 `TABLE DATA` entries and none for `activity_timeseries` while retaining that table's definition, mode `0600` on a `0700` directory, and `railway volume files download` pulled it to a laptop with a matching SHA-256. **The same-platform objection is answered by that download command rather than by an upload pipeline:** an on-demand off-site copy costs no new vendor, credential or bucket, which matters because these archives carry Mark's full analysis/check-in/plan/chat history and the project has consistently paid to keep health data on controlled infrastructure. **Still open, and deliberately not done here:** nothing alerts on a failed backup. `backup_failed` writes an audit row and an `error` log only. `settings.admin_alert_user_id` does *not* cover it — that push is wired solely to brief-generation failure (Batch 141) and by design names a **separate operator profile**, because the app `admin` role belongs to the primary user and an ops alert must never land on his phone. Seeding such a profile is not a config-only change: the scheduler's job loops select every active profile and would attempt Garmin syncs and brief generation for an operator account. Real alerting therefore needs either an operator-profile concept the job loops exclude, or log-based alerting on the structured `scheduled backup failed` event, and that choice is left open rather than guessed at.

### 2026-08-04 — Batch 182 — A deload that understands why the Red happened

263. **Batch 182 reserves the seven-day deload for sustained marker evidence; a Red cluster is qualified as acute evidence and can only propose a week-preserving rearrangement.** Implemented on `feat/batch-182-deload-context` after `/batch-start 182`, superseding only the Red-cluster half of Decision #252. **Red qualification:** each latest-per-day Red inside the existing seven-day window is paired with that day's stored physiology and morning check-in. A deterministic vocabulary classifies explicit acute causes Mark already writes (`alcohol`, `illness`, `travel`, `deliberate_rest`, `training_load`); recognized text excludes that Red from cluster escalation. Without text, more than 24 hours on Garmin's recovery timer — the same settled boundary used by Batch 167 — excludes the Red only when measured HRV is healthy/not below its personal floor and RHR is at or below its personal ceiling. `acute_load` is carried as evidence but gets no invented standalone threshold. An unexplained Red or one with strained HRV/RHR counts. Exclusion, rather than fractional weighting, keeps the two-Red threshold inspectable. The daily verdict is neither recomputed nor softened. **Action split:** ≥2 qualifying Reds produce `kind="rearrange_proposal"`, never `deload_proposal`; the morning assembler reuses `plan_swap_first` within each Monday–Sunday training week covered by the seven-day horizon to offer the first valid hard-for-easy category swap without moving load between weeks, and Mark's existing Apply tap is the human confirmation before the plan/delivery reconcile. The original ≥70% sustained personal-marker miss over ≥10 samples in a ≥21-night window retains its trigger predicate, deload transform and persistent propose→approve→push/idempotency behavior. **Plan awareness:** an active planned workout linked to a recovery/taper/consolidation block anywhere inside the seven-day action horizon suppresses either structural action completely; no partial duplicate is useful when the 2121 plan already supplies the lighter structure. This makes the 1 August → PN2 W03 RECOVERY case a no-op on plan evidence alone. **Reason capture:** no new form or schema. `holiday_windows` already records holiday ranges and the daily manual entry is an upserted durable row Mark already uses; the deterministic cause classifier carries those sources as `recordedTrainingContext` in the stored packet so future low-training analysis can distinguish known holidays/check-in causes from unknown gaps. This respects Mark's correction that he already tells the app and avoids a duplicate prompt. **Wording/contract:** morning prompt → v27; the swap DTO gains optional hard-date/weekday and easier-workout provenance so a future-session proposal never says “today.” The rearrange contract is `restructure_preview_apply`; the sustained deload contract stays `propose_approve_push`; both declare human approval and `verdictImpact="none"`. **Boundaries:** backend-first plus the minimal additive shared/frontend swap wording; no migration, new endpoint, provider, auth, hosting, deterministic threshold, daily-verdict, safety-floor, or Red-never-VO2 change. Local gates pass: backend pytest 755 passed / 332 expected PostgreSQL skips; Ruff/format clean (216 files); mypy clean (129 source files); shared schemas 24 passed; web 380 passed with build/lint clean (six pre-existing lint warnings). Tier 🔴 High. **Shipped via PR #213 / squash `7819434`:** final branch, PR-context, and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs; production Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health served the exact merge SHA, database readiness was `ok`, web `/` returned 200, protected routes returned 401, and deployed classifier plus frontend-copy smokes passed.

### 2026-08-04 — Batch 184 — Tonight's nudge is about tonight

264. **Batch 184 makes the 20:00 sleep nudge a projection-backed message and makes silence an explicit part of the notification policy.** Implemented on `feat/batch-184-tonights-nudge` after `/batch-start 184`, completing the push half deliberately deferred by Batch 46 / Decision #116. **Kickoff service decision — a new leaf assembler, not more router logic:** `SleepProjectionContextService` owns the activity→local training-signal conversion, cached measured-driver lookup, sleep-protocol/weather/temperature assembly and the call to pure `project_sleep`. Its snapshot path reuses rows `DailyLoopService` already loaded, while its scheduler path loads only the four focused source sets; both use `InsightsService.cached_drivers`, including its existing identical live fallback when today's cache is absent. The 45-minute `environment_freshness` rule is applied inside this shared boundary: a stale/future Hive reading becomes `None`, so neither the app nor push can call it a current warm-room driver, while training and forecast evidence remain available. The daily-loop payload — and therefore Home and Sleep — now consumes this same build rather than a router-local reconstruction. **Kickoff quiet decision:** personalized `watch` and `protect` always reach the unchanged `_send_once` path; personalized `routine` sends nothing; an unpersonalized `fallback` keeps the fixed protocol but sends at most once per local Monday–Sunday week. The weekly check excludes the current date so same-day retries still prove idempotent through the unchanged `sleep-protocol-{date}` tag, and legacy static `rule='sleep_protocol'` audits count so a mid-week deploy cannot repeat the generic copy. The `notification-rules` audit version advances to v2. **Message contract:** the projection headline is the push title, its first two prep actions are the body, and `routine`/`watch`/`protect` map to `info`/`warning`/`critical`; the stored audit carries status, tone, summary, evidence and actions. `project_sleep` now makes risk headlines name the facts that earned them — late/hard/big training, a fresh warm bedroom or a warm overnight low — so a protective push cannot be generic protocol copy wearing a critical severity. A warm forecast is only named as risk when Mark's measured weather/bedroom drivers support that link. The existing 20:00–20:20 profile-local window, holiday-overnight skip, VAPID/quiet-hours behavior and per-day tag are unchanged. **Boundaries:** deterministic advisory notification only; no migration, shared schema, endpoint, frontend, provider, auth, hosting, fan threshold, Green/Amber/Red, safety-floor or plan/delivery change. Local gates pass: backend pytest 762 passed / 334 expected PostgreSQL skips; focused projection/nudge/scheduler/daily-loop suites 60 passed / 29 expected skips; Ruff/format clean (218 files); mypy clean (130 source files). Branch CI run `30933007735` passed all seven jobs on implementation SHA `3e99449`, including the two new PostgreSQL-backed tests. **Closeout:** PR #215 passed all seven PR-context jobs plus Vercel preview, squash-merged as `107b5c4`, and the exact post-merge `main` CI run `30936365488` passed all seven jobs. Production served `107b5c4` through Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health, readiness reported `db: ok`, web `/` returned 200, protected daily-loop returned 401 direct and proxied, and a read-only Railway module smoke confirmed the deployed 20:00 time, severity map, shared freshness context and weekly fallback cap. Tier 🔴 High.

### 2026-08-04 — Batch 185 — The weekly review arrives

265. **Batch 185 makes the weekly review a Sunday coach-initiated conversation, with one source of wording and one visible unread state.** Implemented on `feat/batch-185-weekly-review-delivery` after `/batch-start 185`. **Cadence and scope:** the weekly job runs Sunday at 18:00 in the configured profile-local/London timezone, so `resolve_period_window` selects the Monday–Sunday week ending that day rather than Monday's new empty week; it is registered in APScheduler and as `python -m src.run_scheduled weekly-review`. Monthly stays manual — adding a month-end message would create a second unsolicited cadence before the weekly one has earned attention — and an active holiday covering Sunday skips generation, thread delivery and push. **One review, one message, one push:** review prompt v6 starts with an exact `Bottom line` sentence naming the period's most important conclusion, then keeps the existing Trends/Wins/Concerns/Recommendations sections. `WeeklyReviewDeliveryService` writes the full review once as an assistant `BriefMessage`, anchored by `analysis_id` to the stored `weekly_review`, with controlled origin `weekly_review` and the period end as `origin_date`; there is no second summary/model call. `review_bottom_line` feeds the same authored conclusion to a new `weekly_review_push` plan, deduped by `weekly-review-{period_start}` through the existing `_send_once` audit rail and deep-linked to `/?coach=open`; generic “your review is ready” copy is structurally absent. A stable per-user/period PostgreSQL advisory lock in `ReviewService.run` serializes the in-process/external-cron overlap before the existing-row check, so concurrent unattended runs cannot both generate. Sequential retries reuse the review, assistant row and push audit independently, allowing a partial delivery to finish without another Anthropic call. **Failure behavior:** an exception rolls back the review transaction, records one non-pushed assistant turn linking to Reviews for retry, and reuses Batch 141's classified structured log/operator-alert path (now artifact-labelled); no empty-content failure push is sent. **Unread and reply:** `CoachLauncher` now loads the recent thread while closed. A versioned client-side last-seen assistant id, keyed by profile, shows an accessible dot only for an unseen `weekly_review` turn; opening the sheet records it seen. The push query parameter opens the sheet directly. If the latest turn is that review, the next POST carries its `analysisId` and controlled origin, so Mark's user reply and the coach response remain anchored to the review; user replies still enter Batch 151 through the existing outer-join learning path. This deliberately avoids a persisted read-marker migration for a private 1–2-user PWA. **Boundaries:** no migration, new endpoint, provider, auth, hosting, deterministic Green/Amber/Red, safety-floor, or propose/confirm change; the shared origin enum addition is backward-compatible. Local gates: backend pytest 767 passed / 336 expected PostgreSQL skips; Ruff/format clean (220 files); mypy clean (131 source files); shared 24 tests/typecheck; web 382 tests, production build/typecheck and zero-error lint (six pre-existing Fast Refresh warnings). Tier 🔴 High.

### 2026-08-05 — Batch 185 closeout — DST-safe Railway weekly cron

266. **The external weekly-review runner uses two UTC candidates plus a Europe/London hour gate; the API scheduler remains enabled.** Railway cron is UTC-only, while Decision #265 promises Sunday 18:00 in Mark's local timezone across BST and GMT. Production service `weekly-review` therefore uses cron `0 17,18 * * 0`; its start command checks `TZ=Europe/London date +%H` and invokes `python -m src.run_scheduled weekly-review` only at local hour 18, with the other candidate exiting cleanly. This is preferable to a fixed UTC hour that drifts seasonally or a twice-yearly manual edit. The service follows `main`, builds the repo-root Dockerfile, has no public domain, uses `restartPolicyType=NEVER`, and references only the API service variables needed for database access, Anthropic generation, production config validation and VAPID push. The shared root `railway.toml` leaves `/api/v1/health` in the resolved deployment manifest; that web-cutover metadata is not treated as cron verification — the scheduled service status and each run's exit/logs are authoritative. `SCHEDULER_ENABLED` is deliberately not disabled on `api`: only this one job has an external service, so APScheduler remains the fallback for it and the primary scheduler for every other job. Decision #265's advisory lock and three independent idempotency rails make overlap safe.

### 2026-08-05 — Batch 186 — The week ahead, folded into Sunday review

267. **Batch 186 folds forward-week guidance into the scheduled weekly review rather than creating a new analysis type or second push.** Implemented on `feat/batch-186-week-ahead-guidance` after `/batch-start 186`. **Delivery decision:** use the Batch 185 Sunday coach message as the single proactive surface for both halves of the horizon — the week just ended and the week about to start — because a separate "week ahead" notification would fragment the exact channel Batch 184-185 are trying to make worth reading. Monthly reviews stay manual/retrospective and receive `weekAhead: null`. **Packet decision:** add a small `WeekAheadService` leaf rather than duplicating schedule logic inside `reviews.py`. It composes the existing forward `TrainingWeekService.build_window`, active `PlanBlock` rows, and pure `summarize_weekly_mix` over the coming Monday-Sunday week, then names the quality sessions, hardest planned session and previous protecting night. The weekly mix target remains derived from that week's own rows, so a recovery/taper/consolidation week with `target=0` quality buckets is explicitly deliberate structure, not a shortfall. **Boundary:** review prompt v7 can narrate what the coming week asks, but the packet declares `classificationImpact="none"` and `proposalPolicy="explanatory_only_no_plan_change"`; it cannot move Green/Amber/Red, approve, skip, move, or directly propose a workout. Any actual plan change remains on Decision #29's propose/confirm rail. **Boundaries:** backend-only; no migration, shared schema break, new endpoint, frontend, provider, auth, hosting, scheduler, or push-idempotency change. Local gates pass: backend pytest 767 passed / 338 expected PostgreSQL skips; Ruff clean; mypy clean across 132 source files. Tier 🟢 Mid.

### 2026-08-05 — Batch 187 — The coach speaks when something changes

268. **Batch 187 delivers one quiet coach-thread turn for a meaningful computed-state transition, not for a standing level.** Implemented on `feat/batch-187-state-change-coach` after `/batch-start 187`. **Kickoff state decision:** no migration. Previous state is derived from existing evaluation/audit rows: previous morning packets for chronic action and weekly-mix risk, and previous `experiment_evaluation` rows for experiment outcomes. The new `state_change_coach` analysis row is an audit of the coach turn itself, not the source of truth for whether the underlying signal changed. **Kickoff budget/ranking decision:** the hard budget is one unprompted state-change coach turn per profile-local seven-day window; if several transitions are eligible at once, chronic action wins over experiment outcome, which wins over weekly-mix risk; suppressed lower-ranked transitions are discarded rather than queued because a delayed “something changed” message gets stale. **Delivery decision:** write an assistant `BriefMessage` with controlled origin `state_change`, anchored to the `state_change_coach` analysis, and do not add a new push type. Batch 185's unseen coach launcher is the visibility rail, keeping proactivity in the one replyable conversation without increasing notification volume. **Safety boundary:** chronic-action messaging reuses Batch 182's `ChronicActionSignal`; signals suppressed by a recovery/taper/consolidation plan block or excluded by Red qualification produce no message. Weekly-mix messages are only heads-up notes while the week is still fixable, and experiment outcomes never auto-conclude the experiment. Every stored turn declares `verdictImpact="none"` and `planMutation="none"`; deterministic Green/Amber/Red, safety floors, Red-never-VO2, and Decision #29's propose/confirm rail are untouched. Backend-only; no migration, shared-schema, endpoint, frontend, provider, auth, hosting, verdict, or plan-delivery change. Full local backend gate passes: 770 passed / 339 expected skips; Ruff clean; mypy clean across 133 source files. Tier 🟢 Mid.

### 2026-08-05 — Batch 188 — Coach-conversation & prompt-policy integrity review

269. **Batch 188 is a diagnose-only review of the coach's *spoken* layer whose durable artifact lives at `docs/reviews/BATCH_188_COACH_CONVERSATION_REVIEW.md`; the review changes no product code, prompt text, prompt version, floor, verdict logic or data, and accesses no production data (that belongs to Batches 190/191).** Run on `chore/batch-188-coach-conversation-review` after `/batch-start 188`, the first lane of the 2026-08-05 five-lane wave-2 audit. **Kickoff disposition decision:** map the surface by *what actually calls a model*, not by what carries a `PROMPT_VERSION`. There are **17** version constants (16 `PROMPT_VERSION` plus `wake_detection.WAKE_CHECK_PROMPT_VERSION`) but only **10** modules call Anthropic, so eight constants name deterministic template/rule revisions rather than prompts. **The lane's lead question is answered and the hypothesis inverts:** `state_change_coach.py` is not the least-covered *prompt* surface because it is not a prompt surface — it makes no model call, sets `model_name=None`/`raw_response={}`, and emits four hand-written templates, so it cannot hallucinate and being outside `READ_PROMPT_FLOORS` is correct (though undocumented). The genuinely uncovered **LLM** surface is `conversation_learning.py`: the only Anthropic-calling module with no version constant, outside the audit, and the only one whose output enters persistent coach memory — though its five-layer confirm-gated design is otherwise strong, so the gap is versioning, not floors. **The flagged contradiction is not real:** Batch 181's own-device precedence is bounded inside the floor sentence itself, restated identically in all eight read prompts as "This applies to observed data only", and explicitly disambiguated by `ANTI_SYCOPHANCY_RULE`; the resolving code path is that the verdict is computed before any model call and passed in as data. **Hard safety boundary confirmed intact:** no spoken surface can move Green/Amber/Red, a safety floor, Red-never-VO2 or plan state; the propose affordance is a server-side decision over live plan rows; Batch 182's Red qualification and recovery-block suppression are inherited in code, not copy. The report ranks **2 High / 8 Medium / 7 Low** findings `CC188-01…20` with `file:line` evidence and remediation stubs, severe-first: the seven-day unprompted-speech budget is spent first-come rather than best-first, so a weekly-mix heads-up can silence a chronic deload transition permanently; the state-change coach recomputes its "current" state with different parameters from the morning brief Mark read that day (weekly mix forced to `verdict_status="Green"`, chronic without `current_verdict`/`sleep_protocol`) and compares it against *yesterday's* packet, so it can contradict this morning's read; `sinceThisRead` reports pre-read workout closures as changes; the floor audit matches topic adjacency rather than the rule (an inverted "on a Red day VO2 is fine" passes `missing_floors`, verified by execution) and is closed-world, so a new prompt surface is never forced in; `local_clock_times` is audited against zero prompts and matched by none of the eight; the app-state block claims "current truth"/"evidence" against the Batch 181 floor in the same prompt; every read packet embeds a verbatim copy of its own system prompt (11,254 chars for morning), doubling it per generation and re-injecting it unbudgeted into every anchored chat turn; unprompted speech has no holiday guard; `verdictImpact`/`planMutation` are declared, not enforced; and the propose affordance and the coach's words are decided by two independent mechanisms. Remediation stubs stay review-local placeholders — no ledger batch numbers are allocated until Craig triages the whole wave. Verification: focused backend pytest 19 passed / 10 expected PostgreSQL skips over the prompt-policy, chat-context and state-change suites; Ruff clean across `src` and `tests`; the only executable work was read-only introspection of `coach_policy` patterns against in-repo prompt strings. **Shipped via PR #219, squash `0d9384f`:** branch-push, PR-context and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs; Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health served the exact merge SHA, database readiness was `ok`, web `/` returned 200, and the protected coach-thread and daily-loop routes returned 401 direct and proxied. Tier 🔴 High.

### 2026-08-06 — Batch 189 — Code review of the 157–187 delta and the new rails

270. **Batch 189 is a diagnose-only code review of the `7e77169..cc344f0` delta (82 commits, 228 files, +25,086 −3,362) whose durable artifact lives at `docs/reviews/BATCH_189_CODE_REVIEW.md`; it changes no product code, migration, configuration or production data.** Run on `chore/batch-189-code-review` after `/batch-start 189`, the second lane of the 2026-08-05 five-lane wave-2 audit. **Kickoff method decision:** verify each batch's "boundaries untouched" claim by tracing the *data path* rather than re-reading the diff — for a verdict claim, whether the new value appears in `_morning_verdict`'s parameters or only downstream of the computed `status`. That method settles nine of the ten claims quickly and correctly, and it is what surfaced the residual in Batch 180's coverage guard that a diff read would have missed. **Nine of ten boundary claims hold, several better than they had to:** Batch 173's `verdict_scaling.py` is provably downstream (`_verdict_adjustment_packet` takes the computed status as an argument); Batch 176/177's `body_metrics.py` is read-only and reaches only two packet builders; Batch 180's guard genuinely gates the driver correlation chronic patterns depend on, and the verdict's only load inputs (`acuteChronicLoadRatio`, `recoveryTimeMin`) are not columns the guard covers, so the verdict cannot move; Batch 178's `build_window` refactor is behaviour-preserving by equivalence; Batch 179's nullable `analysis_id` is handled by every consumer found; Batch 185's advisory lock is taken *before* the existence check, which is the correct order. **A checked non-finding worth recording:** `022`/`024` enable RLS without creating policies, but so do `015`, `019`, `020` and `021` — the pattern is deliberate and consistent, not an omission, and deployed verification belongs to Batch 190. **The tenth claim does not hold, and it is the lane's lead finding:** Decision #268 records "Batch 185's unseen coach launcher is the visibility rail" as the reason Batch 187 needed no new push type, but `CoachLauncher.tsx:80-82` hard-codes the unread dot to `originKind === 'weekly_review'`, so a `state_change` turn lights nothing and sends nothing; the backend added `state_change` to `ORIGIN_KINDS` while `coachOriginKindSchema` and `ORIGIN_PROMPTS` never did. **The second High is a reliability defect on the morning verdict:** only the two jobs added by Batches 185 and 187 call `session.rollback()` in their per-step handlers; every other `scheduler.py` job carries on with a Session SQLAlchemy has already invalidated, so one DB error inside Batch 180's four-date `_sync_garmin_daily` poisons the Session, `_sync_morning_inputs`'s `commit()` raises outside any per-step handler, and the whole morning pipeline aborts before generating a verdict — logged as a single generic line. **Test-gap conclusion:** the pure reducers are well covered and the mechanisms that make them safe are not — the one DB test for the state-change coach subclasses the service to replace `_candidates`, leaving the entire detection layer (and Decision #268's "derive previous state from stored packets" mechanism) unexecuted; no test anywhere opens two concurrent sessions against an advisory lock or the generation lease, so the suite cannot distinguish serialized from unsynchronized; and `test_coach_rls_migration.py` asserts each migration's `RLS_TABLES` *constant*, never its SQL. The report ranks **2 High / 9 Medium / 9 Low** findings `CR189-01…20` with `file:line` evidence and remediation stubs, severe-first: the mute state-change rail; the poisoned-Session scheduler; `StateChangeCoachService.run` as the only new writer with no lock and no unique constraint while the same window shipped two locked mechanisms; experiment detection writing an audit row on a path that never commits it (so those rows are discarded or committed under the next profile's transaction); two divergent definitions of "VO2 today" (workout-type name vs step intensity ≥106% FTP) that can leave the morning read silent about a session delivery will refuse; migration `022`'s backfill pairing the Nth activity to the Nth planned workout by two unrelated UUID orderings and overwriting `status` with `completed` without excluding `skipped`; an unbounded scan of every historical evaluation packet; and Batch 180's coverage guard reaching three call sites while five other consumers of the same columns stay unguarded. Remediation stubs stay review-local placeholders — no ledger batch numbers are allocated until Craig triages the whole wave. Verification: focused backend pytest 42 passed / 27 expected PostgreSQL skips over the state-change, weekly-review-delivery, generation-request, chat-context, migration and scheduler suites; Ruff clean across `src` and `tests`; and `CR189-02` was **confirmed by execution** — a throwaway script (scratchpad, not committed) drove SQLAlchemy's Session state machine through the exact catch-log-continue sequence `scheduler.py` performs and reproduced `PendingRollbackError` on both the next statement and the trailing `commit()`, with recovery after an explicit `rollback()`. No production database was queried; whether `022`'s backfill actually mislinked anything in Mark's history is handed to Batch 190/191. **Shipped via PR #220, squash `1839f79`:** branch-push, PR-context and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs; Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health both served the exact merge SHA, database readiness was `ok`, web `/` returned 200, and the protected coach-thread and daily-loop routes returned 401 direct and proxied. Tier 🔴 High.

### 2026-08-06 — Batch 190 — Data, security and operations review refresh

It was introduced by Batch 185's own commit `368ef69`, which added `relative` to anchor the new unread dot, and it is **live in the deployed production bundle** (`Layout-DNS4CIE5.js` contains the same call), at desktop width too. Second, the thread opens at `scrollTop: 0` of a 28,380 px pane with a 388 px window — the newest turn, where every proactive message lands, is **27,992 px below the fold**, and the first thing shown is 14 days old; the 60-message window is already truncating a real 82-message history (22 messages unreachable, no "load older" affordance). Third, a failed `GET /api/v1/coach/messages` renders as "Nothing here yet. Ask whatever's on your mind." because `CoachLauncher.tsx:78,149-153` collapses every non-success state to an empty list with no error branch — 82 messages of coaching history read as deleted, with an invitation to start over — and the inline read view is worse, rendering no copy at all. Taken together: had the Sunday review fired on 2026-08-09 as designed, it would have arrived on a button Mark cannot see, in the one part of the pane that is never on screen. **A fourth finding closes Batch 189's `CR189-01` at the UX layer by execution:** a `weekly_review` fixture lights the dot and renames the control "— new coach message", an identical `state_change` fixture lights nothing, because the unread rule hard-codes one origin while the vocabulary is split three ways (backend 15 kinds, shared schema and client 14, `originKind` typed as a plain nullable string so nothing fails loudly). **The fifth re-opens a wave-1 finding the remediation was believed to have closed:** 75 text nodes across 13 of 15 light-mode routes fail WCAG AA — including the active bottom-tab label at 3.55:1 — because Batch 163 defined the correct `*-text` tokens but the decorative fill tokens are still used as text at those sites, and `lib/semanticTextContrast.test.ts` stays green because it checks nine tokens against `--surface` in a CSS file and never renders a page. The report ranks **3 High / 5 Medium / 2 Low** findings `UX192-01…10` with `file:line`, measured geometry and screenshot evidence, plus remediation stubs `R192-A…J`: the unpinned launcher; the thread that opens at the oldest message; the dishonest failure state; the mute state-change rail; the light-mode contrast drift; a pending state indistinguishable from idle (question cleared, nothing appended, `busyCount: 0`); two chat views on one page with asymmetric membership (a launcher question from `/brief` stores `analysis_id = NULL` and can never appear in that read's inline chat, while the inline one appears in both) and two different empty-state vocabularies; seven controls under the app's own 44 px floor including the daily feel slider at 16 px; a 275 KB / 5.4 s `daily-loop` dominating cold time-to-content; and Batch 186's week-ahead guidance having no surface of its own and no channel that has ever fired. All fixtures, the simulated 503 and the held `POST` were fulfilled inside the browser and never reached the production write path or the model provider; the one minted activation produced exactly one device credential, which was revoked (204, then 401 on a protected route) with a follow-up query confirming zero live credentials created on 2026-08-06; the local proxy override and `.env.local` were reverted and are not in the branch diff. **Shipped via PR #223, squash `1f7507e`.** **Closeout deviation, deliberate and authorised:** this batch merged **without a green CI run**. GitHub Actions was in a critical platform outage at the time (githubstatus.com "Incident with Actions", opened 15:22 UTC 2026-08-06, still `investigating` at merge time, stating that "workflow runs are still failing or delayed in starting, and some queued jobs may time out"); the branch run sat queued for 25 minutes and had all seven jobs **cancelled**, so `gh` reported the run as `failure` although no job ever executed, and a second push produced no run at all. Craig authorised the merge on the grounds that the diff is docs-only.
