### 2026-08-15 — Batch 193 — The coach actually reaches Mark

274. **Batch 193 fixes the three UX192 findings that were live in production on the coach launcher, plus the CR189-01/UX192-04 vocabulary gap, ahead of the first-ever Sunday weekly review.** Run on `feat/batch-193-coach-reaches-mark` after `/batch-start 193`, the first slot of the wave-2 remediation roadmap approved 2026-08-06. **193.1 — the launcher is fixed again.** `CoachLauncher.tsx:126` dropped the redundant `relative` Batch 185 added to anchor the unread dot; `fixed` alone already establishes the positioning context an `absolute`-positioned child needs, so no replacement utility was required. Verified live (not by re-reading the class string, which is exactly what let this regression through Batch 185/192 unnoticed): booted the local dev server, seeded `localStorage` with a fake device token/player (no server auth check on load — the documented prod-free method), and read `getComputedStyle(button).position` and `getBoundingClientRect()` in a real browser — `"fixed"` and `inViewport: true`. **193.2 — the thread opens on its newest turn.** `CoachConversation`'s scrolling `<ol>` now sets `scrollTop = scrollHeight` in a `useEffect` keyed on message count and pending state, so it re-fires on mount (open) and after every reply; only the unbounded rolling thread scrolls internally, the inline per-read chat still lives in the page's own scroll. **The 60-message ceiling decision:** left as-is. `BriefChatService.thread()` already returns the most *recent* 60 turns (newest-first query, re-sorted), so raising visibility to the newest turn was the actual bug — a correctly-windowed API was hidden behind a frontend that opened at the top of its own truncated window. "No load older" for the 22 messages older than the window remains real but out of scope for a launcher-visibility batch; it is not re-flagged here as new debt because UX192-02 already named it. **193.3 — three distinct presentations, not one collapsed one.** `CoachConversation` takes an explicit `status: 'loading' | 'error' | 'ready'` prop (defaulting to `'ready'` for the inline per-read chat, which has no separate fetch to fail against) plus an `onRetry` callback; a failed thread fetch now reads "Could not load your conversation. Your past messages are still there." with a Try again button, distinct from both the loading copy and the healthy-empty copy, wired from both `CoachLauncher` (`threadQuery.isError`/`refetch`) and `BriefFollowUpChat` (`historyQuery.isError`/`refetch`). **193.4 — the vocabulary gap closes in both directions.** `coachOriginKindSchema` (`packages/shared/src/schemas.ts`) gains `state_change`, bringing the client's 14 kinds up to the backend `chat_context.ORIGIN_KINDS`' 15; `ORIGIN_PROMPTS` gains a matching entry (TypeScript now enforces this exhaustively via the `Record<CoachOriginKind, string>` type). The unread predicate widens from a hard-coded `originKind === 'weekly_review'` to a new shared constant `PROACTIVE_COACH_ORIGIN_KINDS = ['weekly_review', 'state_change']`, read by `CoachLauncher`'s `hasUnreadAssistant` check — a `state_change` turn now lights the dot exactly like a `weekly_review` one. **A backend parity test closes the drift risk going forward:** `test_chat_context.py::test_origin_kinds_match_the_client_schema` hardcodes the 15-kind set the client is supposed to know (there is no shared runtime between the two languages to check this automatically) and fails the moment `ORIGIN_KINDS` gains a kind without the mirror being updated — this is the test CR189/UX192's own finding said did not exist. **193.5 — the composer feels alive.** `CoachConversation` now appends an optimistic user bubble the instant a question is submitted (before the round trip completes) and a three-dot "thinking" indicator (`aria-live="polite"`) for the duration of `pending`, both cleared by an effect keyed on `pending` dropping — covering success (the real turns replace the optimistic one on invalidation) and failure (the existing toast covers it) identically. **193.6 — the full chain was executed, not just unit-tested.** Per the documented prod-free UI-walkthrough method (live-browser tools are constrained on this Mac), a disposable Vite `configureServer` middleware served a single fabricated `weekly_review` turn with Week-ahead prose from `GET /api/v1/coach/messages`, reverted before commit and never touching prod. Observed in the live browser: turn written → dot lit on load → launcher visible and in-viewport (`position: fixed`, confirmed above) → clicking opens the sheet already showing the turn → "Week ahead" markdown fully readable → opening also persisted `coach:last-seen-assistant:...` to `localStorage`, closing the read-state loop. **Gates:** frontend — `packages/shared` 26 vitest (2 new, covering the widened schema and the shared constant) + `tsc --noEmit`; `apps/web` 386 vitest (7 new/updated in `CoachLauncher.test.tsx` covering the widened unread predicate, the three status presentations with retry, the optimistic-turn/thinking-indicator lifecycle, and the scroll-to-newest effect), `tsc && vite build` clean, ESLint clean (pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings only). Backend — 771 passed / 339 expected PostgreSQL skips (1 new test), Ruff clean, mypy clean across 133 source files. No migration; no product verdict/plan/delivery logic touched. Tier 🟢 Mid.

### 2026-08-15 — Batch 193 closeout — reviewed JS advisory exception for nanoid

275. **`scripts/audit_js_advisories.py` gains a fourth reviewed exception, `GHSA-2v37-7h3g-55p8` (high: nanoid custom generators can loop indefinitely when `size` is zero), approved by Craig at closeout.** The advisory blocked CI's `Security audit (deps)` job on `feat/batch-193-coach-reaches-mark` even though the branch touches no dependency — `nanoid` is a transitive dev-tooling dependency of `postcss`, pulled in via `tailwindcss`/`vite`/`autoprefixer`, and `pnpm audit --prod` reproduces the same finding against `origin/main`, so this was blocking every branch, not something Batch 193 introduced. **Why the exception is safe:** the vulnerable path is `postcss`'s own internal ID generation, which is build-time only, never takes user input, and never calls the generator with `size=0`; `nanoid` itself is never shipped to the browser bundle. Craig chose this over a `pnpm` override/resolution bump (more work across several transitive paths, deferred rather than attempted) or pausing closeout entirely. **Why the underlying dependency stays unpinned rather than upgraded:** the fix is upstream in `postcss`'s pinned `nanoid` range across five separate transitive paths (`tailwindcss`, `vite`, `autoprefixer`, `vite-plugin-pwa`, `vitest`) — a targeted bump risks version-skew across build tooling for a build-time-only, non-shipped advisory, so the reviewed-exception path (already established for three React Router advisories) is the proportionate fix; revisit when those packages naturally pick up a patched `nanoid`. No product code, migration, or runtime dependency changed — `scripts/audit_js_advisories.py` only.

### 2026-08-15 — Batch 194 — A Red cannot be talked away

276. **Red-cluster check-in exclusions are now an explicitly bounded acute exception, and undecided chronic-deload offers live only while their deterministic signal does.** Implemented on `fix/batch-194-red-qualification` after `/batch-start 194`, closing CI191-01/03 and narrowly superseding Decision #263's blanket “recognized text excludes that Red” rule. **Cause boundary:** `training_load` and `deliberate_rest` are endogenous training context and always count toward the cluster — including when >24 hours of recovery debt sits beside intact HRV/RHR, because that is exactly the structural signal this rail exists to notice. `alcohol`, `illness`, and `travel` are acute exogenous reasons and retain a bounded exception. If HRV is below its floor/Low/Poor or RHR is above its personal ceiling, physiology contradicts the note and the Red counts. If both acute and endogenous tags appear, endogenous wins. **Cap and decay:** within the existing rolling seven-day Red window, at most one acute Red may be excluded; Reds are evaluated newest-first so the exception belongs to the most recent eligible event. That exception remains live for the Red date plus the following two calendar days, then the Red counts again while it is still inside the seven-day window. One slot preserves context for a genuinely isolated acute event; the two-day lifetime lets a short acute aftermath be recognized without granting a user-authored tag the full seven-day veto. The packet exposes `acuteRedExclusionLimit=1`, `acuteRedExclusionMaxAgeDays=2`, acute/endogenous reason partitions, and the qualification outcome. The real 2026-07-31/08-01 evidence now counts 2 rather than 0: the first is `endogenous_training_signal`; the second is `acute_cause_with_systemic_strain` because RHR 48 exceeds its ceiling 45. This can only retain or add structural caution relative to Batch 182 and still has `verdictImpact='none'`; the daily Green/Amber/Red ladder, thresholds, adjustment transform, and Red-never-VO2 do not receive this input. Morning prompt v28 explains the frozen classification without arguing it down. **Proposal lifetime:** when a well-formed current packet no longer carries an active `deload_proposal` (cleared or changed), only future `status='proposed'`, `origin='chronic_deload'` rows with no `intervals_event_id` expire to the existing terminal `deleted` state. Each gets an idempotent `workout_proposal_expired` analysis row naming `chronic_action_cleared` or `chronic_action_changed`. Past, other-origin, event-bearing, approved, failed, and pushed proposals stay untouched; no cloud delete is attempted. A missing/malformed current action fails closed and changes nothing. **Boundaries:** backend + prompt/tests/docs only; no migration, shared schema, endpoint, frontend, provider, hosting, plan mutation, daily-verdict change, or production intervention. The 08-08/08-09 rows were already manually retracted on 2026-08-06, so this batch adds the durable mechanism rather than replaying that intervention. Local gates: 781 passed / 340 expected PostgreSQL skips; Ruff clean; mypy clean across 133 source files. Branch-push CI passed all seven jobs, including PostgreSQL pytest. Tier 🔴 High.

### 2026-08-15 — Batch 195 — Jobs that fail say so

277. **A scheduled invocation has one typed outcome and one independent operator record; partial failure is success only when it is explicitly a no-op.** Implemented on `feat/batch-195-job-failure-signals` after `/batch-start 195`, closing CR189-02/20 and DS190-02/04 while leaving DS190-01's hosting shape gated. **Result contract:** every real APScheduler workload and every external `run_scheduled` invocation returns `succeeded`, `skipped`, `degraded`, or `failed` with a stable reason and integer counters. External mode exits 0 for succeeded/skipped and 1 for degraded/failed; inability to persist the outcome itself becomes `failed/job_run_persistence_failed`, so a cron platform can never mistake missing evidence for success. **Durable evidence:** migration `027` adds operator-only `job_runs` (cadence window, start/finish, status, reason, counters), RLS-enabled with no authenticated-user policy. The tracker writes in a fresh transaction after the job session closes, so a poisoned/rolled-back unit of work cannot erase the failure record. **Morning survival:** weather profiles and Garmin dates commit at isolated recovery boundaries; every caught write-capable morning step rolls back before the next query, and guarded input commits return degradation rather than unwinding past analysis. A real PostgreSQL aborted-transaction regression proves the session can execute the downstream verdict query after one failed Garmin date; a second orchestration regression proves an input failure still reaches morning generation. **Deploy freshness:** a post-successful-`main`-CI GitHub workflow polls both Railway-direct and Vercel-proxied `/api/v1/health` for the exact merge SHA, bounded at 15 minutes; mismatch, unknown SHA or unreachability fails the workflow and emits the operator alert. **Explicit non-decision:** the API scheduler remains enabled and no Railway service/cost/configuration changes. Craig must still choose always-on API versus full externalisation, and every external workload must prove a successful run before any cutover; the in-process-only 20:30 post-workout backstop is not silently externalised here. No endpoint, shared/frontend contract, prompt, verdict, plan, provider, or production row changes beyond the new run ledger. Local gates: 789 passed / 341 expected PostgreSQL skips; Ruff/format/mypy clean across 135 source files; Alembic head is `027`. Branch-push CI passed all seven jobs, including PostgreSQL pytest and migration upgrade/downgrade. Tier 🔴 High.

### 2026-08-15 — Batch 196 — A backup you have actually restored

278. **The backup proof is an external restore drill plus operator monitoring, not an end-user push or another app table.** Implemented on `feat/batch-196-backup-restore` after `/batch-start 196`, closing DS190-03 without a migration. The new `backup-drill` external runner restores the newest `coach_*.dump` archive into `BACKUP_RESTORE_DATABASE_URL` with `pg_restore --clean --if-exists --no-owner`, refuses to target the same host/user/database as production even if the password differs, then asserts real recovery invariants: at least 20 `coach` base tables, a non-empty `coach.alembic_version`, at least one profile, at least one analysis, and zero restored `coach.activity_timeseries` rows because Decision #262 intentionally dumps that replayable table's definition only. **Alerting decision:** backup and restore-drill failures emit the structured log `operator backup alert` (`kind=backup_failed` or `backup_restore_drill_failed`) and return failed `JobResult`s so Batch 195's external runner exits 1 and writes `job_runs` evidence where possible. This is deliberately outside `admin_alert_user_id` and app pushes: Mark must not receive operator backup pages, and creating an operator profile would accidentally enroll it in user-facing scheduler loops. **Scheduling / recovery target:** `backup-drill` is external-runner only and should run weekly after the daily backup (Sunday 04:00 UTC in the runbook), once a disposable restore database is provisioned. Runbooks now require a weekly encrypted off-site archive after the drill passes, keep at least four weekly copies, and state the recovery contract honestly: 24-hour RPO while the Railway volume survives, 7-day RPO for a Railway/Supabase platform-loss event after off-site cadence begins, and a 4-hour RTO once a PostgreSQL target and app environment are available. Boundaries: no migration, no production write during implementation, no hosted storage choice embedded in app code. Local gates: 794 passed / 341 expected PostgreSQL skips; Ruff clean; mypy clean across 135 source files. Tier 🟢 Mid.

### 2026-08-15 — Batch 197 — Unprompted speech agrees with the brief

279. **The morning brief's stored packet is the authority for morning-backed unprompted transitions, and the shared seven-day speech budget may be pre-empted only once by strictly more important news.** Implemented on `fix/batch-197-unprompted-speech` after `/batch-start 197`, closing CC188-01/02/08/10/20, CR189-09, and CI191-07's rail half without a migration. **Snapshot boundary:** chronic action and weekly-mix candidates compare today's latest stored `morning` packet with the latest stored packet before today; the coach no longer recomputes chronic state through insights/chronic-pattern services or forces weekly mix through a synthetic Green verdict. This makes its current snapshot byte-for-byte the state Mark's brief used and removes three service calls per profile/day. If either morning packet is absent or malformed, those comparison lanes are unknown and silent rather than treating missing state as a change. Experiment outcomes retain their independent evaluation-history comparison; its writer/concurrency cleanup remains Batch 198. **Budget boundary:** candidates are ranked before the budget is read (`chronic_action` > `experiment_outcome` > `weekly_mix`). An empty rolling seven-day window admits an `initial` spend. One existing lower-ranked spend may be pre-empted once by a strictly higher-ranked candidate; that second analysis records `budgetSpend='preemption'` and `preemptedAnalysisId`. A same/lower-ranked candidate, a window already containing two rows, an existing pre-emption, or missing/ambiguous rank metadata stays silent. A pre-Batch-197 row with a valid `transitionKind` is treated as the window's initial spend, so a new higher-ranked transition is not stranded by the rollout. This preserves a tight global interruption budget while preventing a weekly-mix heads-up from permanently swallowing a new chronic-deload transition. **Holiday boundary:** the scheduler applies the weekly-review active-holiday guard before candidate generation and records `skipped_holiday`; an all-holiday run is a typed skipped job. The deterministic templates move to `state-change-coach:v2-2026-08-15`; their words, Green/Amber/Red, safety floors, Red-never-VO2, plan/proposal state, endpoints, frontend, providers, auth, and hosting are unchanged. Real unstubbed PostgreSQL regressions seed stored morning packets for standing state, a genuine flip, and a missing previous read; budget regressions cover chronic pre-emption and the one-pre-emption ceiling. Local gates: 795 passed / 346 expected PostgreSQL skips; Ruff/format clean; mypy clean across 135 source files. After correcting one test-fixture flush exposed by the first PostgreSQL run, branch CI passed all seven jobs with 1,141 tests, the Alembic round trip, dependency/RLS/secret audits, frontend tests/build, Ruff and mypy green; the first run's unrelated `pip-audit` vulnerability-service connection reset also cleared unchanged on rerun. Tier 🔴 High.

### 2026-08-15 — Batch 198 — The unprompted writer is safe to run twice

280. **One state-change run is one user/date artifact, and suppressed transitions are not queued for replay.** Implemented on `fix/batch-198-state-change-writer-lock` after `/batch-start 198`, closing CR189-03/04/07/16 and folding in CC188-17/19 without a migration. **Concurrency boundary:** `StateChangeCoachService.run()` now takes a transaction-scoped `pg_advisory_xact_lock` as its first database statement, keyed by `state-change:{user_id}:{subject_date}` before any budget, candidate, or insert work. This mirrors the review-writer guard: in-process APScheduler and external cron may overlap, but the second transaction waits until the first commits, then observes the spent budget/current row rather than creating a duplicate unsolicited assistant turn. A real two-session PostgreSQL regression pauses the first writer after budget approval and starts a second independent `AsyncSession`; the final state is one analysis and one message, with one run delivered and the other budget-spent. **Experiment read boundary:** state-change detection needs the current deterministic recommendation, not an evaluation audit artifact, so `_experiment_candidates()` calls `ExperimentEvaluationService.evaluate()` and builds the same packet in memory via `evaluation_packet()` instead of `run(commit=False)`. That removes the prior uncommitted audit-row side effect, where rows could be flushed and later committed by unrelated profile work or discarded. **History lookup boundary:** previous experiment comparison now pushes `context_packet ->> 'experimentId' = <id>` into the `Analysis` query with date ordering and `limit(1)`, replacing the unbounded Python scan of every historical evaluation packet for every active experiment. **Delivery semantics:** the old `already_delivered` duplicate-delivery branch is deleted rather than becoming an outbox. If a transition loses the seven-day budget, a same-day double-fire, or an unknown/missing comparison state, that transition is dropped permanently; the next unsolicited turn is only for a later newly observed state change. This keeps the rail a low-interruption coach nudge, not a durable retry queue. Backend-only; no prompt, Green/Amber/Red, plan/proposal, delivery rail, endpoint, frontend, provider, auth, hosting, or schema change. Local gates: 795 passed / 347 expected PostgreSQL skips; Ruff/format clean; mypy clean across 135 source files. **Shipped via PR #231 / squash `3990ce9`:** branch, PR-context and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs; production freshness passed; exact-SHA Railway/Vercel health, web, protected-route and read-only deployed-code smokes passed. Tier 🟢 Mid.

### 2026-08-15 — Batch 199 — Floors that can actually fail

281. **A prompt floor is a semantic prohibition with a failing negative control, and every model-calling surface must be discovered or explicitly justified.** Implemented on `fix/batch-199-prompt-floor-audit` after `/batch-start 199`, closing CC188-04/05/06/07/11/14/18 and CI191-04 without a migration. **Recognition boundary:** each of the ten `Floor` records now carries canonical conversation wording, a recognizer, and an inverted negative control. The Red/VO₂, power-balance and skipped/holiday recognizers require a prohibition plus the protected subject inside one clause; nearby topic words no longer count as compliance. The policy test proves both sides for every floor: its canonical statement matches and its inversion is reported missing. **Discovery boundary:** the audit parses the service tree and discovers real calls to `generate_anthropic_text` or `AnthropicReviewClient`; the discovered set must equal the audited module registry plus the two commented exceptions. `brief_chat` is exempt because it composes every canonical floor directly. `conversation_learning` is non-user-facing, strict-schema filtered and confirm-before-apply, so it is versioned and provenance-tested rather than forced to recite coaching floors. Deterministic `state_change_coach` / `nudge_alerts` templates make no model call and remain under their own output tests; that disposition is now documented beside the registry. **Ownership boundary:** `local_clock_times` is recognized as `local clock time` as well as `local time/timezone`, requires the explicit no-UTC rule, and is owned by morning plus the ride/strength/flexibility/walk post-session prompts. The five deterministic protections are promoted to shared floors: explain `trainingLoadCap`, `sleepCreditCeiling`, `cumulativeEscalation`, `readinessBaselineTrend`, and `chronicAction` when present, but never soften or argue them down. Only the morning prompt owns those fields. **Truth and mutation boundary:** ask-time trend/state descriptions now call their figures app records, never independent truth; a shared PostgreSQL test helper snapshots every planned-workout row and every verdict/action analysis before speech and proves they are byte-for-byte unchanged afterward, replacing assertions that trusted `verdictImpact='none'` / `planMutation='none'` labels. **Learning provenance:** `conversation_learning` gains `conversation-learning-v1-2026-08-15`; the version is persisted with each evidence record and enters the proposal fingerprint, so a future prompt revision has attributable, distinct proposals without a schema change. Changed user-facing prompts move to coach chat v8, morning v29, post-workout v16 and post-strength/flexibility/walk v6. Green/Amber/Red computation, thresholds, plan/proposal behavior, endpoints, frontend, providers, auth and hosting are unchanged. Local gates: 798 passed / 347 expected PostgreSQL skips; Ruff/format clean; mypy clean across 135 source files. **Shipped via PR #232 / squash `88a8441`:** branch-push, PR-context and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs; the Vercel preview and production-freshness workflow passed; exact-SHA Railway/Vercel health, web, protected-route and read-only deployed-container policy/version smokes passed. ### 2026-08-15 — Batch 201 — Verdict-ladder residuals

282. **Age credit may lift a raw-Red sleep night only to Amber; a Low-readiness `load_driven` escape needs proved-benign load; and every Amber adjustment shares a 94% FTP Sweet Spot ceiling.** Implemented on `fix/batch-201-verdict-ladder-residuals` after `/batch-start 201`, closing CI191-05/06 and CI191-07's remaining load half without a migration. **Sleep-credit boundary:** when Garmin's raw sleep score is below 60 and age scoring lifts it across 60, `sleepCreditCeiling` sets `maximumStatus='Amber'` even when HRV, RHR, readiness and the current check-in all corroborate; the age model can still prevent an automatic Red, but it cannot turn a Garmin-Poor night Green. Batch 170's separate raw-60…73 → adjusted-74+ complete-corroboration exception is preserved. **Load boundary:** Low readiness relaxes only when the existing age-adjusted sleep/HRV/check-in evidence is positive, ACWR is observed at or below 1.30, and any observed Garmin recovery clock is at most 24 hours. Missing ACWR and ACWR 1.31–1.49 are unknown/elevated rather than benign; >24 hours independently conflicts and still triggers Batch 167's Amber cap. The packet exposes this under `loadDrivenEligibility`. **Adjustment decision:** do not split load-cap Amber from recovery Amber and reintroduce the four-path drift Batch 173 removed. Harden the one shared transform instead: duration remains 75%, Zone 2 remains intensity-held, harder work still drops a zone, and the universal Amber cap moves from threshold (98%) to the top of the app's existing Sweet Spot range (94%). Delivery regeneration, the interval-editor Scale down preset, the deterministic packet/action copy and prompt v30 therefore continue to quote one result; generic wording says former HIT/VO2 is converted to no more than 94% rather than falsely saying the work disappeared. The combined matrix proves raw-Red credit and unproved load Green→Amber, 115% work 98→94 at the same Amber light, and preserved corroborated-Green, benign-load and acute-Red controls. Backend gates: 805 passed / 347 expected PostgreSQL skips; Ruff/format/mypy clean across 135 source files. No shared-schema, frontend, plan, provider, auth, hosting, or Red-never-VO2 change. **Shipped via PR #234 / squash `c6e5f21`:** after an approved one-line correction to a stale PostgreSQL-only expected string, branch-push, PR-context and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs; Vercel preview and production freshness passed; exact-SHA Railway/Vercel health, web, protected-route and read-only deployed-container boundary smokes passed.
