### 2026-08-15 — Batch 202 — Chat context tells the truth about itself

283. **Chat receives app state as app state, not re-labelled instructions or self-referential deltas.** Implemented on `fix/batch-202-chat-context-truth` after `/batch-start 202`, closing CC188-03/09/12/13/15/16 and CI191-08 without a migration. **Prompt metadata boundary:** generated read packets now store `prompt.version` and `prompt.systemHash` only; the full system prompt is still passed to the model at generation time but is not persisted in `context_packet`, so anchored chat cannot re-ingest an old read's instruction set as "Mark's information." A chat-time sanitizer converts legacy stored `prompt.system` values to the same hash, avoiding a production data rewrite. **Delta honesty:** activity deltas are keyed to `Activity.created_at` and named `activitiesIngestedSinceRead`, while still carrying `startUtc`; a late-synced pre-read ride is visible, and a ride whose ingestion predates the read is not mislabeled as new. Closed planned workouts on the read's subject date are renamed `subjectDateClosedWorkoutsCurrent` and explicitly described as current status, not as "closed since read," because there is no status-change timestamp on `planned_workouts` and adding one would be a separate migration decision. **Budget/truncation honesty:** review conclusions and plan-change summaries that are shortened are named in `omittedForLength`; whole-section drops keep the same named-omission semantics, and if safe trims still leave the app-state block over `APP_STATE_CHAR_BUDGET`, the block records `best_effort_over_budget` and logs it rather than pretending the cap is hard. **Proposal gate:** a chat proposal affordance now requires three facts together: Mark's question contains adjustment intent, today has a live adjustable bike workout, and the model's answer includes the exact service-stripped marker that it actually offered a proposal. This prevents keyword-only cases like "make it harder" from attaching a proposal when the coach refused or answered another point. **Body metrics:** ask-time app state and morning packets now carry effective Garmin weight and VO2max with as-of dates, using the same carry-forward windows as post-workout reads, so metrics questions can be answered from app data rather than from numbers Mark supplies in chat. Backend-only; no schema/shared/frontend/provider/auth/hosting/verdict/plan mutation. Local gates: 806 passed / 352 expected PostgreSQL skips; Ruff clean; mypy clean across 136 source files. **Closeout:** shipped via PR #235 / squash `214f9e0`; branch, PR-context and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs; Vercel preview and production freshness passed; exact-SHA Railway/Vercel health, web `/`, and protected coach-route smokes passed. Tier 🟢 Mid.

### 2026-08-15 — Batch 203 — Correctness cleanup

284. **Small correctness gaps should reuse the existing deterministic boundaries rather than add schema.** Implemented on `fix/batch-203-correctness-cleanup` after `/batch-start 203`, closing CR189-05/08/10 and the listed low folds without a migration. **VO2 boundary:** the morning verdict now determines `hasVo2WorkoutToday` from the same structured-workout IR intensity predicate the delivery rail uses (`ir_has_vo2`, >=106% FTP) whenever the IR is buildable, falling back to the workout type name only when the IR cannot be built. This connects Mark-facing Red guidance to the hard push blocker for manually overridden sessions whose type is generic but whose steps are VO2 intensity. `red_never_vo2` is now listed in `safetyRulesApplied` only when a Red day actually has VO2-intensity work to block, so the packet stops claiming a rule fired on a plain recovery/endurance day. **Aggregate-coverage boundary:** Garmin local-day stress and Body Battery columns are valid only when their raw source window covers the complete local day. The shared `daily_metric_coverage` helper now owns complete-value accessors, and the remaining consumers use them before partial rows enter morning baseline comparisons, yesterday whole-day cost, DB-history Body Battery baselines, review Body Battery averages, or insight driver records. Readiness/HRV/RHR remain raw because this coverage guard is for Garmin aggregate windows, not point-in-time recovery markers. **Idempotency boundary:** notification push dedupe remains tag-based and migration-free, but `_send_once` now takes a transaction-scoped PostgreSQL advisory lock over `(user, analysis_type, subject_date, tag)` before the existing read/write, so concurrent workers cannot both observe "not recorded" and send. A two-session regression pauses the first send and proves the second call waits and returns false after the first commits. **Weekly-review delivery boundary:** scheduled weekly-review coach turns dedupe by `(user, origin_kind, origin_date)` — the delivered week — rather than `analysis_id`, so a prompt-version refresh updates the one Sunday thread turn instead of appending another. Failure turns also dedupe by origin/date rather than exact copy, so copy changes do not create a second failure message. **Date-helper cleanup:** the repeated activity-local-date conversion moved to one shared helper used by the former duplicate call sites. Backend-only; no endpoint, prompt, shared/frontend contract, Green/Amber/Red thresholds, plan/proposal behavior, provider, auth, hosting, or production row change. Local gates: focused/broadened backend service tests 155 passed / 41 expected local PostgreSQL skips, targeted non-DB checks 9 passed / 3 expected skips, Ruff clean, mypy clean across 137 source files. Tier 🟢 Mid. **Closeout:** CI was the first real run against Postgres (no local `DATABASE_URL`, so the new DB-backed tests had been silently skipped, not verified) and caught two gaps: three new concurrency/dedupe tests added a `Profile` and a dependent FK row to the same session without the codebase's established `flush()`-before-dependents pattern (`ForeignKeyViolationError` on real Postgres), and four `post_*_analysis.py` modules imported the shared `activity_local_date` helper re-exported through `post_workout_analysis.py`, which mypy's no-implicit-reexport rule rejects for an underscore-aliased import. Both fixed pre-merge (`c877d6e`, `18fe650`). Shipped via PR #236 / squash `f7be1c3`; branch, PR-context, and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs; Vercel preview and production freshness passed; exact-SHA Railway/Vercel health, web `/`, and protected daily-loop 401 smokes passed.

### 2026-08-16 — Batch 204 — Security hygiene

285. **An egress budget monitor without a provider API is a documented proxy, not a fabricated meter; and an RLS-coverage test must be honest about what CI's plain Postgres can actually prove.** Implemented on `fix/batch-204-security-hygiene` after `/batch-start 204`, closing DS190-07/09 and CR189-11/13 without a migration. **Egress boundary:** Supabase has no public Management API for egress bytes (confirmed against its docs and the community `supabase-management-js` SDK while authoring this batch) — only the account dashboard exposes the org-wide meter. `services/egress_budget.py` and a new `EgressBudgetMiddleware` therefore build a leading-indicator proxy instead: response `Content-Length` accumulates into a UTC-day-scoped in-memory counter, and a new `egress-budget` scheduled job (every 15 min, alongside the other interval jobs) drains it, durably persists the delta as a `job_runs` counter (surviving the frequent container restarts DS190-01 already documented), adds the day's backup archive size, and logs a structured `operator egress alert` only when the day's highest staged threshold (50%/85% of the known 5.5GB org cap, DECISIONS #262) increases — not on every run, so a sustained overage does not spam. The runbook documents this explicitly as "go check the dashboard," not proof of the real total, and adds the scoped `pg_dump --schema=coach` command for manual/ad hoc dumps so an operator never runs a bare unscoped one again. **RLS-test boundary:** CR189-11 flagged that `test_coach_rls_migration.py` only asserted each migration's `RLS_TABLES` Python constant, never the SQL a migration actually emits. A first implementation added a `pg_class.relrowsecurity` Postgres-fixture test — it failed in CI, correctly by design: every RLS migration (015 onward) wraps its `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` statement in a guard that only fires when an `auth` schema exists, deliberately no-op on CI's plain `postgres:16` service so the migration-check and unit-test jobs stay green without Supabase's schema. A live check there would read `relrowsecurity=false` for every table regardless of whether the ENABLE statement is present or missing, which is not the gap CR189-11 describes. Replaced before merge with a pure test that runs each RLS migration's real `upgrade()` against a recording mock `op` (the same technique `test_auth_cutover_migration.py` already uses) and asserts the captured SQL contains the ENABLE statement for every table in that migration's own `RLS_TABLES` — this does catch a migration that declares a table and forgets the ALTER. Live deployed-state verification against a real Supabase-shaped database continues to be `scripts/check_rls_posture.py` + `RLS_POSTURE_DATABASE_URL`, already wired into CI. **Disclosure boundary:** `brief_chat._owned_analysis` now returns 404 for both an absent and a foreign read anchor (previously 403 for foreign), so an authenticated second user who obtains or guesses a UUID cannot distinguish "does not exist" from "exists but is not yours" from the HTTP status alone; the distinction is retained only in a structured log line for operator diagnosis. `history()` now filters `BriefMessage.user_id` redundantly alongside `analysis_id`, matching the ownership check that already gates it, so a future writer or repair script producing a mismatched row could not make it visible here. `conversation_learning._sources`'s outer join now carries the same `Analysis.user_id` predicate its sibling `_resolve_source` join already had, closing the one asymmetric defence-in-depth gap between the two. Backend-only; no endpoint contract, prompt, Green/Amber/Red, plan/proposal, shared/frontend, provider, auth, or hosting change. Local gates: 823 passed, Ruff/format clean, mypy clean across 138 source files (the RLS-migration-source test needs no `DATABASE_URL` and runs locally, unlike the discarded live-DB attempt). **Shipped via PR #237 / squash `4478ad5`:** branch, PR-context and post-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs — the real Postgres pytest run in CI is what caught the RLS-test design flaw before merge, fixed on the branch (`d20dde0`); Vercel preview and production freshness passed; production verified exact-SHA Railway + Vercel health (`4478ad583689bb5332579d8655ae41ec9d71b615`), web `/` 200, protected `/api/v1/daily-loop` 401. Tier 🟢 Mid.

### 2026-08-16 — Batch 205 — The day's record means one thing

286. **A day's Garmin record is two observations, not one mutable row, and the verdict a date is remembered by is the one Mark was shown.** Implemented on `fix/batch-205-daily-metric-phase` after `/batch-start 205`, closing CI191-02 with migration `028`. **Why a fix was needed at all:** `recorded_at_utc` is Garmin's own timestamp on the training-readiness reading, not our sync clock, and there is no evening sync job — the overwrite came from the *next* morning's run, whose `D-1..D-3` pass re-fetches each closed day and receives that day's final reading. `daily_metrics` was unique on `(user_id, calendar_date)` and `sync_daily` selected-then-overwrote, so the wake observation the verdict had already been computed from did not survive the day, and every retrospective consumer read the survivor. **Shape decision (Craig at `/batch-start`, over a JSONB snapshot column or reading the stored packet):** phase-keyed rows. The unique key becomes `(user_id, calendar_date, phase)` with a `phase IN ('morning','settled')` check; the wake sync writes `morning`, the closed-day re-sync and both history importers write `settled`. Column shape is unchanged, which is what let all nineteen read sites keep aggregating in Python. `sync_daily` takes `phase` as a **required** keyword and rejects an unknown value, while the model defaults to `settled` — so a test fixture that does not care gets the historically correct meaning and the one writer that must be deliberate cannot silently reintroduce the overwrite. The migration adds the column with a `settled` default, backfills, then **drops the default**. **Backfill:** historical `morning` rows are reconstructed from the earliest stored morning `analyses.context_packet -> 'dailyMetrics'` per date, a faithful column-for-column mirror of the row as it stood at wake including `recordedAtUtc`. Two limits are accepted and documented in the migration: `raw_payload` was never captured in the packet so a reconstructed row carries the same-date settled blob (the scalar readings CI191-02 is about are exact; the fields read back out of that blob are day-level context), and a date with no stored morning read gets no `morning` row — consumers fall back to `settled` rather than inventing a wake observation. **Per-consumer phase, the decision the remediation stub actually asked for:** engine and retrospective narrative read `morning` (personal baselines, the 84-day readiness history, the Red-morning cluster and its expected-training-debt exclusion, trends, reviews, insight early-warning, block progression, the post-session and Home reads, the weekly-restructure recovery signal, and weight/VO2max tie-breaks); `settled` is deliberate in exactly two places — the morning packet's *yesterday's whole-day cost* block, because that is a whole-day question and yesterday's wake reading predates yesterday's session, and the Garmin freshness check, which wants the newest observation of any phase. **Field-level exception:** stress and Body Battery are running local-day totals, not point readings, and `daily_metric_coverage` already reports the wake row's partial window as incomplete and gates it to `None`. Taking them off the morning row would have blanked every historical day, so `index_day_aggregates_by_date` feeds those three fields from the settled row while recovery readings on the same date come from the morning one. This is why Batch 180's in-place repair of a partial aggregate and Batch 205's preservation of the wake reading are complementary rather than opposed, and its regression asserts both halves of the same seeded date. **Verdict decision (Craig at `/batch-start`):** pin, rather than leave the metric row alone. `analyses` keeps every historical morning row by design, and "the newest wins" made a stored verdict mutable (2026-07-05 reads `Amber@07:23 → Green@22:03`). New leaf `services/delivered_verdict.py` counts the last morning read generated at or before the local close of the wake-detection window — a post-check-in regeneration lands inside it and rightly counts, an evening one does not — falling back to a date's earliest read when the morning was missed entirely. It is shared by the Red cluster, the reviews and the block-progression trend, replacing three separate latest-wins collapses. It only selects among stored rows: nothing recomputes a verdict, and the Green/Amber/Red ladder stays deterministic Python. **Boundaries:** no prompt, threshold, verdict logic, plan/proposal, delivery rail, endpoint, shared schema, frontend, provider, auth or hosting change. The read-only `scripts/diagnose_coaching_data.sql` was made phase-aware in the same batch because its 84-day readiness quartiles are the CI191-02 evidence itself and would otherwise have silently mixed both phases in the next audit. Local gates: 840 passed / 362 expected PostgreSQL skips; Ruff check/format clean; mypy clean across 140 source files. Branch CI passed all seven jobs (`1202 passed, 0 skipped` — every locally-skipped Postgres test really ran, including the twelve new ones, plus the real `upgrade head`/`downgrade base` round trip over `028`). **Gotcha worth carrying forward:** this machine has no Postgres and no Docker, so the twelve new PostgreSQL-backed regressions could not run locally and CI is their first real execution — the Batch 203 trap. What *was* validated locally is the migration itself: `alembic upgrade 027:028 --sql` and `downgrade 028:027 --sql` render the full DDL offline with no database, which proves the Alembic API calls and the backfill SQL without waiting for CI. Also: the packet key is `dailyMetrics`, plural — a backfill written against `dailyMetric` would have silently inserted zero rows and passed every test. **Shipped via PR #238 / squash `08e6859`:** branch, PR-context and post-merge `main` CI all passed seven jobs with `1202 passed, 0 skipped` — every locally-skipped Postgres test really ran, including the twelve new ones, plus the real `upgrade head`/`downgrade base` round trip over `028`. Production verified on the exact merge SHA `08e6859be8086a633b03893e8fe5634af7c011dc`: Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health both served it, readiness `db: ok`, web `/` 200, protected `/api/v1/daily-loop` 401 direct and proxied. The migration ran at Railway container startup as designed. Read-only batch smoke against the live database: `alembic_version` `028`, 475 rows (419 settled + 56 morning across 419 distinct days), `uq_daily_metrics_user_date` gone and `uq_daily_metrics_user_date_phase` + `ck_daily_metrics_phase` present, `phase` server default `NULL` (the drop-default step worked, so a writer that omits phase now fails loudly), zero morning rows missing `recorded_at_utc` or carrying an empty `raw_payload`. A read-only pre-merge measurement predicted all 56 rows exactly and quantified the defect on Mark's own data: readiness differs between the two phases on **45 of 56** dates, wake readiness higher on 30 and the wake recovery clock lower on 30, mean deltas +6.4 points and -397 minutes; all 56 sit inside the 84-day baseline window, of which 56 now carry a morning row and 28 fall back to settled. **2026-07-30 — the audit's own headline failure case — now holds both observations**: `morning` MODERATE/64 with a 943-minute clock recorded 07:15, `settled` POOR/19 with 3233 minutes recorded 20:36. Tier 🔴 High.

### 2026-08-16 — Batch 206 — The daily loop stops shipping what nothing reads

287. **An API response is a contract about what the client needs, not a mirror of what the row happens to hold — and a payload decision is made against a measurement, not an assumption.** Implemented on `fix/batch-206-daily-loop-payload` after `/batch-start 206`, closing UX192-09 without a migration. **What was wrong:** `DailyMetricOut.rawPayload` and `SleepOut.rawPayload` serialised the entire stored Garmin response into every `/api/v1/daily-loop` payload. Nothing in `apps/web/src` ever read either one — they were validated by the shared zod schema and then dropped on the floor — and the daily loop is the endpoint the morning path cannot avoid. **The measurement is what makes this batch decisive.** The wave-2 triage graded UX192-09 Low and deferred it on the assumption that slimming the endpoint meant splitting it. Measured against production instead: the two blobs serialise to **264,729 bytes on average** across the last 14 days (metric ~59 kB, sleep ~206 kB, peak 281 kB combined) against the review's measured 275,530-byte payload — **96.1%** of it. The earlier `pg_column_size` readings (~14 kB / ~22 kB) are TOAST-*compressed* storage and were the wrong unit; JSON text is what crosses the wire, and there is **no compression middleware anywhere in the API** — no `GZipMiddleware`, nothing in `vercel.json` — so the response genuinely was served uncompressed. **Decision at 206.2, on the pre-agreed criterion:** stop at 206.1 and do not build 206.3. Deleting two fields takes the endpoint from ~275 kB to roughly 11 kB; a hero-first split would now be optimising an 11 kB payload, so the architectural change the finding assumed is unnecessary and would add a second code path for no measurable gain. **Boundary:** the columns are untouched and every server-side reader still reads the row directly — `routers/bedroom.py`'s `extract_hypnogram`, `morning_analysis`'s `_training_and_activity_fields` and `_extract_fitness_age`, and `daily_metric_coverage`. Only the wire format changed. **Rollout is safe in both directions and was checked before shipping:** zod strips unknown keys, so a new bundle against an old server simply ignores the field, and the pre-batch schema declared `.default({})`, so a cached old bundle against the new server does not fail validation. **Guards, because the regression is silent:** re-adding the field costs nothing locally and surfaces only as a slower cold load on Mark's phone, so two backend assertions pin its absence from both response models — deliberately paired with a positive assertion that the neighbouring readings Home and the sleep page actually render are still present, since a field's absence cannot prove itself correct — plus a shared-schema test that parses input *containing* `rawPayload` and asserts the result has no such key, because zod's silent stripping means a regressed server would otherwise raise no error anywhere. **Noted and deliberately not taken:** `SleepOut.factorsJson` is also unread by the web, but at ~1.1 kB it is 3% of the win and sleep factors are plausibly displayable later; and the absence of response compression across the whole API is a real cross-cutting win that belongs in its own batch rather than riding along here. Backend + shared-schema only; no migration, verdict, prompt, plan, delivery, endpoint-shape, auth or hosting change. Local gates: backend 842 passed / 362 expected PostgreSQL skips, Ruff check+format clean, mypy clean across 140 source files; `pnpm -r test` green (shared 27, web 386), shared `tsc --noEmit` clean, web lint 0 errors / 6 known warnings, production build clean under Node 20. **Gotcha worth carrying forward:** `pnpm --dir apps/web test` — the command `AGENTS.md` documents — does **not** run `packages/shared`'s tests; CI uses `pnpm -r test`, and the new shared-schema guard was invisible to the documented command. **Shipped via PR #239 / squash `c5231ca`:** branch, PR-context and post-merge `main` CI all passed seven jobs (`1204 passed, 0 skipped`); the Vercel preview built the tree. Production verified on the exact merge SHA `c5231cac46ee67bfe3cdf68f4dbc45df2c3ce338` — Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health both served it, readiness `db: ok`, web `/` 200, protected `/api/v1/daily-loop` 401 direct and proxied. **The batch smoke is a read-only introspection of the running container** (`railway ssh --service api`), because the endpoint is authenticated and production exposes no OpenAPI schema (`/openapi.json` and `/docs` both 404): the deployed `DailyMetricOut` and `SleepOut` report `rawPayload` **absent from both** (22 and 24 fields), `readinessScore`/`recoveryTimeMin`/`vo2max` all still present, and the deliberately-kept `factorsJson` intact. A deployed-bundle grep was tried first and rejected as an unsound smoke: the shared schema lives in a lazily-imported route chunk not linked from `index.html`, so the check found nothing even against the *pre-deploy* build and could never have distinguished before from after. Tier 🟢 Mid.

### 2026-08-16 — Batch 207 — One coach everywhere

288. **There is one coach and one conversation; a screen showing a read anchors that conversation to it rather than hosting a second one.** Implemented on `fix/batch-207-one-coach` after `/batch-start 207`, closing UX192-07 without a migration. **What was wrong:** `/brief` carried two chat affordances — an inline box headed "Ask about this read" beside a launcher headed "Ask about this morning's brief" — and membership ran one way only. The launcher set `analysisId` only when replying to a weekly review, and `BriefChatService.ask` resolves an anchor solely from an explicit `analysis_id`, never inferring one from origin, so a launcher question asked *while standing on the brief* was stored with `analysis_id = NULL` and could never appear in that brief's own chat, though inline questions appeared in both. The same split existed on Home and in the Week workout sheet: three inline views against one app-wide view, with two names for the same coach on one page. **Shape decision (Craig, 2026-08-16), chosen over two alternatives that are recorded so they are not re-litigated:** *one coach everywhere*. The three inline views and `BriefFollowUpChat` are deleted; the floating launcher is the only way to talk to the coach. Rejected: **anchor-by-route**, because the same button would behave differently depending on which screen Mark stood on; and **explain-the-relationship**, because it signposts the wrong-box trap rather than removing it. **The stated cost was avoided rather than paid.** Retiring the inline views would have discarded per-read context along with the confusion, so the useful half is kept: a screen showing a read registers it through the new `contexts/CoachAnchorContext`, and the launcher anchors that screen's questions to it. Asking "why so easy?" while looking at the brief still reaches the coach with that brief attached — there is simply only ever one box and one thread. The anchor *seeds* a question exactly as `originKind` already does; it never fences what Mark may ask, and an explicit reply to a weekly review still wins because that reply is to a message rather than to the page behind it. **Registrations are a stack, not a single value, and this was a real defect caught by its own test rather than a precaution.** A workout sheet opens over a page that has already registered its read; with a single value the sheet's unmount cleanup cleared the anchor while the page's effect — dependencies unchanged — never re-ran to restore it, leaving a visible read with no anchor. Keeping every live registration and reading the top entry means closing the sheet simply uncovers the page again. **Two consequences of removing the inline views:** Home's collapsed analysis anchors only while it is expanded, because a collapsed read is not what Mark is looking at; and the `timeZone` prop chain that existed solely to timestamp inline chat turns is now dead through four levels (`DayPlanBody` → `WorkoutRow` → `CompletedRideRead`, plus `WorkoutDetailSheet` → `CompletedWorkoutRead`) and was removed rather than left as plumbing to nowhere. `GET /api/v1/briefs/{analysisId}/messages` loses its only consumer but is deliberately left in place: it is authenticated, tested and harmless, and removing an endpoint is a separate concern from removing a UI surface. Frontend-only; no backend, schema, migration, prompt, verdict, plan, provider, auth or hosting change, and the rolling thread's storage and anchoring semantics are untouched. Local gates: `pnpm -r test` green (shared 26, web 386 across 61 files), web `tsc --noEmit` clean, ESLint 0 errors / 8 warnings — `CoachAnchorContext` joins `AuthContext` and `ThemeContext` in the existing provider-plus-hooks react-refresh warning — production build clean under Node 20. **Runtime check:** the dev server boots with the new provider in the tree with zero console errors and the app renders. A logged-in walkthrough of `/brief` was deliberately *not* run: it requires minting a production activation credential against the live API (the Batch 192 method, which then has to be revoked), which is disproportionate for a deletion whose three affected screens each have their own unit tests asserting the chat is gone and the read still renders. **Shipped via PR #240 / squash `cb9d6a7`:** rebased onto the Batch 206 merge (three doc conflicts — `DECISIONS.md`, `STATUS.md` and the ledger — all resolved keep-both; the ledger block spanned *two* rows, so taking main's side silently reverted this batch's own row to `Planned` and it had to be re-struck, which is why a resolution is worth re-reading rather than trusting). Local gates re-ran green on the rebased tree. All nine PR checks passed, as did post-merge `main` CI. Production verified on the exact merge SHA `cb9d6a735c3d865eafc6967ac249617ef8e49559` — Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health both served it, readiness `db: ok`, web `/` 200, protected `/api/v1/coach/messages` POST and `/api/v1/daily-loop` both 401 proxied. **The batch smoke was baselined against production *before* the merge**, per the lesson Batch 206 recorded: the pre-merge build served a lazily-loaded chunk literally named `BriefFollowUpChat-DNv5HFUG.js` containing the string `Ask about this read`, proving the check could detect the old state. After deploy: the index chunk hash changed (`index-z2JTNt5Q` → `index-ByUTmOiC`, so Vercel really rebuilt), lazy chunks went 70 → 69, **no chunk is named `BriefFollowUpChat`**, and `Ask about this read` appears in **0 of 69 chunks** against a baseline of 1. Tier 🟢 Mid.

### 2026-08-17 — Batch 211 — Coaching-integrity audit refresh

289. **A remediation is closed when it is demonstrated against real data, and the strength of that demonstration must be labelled rather than assumed.** Diagnose-only refresh on `chore/batch-211-integrity-refresh`, the third since the 2026-07-10 baseline. **Grade moved to A− from B+ — the first refresh where it moved** — because both of Batch 191's Highs are closed. **CI191-02 is closed and *observed*:** Batch 205 shipped 2026-08-16 15:25 UTC and exactly one wake sync has run since, on 2026-08-17; it wrote today's `morning` row and re-synced the closed days into `settled` rows without touching a wake row. The retained case is 2026-08-15, where the wake row reads `69/MODERATE` with a 1-minute recovery clock and the settled row reads `26/LOW` with 2,849 minutes — under the old single-row schema every retrospective consumer would by now read the second number for a morning the coaching called moderate. **CI191-01 is closed and *proved*, deliberately not called observed:** the decisive probe drives the real `_qualify_red_morning` with the physiology copied verbatim from the 2026-08-13 production packet's record of the 08-07 Red — `alcohol`, HRV 43 against its own floor of 45, resting HR 47 against its own ceiling of 45 — which the pre-Batch-194 rule classified `explained_by_check_in` and excluded, and which the current rule classifies `acute_cause_with_systemic_strain` and counts. All four of Batch 194.2's bounds are independently demonstrable rather than asserted: physiology may contradict the excuse, `training_load`/`deliberate_rest` never excuse at all (endogenous wins even when an acute tag is also present), the exclusion expires after a few days, and it is capped at one. It has not fired on a live morning because Mark has had no Red since 2026-08-08, which predates the fix; **that reservation is the only thing between A− and A**, and it cannot be forced honestly. **Labelling decision, which is the durable part:** every claim in the report is marked *observed* (a live morning did it), *proved* (the real function driven with real recorded inputs — no observation window needed) or *implemented* (code read only, weakest). Batch 191's kickoff rule said a remediation counts as closed only when observed working on real data; replaying a real recorded failure through the real current function satisfies that rule's intent better than waiting for chance to re-run it, but it is a different and weaker claim than watching it fire, and conflating the two would be the easiest way for this audit to start flattering itself. **CI191-08 closed:** all five deterministic protections are now in `coach_policy.FLOORS`, which carries ten floors each with a recognizer and a failing negative control (Batch 199). **The spoken layer has started speaking** — `state_change` turns 0 → 1, newest `weekly_review` 2026-06-29 → 2026-08-16, user turns 41 → 92 — so Batch 191's mitigating "it has barely spoken" no longer applies; it is now speaking into a materially better-guarded surface. **Verdict distribution reversed** across the four windows, 48% → 71% → 75% → **59% Green** (32 reads / 30 days: 19 Green, 5 Amber, 8 Red), which is the 194/199/201 hardening working rather than a regression. **One new finding, `CI211-01` (Low):** sixteen `workout_delivery_proposals` sit at `proposed` and every one is for a workout date already in the past (27 Jun → 13 Aug), none ever pushed, and no expiry path exists — graded Low rather than higher because the daily loop looks proposals up by `planned_workout_id`, so a stale row cannot surface against a current session; the remediation stub prefers a read-time derivation over a new writer, matching how Batch 144 handled the orphaned `generating` brief state. Diagnose-only: no product code, prompt, threshold, verdict logic, plan, proposal, migration, config or production row changed; every production query was read-only. **Gotcha reconfirmed for the third time:** `UpdatedAtMixin` sets `updated_at` with a `server_default` and no `onupdate`, so a row updated in place keeps its insert timestamp — it misled the reading of `daily_metrics` twice during this audit. Never use it to date a change. **Shipped via PR #241 / squash `e54f60f`:** all nine PR checks and post-merge `main` CI passed. Production verified on the exact merge SHA `e54f60f51bfc5144b30960c41c618dfff7edc8f4` — Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health both served it, readiness `db: ok`, web `/` 200, protected `/api/v1/daily-loop` 401 proxied. **The diagnose-only boundary is evidenced rather than asserted:** `git diff --name-only origin/main...` returned **zero** non-`.md` files before the merge, so there is no functional surface for a behavioural smoke to test — the health/readiness/route checks confirm the deploy is intact and unchanged, which is the whole claim for a review batch. Tier 🔴 High.

### 2026-08-19 — Batch 212 — The check-in classifier stops diagnosing illness from the weather

290. **Code that reads the user's own prose is coaching logic, not string handling — an over-broad pattern is a clinical claim about him, and any tag derived from his words must carry the words that produced it.** Implemented on `fix/batch-212-check-in-cause-classifier` after `/batch-start 212`, closing the 2026-08-14 report without a migration. **What was wrong, and it was worse than reported.** Mark's brief carried a line saying an illness context had been recorded and was pending human review with zero verdict impact; he had reported no illness, it appeared nowhere else in the read, and when he asked the coach directly it could not account for it. The cause is one expression: `_CHECK_IN_CAUSE_PATTERNS["illness"]` held `\b(?:head|chest\s+)?cold\b`, whose optional group made the qualifier optional, so the **bare word** matched. Driving the real dict against his verbatim notes returns `('illness',)` for both 2026-08-14 ("*more awake from this time as felt cold from drafts*") and 2026-08-15 ("*This backfired as too cold*"). He was describing his bedroom. The same expression was also broken in the direction it intended — only `chest` carried `\s+`, so "head cold" never matched that branch and reached the tag through the accidental bare word instead. **Two things found during the batch that the spec did not anticipate, both raising the severity.** First, the bogus tags **accumulate and persist**: production shows one illness row added on 08-14, a second on 08-15, and both still present on 08-16, inside the 28-day evidence window. Second, and the reason this is not merely narration noise — `illness` is in `_ACUTE_EXOGENOUS_CHECK_IN_CAUSES`, so a tag reaches `_qualify_red_morning`'s acute branch, and a Red morning whose physiology is *intact* is excused from the chronic cluster by it. Batch 194 bounds that (contradicting physiology still counts, endogenous tags win, cap of one, decay after a few days), so no live escalation was actually suppressed — but a wrong reading of one word in Mark's prose could have retired a genuine Red. **A second over-match was found by auditing the other four families rather than fixing only the reported one:** `\bdrank\b`/`\bdrinking\b` tag "drinking plenty of water", and hydration is the single most likely neighbouring subject in a recovery note. Both now decline an explicit non-alcoholic object; `\bsick\b` declines "sick of". The remaining three families (`travel`, `deliberate_rest`, `training_load`) are multi-word and specific, were audited at the same time and are deliberately unchanged. **Provenance, which is the durable half.** `RecordedTrainingContext` gains `matched_text`, and a check-in-derived row now emits `matchedText` plus a `basis` of "phrase matched in the check-in note". The reason the coach could not answer Mark is that the *reason the tag existed* — one word in his own note — was nowhere in the packet; carrying it means a tag can always be explained, and a wrong one recognised as wrong rather than merely denied. A `holiday_plan` row emits neither, because it comes from the plan rather than from prose. `classify_check_in_cause_matches` is the new single source of truth and `classify_check_in_causes` is its cause-only view, so the engine keeps consuming bare causes with an unchanged signature and no duplicated matching logic. This is a deliberate down-payment on Batch 217's packet-wide provenance convention. **The prompt gate.** Morning prompt guidance instructed the model to explain `chronicAction` and state that it is human-approved with `verdictImpact` none — so the model produced exactly that sentence, faithfully, on a morning when `triggered` was `false` and nothing had been excluded. Production confirms `triggered: false` on every day 08-13 → 08-16 while the context log was narrated regardless. The fix adds a gate rather than weakening the existing rule: when `chronicAction.triggered` is false **and** no Red was excluded, it is internal bookkeeping and must not be mentioned at all; every existing never-soften instruction still applies whenever it *is* doing something. The prompt also now tells the model that `matchedText` is Mark's own phrase, to quote it if he questions a tag, and to call a mismatch our recording error rather than defend it. **Boundary:** no migration, no verdict/threshold/ladder change, no plan, proposal or delivery change, no endpoint, shared-schema, frontend, auth or hosting change. **No production repair is needed and none was attempted, for a better reason than first recorded:** cause tags are *derived* at packet-assembly time from `manual_entries` (`_check_in_training_context`, and `classify_check_in_causes` inside the Red evidence build) and are never persisted to a table, so the two bogus illness rows vanish from the next brief generated after deploy rather than ageing out of the 28-day window. The historical `analyses.context_packet` snapshots keep them, which is correct — they are the record of what the coach actually said to Mark. **`PROMPT_VERSION` moves v30 → `morning-analysis-v31-2026-08-19`, and that is load-bearing rather than cosmetic:** it is the regeneration cache key (`existing.prompt_version == PROMPT_VERSION` short-circuits to `generated=False`), so editing the prompt text without bumping it would have served an already-generated pre-fix brief as current and left two materially different prompts sharing one version string. The repo's existing tripwire pins the version prefix so a bump forces review, but cannot catch a prompt edit that omits the bump; a general prompt-content guard is recorded under Future enhancements rather than built here. **Verification is old-versus-new, not merely green:** a test that passes proves nothing unless it would have failed before, so the pre-fix module was loaded alongside the new one and run over the same inputs — 08-14 `('illness',)` → `()`, 08-15 `('illness',)` → `()`, "drinking plenty of water" `('alcohol',)` → `()`, "sick of this weather" `('illness',)` → `()`, while "head cold", "caught a cold" and "drinking wine" are unchanged. Coverage: both verbatim notes as a parametrised regression, seven genuine illness phrasings, one negative control per cause family, an assertion that the hydration guard has not blunted real alcohol signal, and the packet contract for both row sources. The `matchedText` wiring is asserted in the existing DB-gated morning-packet test, which runs in CI. Gates: backend **861 passed / 362 expected PostgreSQL skips** (842 baseline plus 19), Ruff check + format clean across 237 files, mypy clean across 140 source files. **Shipped via PR #242 / squash `9f72e4f`:** all sixteen PR checks and post-merge `main` CI passed (**1224 passed, 0 skipped**). Production verified on the exact merge SHA `9f72e4fe9a02f228dceb805cc791946883bdf691` — Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health both served it, web `/` 200, protected `/api/v1/daily-loop` 401 proxied. **The batch smoke is a read-only introspection of the running container**, because a classifier change has no unauthenticated surface to probe and a green suite cannot prove what is deployed: the live container returns `()` for both of Mark's verbatim notes, `('illness',)` for "streaming head cold" and "caught a cold", `()` for "drinking plenty of water", `('alcohol',)` for "drinking wine", reports `PROMPT_VERSION = morning-analysis-v31-2026-08-19`, and confirms the new gate and the surviving never-soften rule are both present in the deployed prompt. Tier 🟢 Mid.

### 2026-08-19 — Batch 213 — The weekly mix counts sessions, not rows

291. **A version chain is scoped to one date, a skipped session is never a plan commitment, and a reducer must not trust the database to already be clean.** Built on `fix/batch-213-weekly-mix-sessions` after `/batch-start 213`, closing Mark's 2026-08-15 "VO2 has 1 of a 2-session target done" report without a migration. **Both facts in the spec were re-verified against code and the live database before building, per the standing `/batch-start` rule, and both held exactly as written.** Read-only exploration confirmed `summarize_weekly_mix` (`weekly_mix.py:204-207`) counted `target = len(in_bucket)` over `is_active=True` rows with no dedupe, and a direct query of the live 2026-08-11 chain confirmed the exact three-row shape the spec described: v1 `skipped`/`is_active=true`, v2 `planned`/`is_active=false`, v3 `completed`/`is_active=true` — both v1 and v3 active, doubling the VO2 target. 2026-08-13 and 2026-08-15 were confirmed correctly shaped (exactly one active row per date), matching 213.4's claim that they are right by luck rather than by rule. **Data-integrity fix.** The supersede gap is `ExecutableCoachingService.swap_day` (`executable_coaching.py`): it looks up the target date's occupant via `_active_workout_on`, whose query deliberately excludes `status == "skipped"` because a skipped row is not a valid swap partner. When the occupant *is* skipped, that lookup correctly returns `None` — but the code then only deactivated `workout` (the source) and `target_workout` (`None`), never the skipped row itself, leaving it `is_active=true` forever once a reslotted replacement landed on its date. Fixed by adding `_active_occupants_on`, an unfiltered superset query (any status, same category), and having `swap_day` deactivate every active occupant on both the source and target dates rather than only the two rows chosen as swap partners — closing the gap generally rather than patching the one observed shape. Confirmed by a subagent that every version-increment path (`plan_actions.py`'s `_next_version`, and `_reslot`'s inline `MAX(version) WHERE workout_date = ...`) scopes strictly to one date, so grouping by `workout_date` is a safe proxy for "one session slot" everywhere in this codebase, not an assumption specific to this fix. **Accounting fix, independent of the data fix by design** (213.3's "correct data should not be the only thing standing between Mark and a wrong denominator"): `MixSession` gains `skipped` and `version`, and `summarize_weekly_mix` now dedupes to one session per `workout_date` before counting — the highest-version *non-skipped* row wins; if every row for a date is skipped, that date contributes nothing to `target`, because a skipped session was never delivered and is not a plan commitment. This holds even for a lone skip with nothing superseding it, a deliberate reading of 213.3 rather than an accident: the target reflects what the plan currently commits to, not what it once scheduled and then skipped. **Live production repair**, per 213.2's explicit instruction not to hand-edit without an audit trail: a read-modify-write script run via `railway run` (the Batch 152 pattern) re-verified the ghost row's exact pre-repair shape immediately before writing (aborting if it had changed), flipped `is_active` false on the 2026-08-11 v1 row, and inserted a `coach.analyses` row (`analysis_type='workout_data_repair'`) recording the before/after state, the reason, and the method — queryable the same way every other plan-action audit row is. Verified post-write: 2026-08-11 now holds exactly one active row (v3, completed). **Boundary:** no migration, no verdict/threshold/ladder change, no endpoint, shared-schema, frontend, provider, auth or hosting change; the only production data touched is the one repaired row. **Verification is old-versus-new, not merely green:** the new dedupe/skip tests were run against the pre-fix `weekly_mix.py` via `git stash` and failed with `TypeError` (the old `MixSession` lacks the new fields) rather than passing vacuously, proving the tests exercise the fix rather than merely matching current behaviour. Coverage: the real 08-11 three-row chain as a pure-reducer fixture (`target=1, done=1`); a lone never-superseded skip (`target=0`); a duplicate-row stress case across all three buckets; a skip-then-reinstate lifecycle through `WeeklyMixService` end to end (DB-backed); a `swap_day` regression reproducing the exact 08-11 shape and asserting exactly one active row survives on the target date. Gates: backend **865 passed / 364 expected PostgreSQL skips** (862 baseline plus 3 pure-reducer tests; two further DB-gated regressions skip locally and will run in CI). Ruff check + format clean, mypy clean across 140 source files. Tier 🟢 Mid.

### 2026-08-19 — Batch 214 — A 12-second sprint is graded on evidence that exists

292. **A pause stops the workout but not the ride, so the planned clock and the trace clock are different clocks — and a deterministic grade the model cannot override is worse than a missing one, because the model will invent a mechanism to justify it.** Built on `fix/batch-214-sprint-grading` after `/batch-start 214`, closing Mark's 2026-08-13 report without a migration. **Two of the spec's five premises were wrong and the root cause was absent from it entirely; the ledger row was corrected before any code was written, per the standing `/batch-start` rule.** **What was actually wrong.** `_sample_time` segmented on `elapsed_sec` — Garmin's `sumElapsedDuration`, which counts paused seconds — while `_planned_windows` builds its cumulative clock from IR step durations, which are timer time. The 08-13 trace holds exactly one gap: at sample 755 `elapsed_sec` jumps 1508 → 1618 (**110 s**) while `moving_duration_sec` advances by 1. Every planned window after that pause therefore sat 110 s ahead of the ride. On a steady block this is invisible — 65% either side of every boundary — but the six 12-second sprints are the one place the target moves faster than the offset, so sprint window 1 landed back in the Z2 block and windows 2–6 landed in recovery valleys. **The result Mark was shown was not merely wrong, it was self-contradicting and confabulated.** The packet graded all six "under" at 54.1–64.7% FTP against a 185% target while recording the *recoveries between them* at 63.5–68.6% — nobody rests harder than they sprint. The model then supplied a physical mechanism that never happened: "the ERG trainer held the locked Z2 base load during the sprint windows", "ERG hadn't released before the boundary", "confirming ERG held base load", and told him the neuromuscular stimulus was "materially lighter than the prescription intended". Two sections later the same output reported "2.1% Z6 (sprint peaks)", computed from the whole trace and so immune to the bug. Garmin's own activity summary records `max_power_watts` **519 W**, and the trace holds six clean bursts peaking at 515 / 512 / 519 / 509 / 510 / 514 W — 182–185% of his 280 W FTP. He delivered every one. **Blast radius, measured rather than assumed: 31 of 57 rides since 1 May carry a pause over 30 s** (mean 135 s, max 501 s), including every structured ride in August — 08-19 (104 s), 08-16 (105 s), 08-15 (84 s), 08-13 (109 s), 08-09 (200 s). All were being graded against shifted windows; only the sprint ride made it visible. **The fix is four layers, each independently justified and each measured on the real trace.** (1) `_segmentation_clock` prefers `moving_duration_sec` when it is present and monotonic across the whole trace, falling back to elapsed time otherwise so a legacy or half-imported trace is never segmented on a partial column; the chosen clock and the pause length ride in the packet as `clockSource` / `pausedSec`. `_actual_lap_windows`' preference for a lap's `elapsedDuration` over `duration` flips with the clock, since pairing a wall-clock lap with a pause-excluding sample clock would reintroduce the same drift. (2) A work step at or below `PEAK_GRADED_MAX_DURATION_SEC` (30 s) is neuromuscular and grades on **peak** power, not window mean — a 12 s mean is dominated by the effort's own acceleration ramp — and such an effort can never be graded "over", because beating a sprint target is the point of a sprint. `maxPowerWatts` and `peakPctFtp` are new packet fields; a 515 W peak was previously unrepresentable, so Mark's argument could not be answered from the data model at all. (3) Because a planned-clock boundary can be out by as much as a short effort is long — a residual ~8 s remains on this ride even after the clock fix — a short effort's peak is searched either side of its nominal window by the step's own duration, clamped to the midpoint of each neighbouring step so it can never be graded on a neighbour's peak. Self-scaling rather than tuned: measured on the real trace the graded peaks are flat at 182–185% from ±6 s through ±30 s. (4) `_withhold_inconsistent_grades` removes any work grade that records below an adjacent recovery and records `gradeWithheldReason`; `summarize_execution` counts it as `ungradedCount` and carries `UNGRADED_INTERVAL_NOTE`, which instructs the model to say the app could not measure the effort and forbids offering any trainer, ERG, pacing or fatigue explanation for it. **The guard is a detector, not a suppressor:** on the real ride it fires on 6 of 6 sprints before the clock fix and 0 of 6 after. **`prescribedErgMode` was deliberately not populated, reversing the spec's phase 214.5.** Its premise — that Mark had to supply ERG himself — is false: `athleteProfile.indoorTrainerMode` already carries `mode: "erg"`, `prescribedPowerIsDelivered: true` and `neverSuggestErgOff: true`, and the prompt already instructs the model to treat prescribed %FTP as delivered. The model overrode all of it because the packet hard-stamped `adherence: "under"`; a deterministic fact beats a prompt instruction, which is the whole lesson. Populating the field would also have been actively harmful, since the value `"off"` triggers the existing `single_honest_note_when_erg_ridden_on_erg_off_surge_protocol` rule and would have had the coach tell Mark ERG smoothed surges that measurably were not smoothed. **Knock-on, caught by reading the consumer rather than by a test:** `detect_ride_deviation` reads `onTargetCount`/`overCount`/`underCount` and flags `easier_than_planned` when every work interval is under. A withheld grade is missing evidence, not evidence of a lighter ride, so the "all in one direction" branch now requires `ungradedCount == 0` — otherwise a segmentation failure on two of six sprints would be reported to Mark as a session he chose to ride easier. **The frontend was changed because the goal is not met in the packet alone:** the dashboard interval table rendered a 12-second sprint as "0 min", showed the window mean beside a 185% target, and rendered nothing at all for a withheld grade. It now renders sub-minute efforts in seconds, shows the number the grade was actually made on (peak and watts for a sprint), and shows a "Not measured" badge with a plain explanation. The five new interval fields are declared in `rideIntervalSchema`, without which zod would have stripped them before the screen ever saw them. `PROMPT_VERSION` moves v16 → `post-workout-analysis-v17-2026-08-19`, load-bearing as the regeneration cache key. **Boundary:** no migration, no verdict/threshold/ladder change, no plan, proposal or delivery change, no endpoint, provider, auth or hosting change, and **no production repair** — Craig's call at `/batch-start`, on the principle set in Batch 212 that a stored analysis records what the coach actually said and rewriting it destroys the evidence. **Verification is against real production data, not only fixtures:** the real 08-13 trace and IR were pulled read-only and replayed through the real `segment_ride_intervals`, reproducing the stored packet's 24 intervals value-for-value before the fix and grading all six sprints "on" at 183.9 / 182.9 / 185.4 / 181.8 / 182.1 / 183.6% after it. The new tests were also run against the pre-fix module: all seven fail, and the pause regression fails on substance — the old code passes its "before" assertions (so the fixture faithfully reproduces the bug) and fails the "after" one. **Also recorded because it rules out the obvious alternative fix:** the existing DTW rescue cannot save this ride. `_trace_aligned_windows` bails immediately because the planned clock's RMSE is 6.85 %FTP against an 8.0 threshold — a ride that is 97% steady-state looks well-fitted in aggregate while being catastrophically wrong on the 2% that matters — and forcing the DTW path past its bail-out was measured on both clocks, where it declines anyway. This is not a threshold-tuning problem. Gates: backend **873 passed / 364 expected PostgreSQL skips** (865 baseline plus 8), Ruff check + format clean across 237 files, mypy clean across 140 source files, `pnpm -r test` green (shared 27, web 388 against a 386 baseline), shared and web `tsc` clean, web lint 0 errors / 8 pre-existing warnings, production build clean under Node 20. **Shipped via PR #244 / squash `f92d6b7`:** all sixteen PR checks and post-merge `main` CI passed (**1237 passed, 0 skipped** against a 1224 baseline). Production verified on the exact merge SHA `f92d6b74d7de63066aca4fbc02dfa8951e4416e8` — Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health both served it, readiness `db: ok`, web `/` 200, protected `/api/v1/daily-loop` 401 direct and proxied. **The batch smoke is deliberately two-sided, because a pure-function fix has no unauthenticated surface and "the new code is deployed" is a weaker claim than "the new behaviour is deployed".** A read-only introspection of the running container (`railway ssh --service api`) drives the *deployed* `segment_ride_intervals` with Mark's real 08-13 shape and reports `clockSource=moving_duration_sec`, `pausedSec=110`, all six sprints `on` at 183.9% FTP on 515 W peaks, and `PROMPT_VERSION = post-workout-analysis-v17-2026-08-19`; the same script then runs a **wall-clock control** — the identical trace with the moving column removed — which returns six withheld grades, `ungradedCount=6 / underCount=0` and the ungraded note, so the consistency guard is demonstrated firing in production rather than merely being present in the image. On the frontend the baseline was captured **before** the merge, applying Batch 206's lesson that a bundle grep proves nothing unless it can see the old state: the pre-deploy `DashboardPage-DtPP2tqU.js` contained `Interval execution` once and all three new strings zero times. After deploy both hashes changed (`index-BU_lH9pf` → `index-D3-IK_yV`, `DashboardPage-DtPP2tqU` → `DashboardPage-C3dUEEEZ`, so Vercel did rebuild) and "could not place this window on the effort", "Not measured" and "% peak" each read 1. Tier 🔴 High.

### 2026-08-19 — Batch 215 — Red protects recovery without deleting the sleep benefit

293. **A Red morning does not mean one thing: sustained easy work is not a stimulus recovery needs protecting from, and a tool that pre-fills a different number from the one the read quoted is the app arguing with itself.** Built on `fix/batch-215-red-endurance` after `/batch-start 215`, closing Mark's 2026-08-08 report without a migration. **This reverses half of Decision #61 and is written as a new entry rather than an edit, because #61's Red shape was deliberate and the module docstring said so.** *What changed:* `adjust_ir_for_verdict` now splits Red on what the ride actually is. A ride whose hardest working step is already at or below `ENDURANCE_CEILING_PCT` holds its planned Zone-2 intensity and takes `RED_ENDURANCE_DURATION_SCALE = 0.85`; anything harder — and any day already carrying another session — takes the original recovery substitution unchanged, half duration capped at `RECOVERY_CAP_PCT`. *Why:* Mark's argument is physiological and the app had no counter to it. Sustained low-intensity work builds sleep pressure without the cortisol/sympathetic arousal harder sessions produce, so halving the *easy* ride on a Red morning works against the Red rule's own purpose — protecting recovery — and the sleep it costs is the thing recovery most depends on. The evidence that this was not being followed is unambiguous: across every Red morning in production with an endurance ride, Mark **never once** accepted the substitution (07-04 proposed 22 min, he rode the planned 45; 07-22 proposed 29, he rode 58; 08-08 proposed 22, he rebuilt it to 45 and rode that). The only Red substitution ever actually pushed, on 08-01, was a ride carrying 185% FTP work — the case the rule exists for, and the case that is deliberately unchanged. **The safety rail is untouched and is now unreachable by construction as well as by gate:** a ride carrying VO2 can never be classified endurance, so it always takes the substitution, and `blocks_red_vo2` plus the `never_vo2_on_red` policy floor still gate the push independently. **Two of the spec's premises were re-verified and held exactly; its central one did not, and correcting it moved the fix.** The row said the canonical transform "never ran" on 08-08 because the three `planned_workouts` rows carry no `origin`/`adjustment` — but those are IR-level annotations that live on proposals, and no `planned_workouts` row carries them on any day. The transform *did* run: proposal `54100516…` at 07:17:23 holds `origin: red_substitution`, `durationScalePct: 50`, `powerCapPct: 60`, 45 → 22 min, and the brief quoted it exactly. It was never approved or pushed. The 37 minutes Mark ended up with came from the **interval editor**, whose `scale_block` preset is verdict-blind — always the Amber rule, applied to the primary block only — and which the router returns as `changeTo` while the UI pre-selects it. On a Red morning the editor opened pre-filled at 36 minutes while the read said 22, and the read-only 5+5 ramps absorbed none of the cut. **Mark approved the number the app offered him.** So the editor's pre-filled block is now today's own verdict adjustment, and because it can only edit the primary block it absorbs the *whole* reduction and lands on the transform's total: 2295 s from both paths, where before they said 2295 and 2175. "Scale down" survives as an explicit generic preset. **Three shapes were Craig's call at `/batch-start`:** 0.85 over holding full duration (which would make a Red morning deliver a Green-looking session) or reusing Amber's ×0.75 (which would make Red and Amber identical for a Zone-2 ride); a verdict-aware editor *default* over a second button, since the default is what Mark actually approved; and resolving the combined-load fact at the call sites — which already load the day's plan — over widening the pure transform's signature, so `verdict_scaling` stays database-free and unit-testable. **`intensityHeldAtEndurance` now reads the outcome rather than the verdict's name**, because it was hardcoded to Amber and so reported `false` on 08-08 while the ride was already Zone 2 — the read had no way to say the intensity survived, because it had not. `PROMPT_VERSION` v31 → `morning-analysis-v32-2026-08-19`, load-bearing rather than a label: the morning regeneration identity is `(user, date, checkInVersion, promptVersion)` and does **not** hash the packet, so a packet-logic change without the bump would serve an already-generated pre-fix brief as current on the day this ships. The plan-adjustment instruction and the eased-ride copy now follow the transform instead of asserting a substitution that did not happen — Batch 214's lesson that a false deterministic fact is worse than a missing one, since the model will rationalise it rather than contradict it. **Verification is old-versus-new against real production data:** the pre-fix module, driven with Mark's real 08-08 workout, reproduces the stored proposal value-for-value (1350 s at 60%, and the editor's 2175 s), and every new packet/editor test fails against it on substance while `test_red_packet_still_substitutes_recovery_for_a_hard_ride` passes on both — the rail that must not move. No migration, no verdict-ladder change, no threshold change, no provider/auth/hosting change. **Shipped via PR #245 / squash `a651a96`:** all sixteen PR checks and post-merge `main` CI green (**1258 passed, 0 skipped** against a 1237 baseline; locally 891 / 367 expected PostgreSQL skips). Production verified on the exact merge SHA `a651a96330f8d2f0b18b222f8a589ed03d286e4b` — Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health both served it, readiness `db: ok` on both, web `/` 200, protected daily-loop 401 direct and proxied. **The batch smoke is two-sided, because a rule that only ever loosens is not the rule this batch claims to have shipped.** A read-only introspection of the running container drives the *deployed* transform with Mark's real 08-08 shape: `ir_is_endurance=True`, `origin=red_endurance_hold`, 2700 s → 2295 s (38 min) with the work step held at 62% and `intensityHeldAtEndurance: true`. The two controls both hold on the same deployed code — the same ride with a companion session returns `red_substitution`, 1350 s (22 min) at 60%, reproducing the pre-fix output value-for-value, and the 08-01 sprint shape returns `ir_is_endurance=False`, `red_substitution`, 185% → 60% with `blocks_red_vo2` True before the transform and False after. The deployed editor returns no `todays_adjustment` on an unknown verdict (falling back to the unchanged 36-minute generic preset) and 2295 s on Red, equal to the transform's own total. On the frontend both new strings — the preset label and the verdict provenance line — are present in the deployed `DashboardPage-BD78A-qr.js`, and the check's reach is validated rather than assumed: the pre-existing `Scale down` and `Keep current` labels resolve to that same chunk, so a positive result cannot be an artefact of a grep that never reached the editor (Batch 206's lesson, in the form available after the merge).

### 2026-08-20 — Batch 216 — A running local-day total needs the settled row

294. **A running local-day total needs the settled row, and "reuse an existing pattern" in a spec can point straight at the bug the batch exists to fix.** Built on `fix/batch-216-body-battery-charge-drain` after `/batch-start 216`, closing Mark's Body Battery report (asked twice: 08-15, 08-19) without a migration. **Shipped via PR #246 / squash `a8275d9`.** **The regression, confirmed exactly as the rewritten spec described:** `_metrics_vs_baselines` (`morning_analysis.py`) took a single `daily_metric` — post-Batch-205 the *morning-phase* row — and called `complete_body_battery_charged(daily_metric)`, which requires a complete local-day window (`daily_metric_coverage.py`) a 09:10 wake row can never have, so it returned `None` even though the same day's data existed (`body_battery_charged: 80` on the 08-19 morning row). `metric_baselines.sample_values` already had the fix on the baseline side — a `day_aggregates` parameter preferring the settled row — because Batch 205 documented the field-level exception there; `_metrics_vs_baselines` had no equivalent parameter at all, so the baseline rendered while the current value went blank. **The fix mirrors the existing pattern exactly rather than inventing a new one:** a new `_day_aggregate_metric` query (settled-row-first, same shape as the existing `_daily_metric` morning-row-first query) is threaded into `_metrics_vs_baselines` as an optional `day_aggregates` parameter, defaulting to the morning row when no settled row exists yet — the same fallback `sample_values` already uses. Recovery reads (readiness, RHR, HRV) are untouched and still read from the morning row; that split is Batch 205's whole point. **The audit found nothing else to fix:** `complete_body_battery_drained`, `complete_body_battery_end` and `complete_stress_avg` are the only siblings, and every other call site (`reviews.py`, `insights.py`, `_yesterday_load_packet`) already sources them from a settled/aggregate row — the defect was isolated to this one call site. **Drain added in the same commit, as specced:** `body_battery_drain` is now a ninth baseline metric (`BaselineMetricSpec` in `sleep_history.py`, `sample_values` in `metric_baselines.py`, `current_values` in `_metrics_vs_baselines`), sourced identically to charge; no migration, no historical backfill — `body_battery_drained` already exists and is populated on settled rows. The frontend `MetricComparisonTable` needed no code change — it renders whatever baseline rows the backend emits — but `body_battery_drain` is deliberately left out of both `HIGHER_IS_BETTER` and `LOWER_IS_BETTER`: a heavier or lighter day than normal has no health-desirable direction, so an out-of-band drain reads as a plain deviation rather than a false good/bad call, and the component comment records this as a deliberate exception rather than an oversight. **Tests:** a failing-first regression pinning the exact 08-19 shape (two `DailyMetric` rows, `phase="morning"`/`phase="settled"`, same date) through both the pure `_metrics_vs_baselines` function and the full `assemble_context_packet` integration path, asserting charge/drain come from the settled row while readiness stays on the morning row; `sample_values` and `compute_metric_baselines` coverage for the new drain key; the DB-backed backfill fixtures (`test_metric_baselines.py::_seed_day`) extended to populate drain so `test_rebuild_creates_db_history_baselines_with_cutoff` proves it backfills with a plausible mean. **Gates:** all sixteen PR checks and post-merge `main` CI green — backend 895 passed / 368 expected PostgreSQL skips (891/367 baseline; four new pure tests + one new DB-gated regression), Ruff check+format clean, mypy clean across 140 source files, `pnpm -r test` green (web 390, unchanged — only a doc comment touched on the frontend), web+shared `tsc` clean, web lint 0 errors/8 warnings, production build clean under Node 20. Production verified on the exact merge SHA `a8275d989a0c05eafbba8e934fbfba50b115d148` — Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health both served it, web `/` 200, protected daily-loop 401 direct and proxied. **The batch smoke is a read-only introspection of the running container** (`railway ssh --service api`), since the fix lives inside a pure packet-assembly function with no unauthenticated surface: the deployed `_metrics_vs_baselines` signature carries the new `day_aggregates` parameter, a synthetic morning-only pair returns `None` for both charge and drain (a partial total correctly withheld), and adding the settled row returns `80`/`64` for charge/drain while readiness stays `68` from the morning row — the exact 08-19 shape, reproduced on the deployed code. **216.6's baseline backfill run at closeout, once the deployed code carried the new key:** `railway run --service api .venv/bin/python -m src.metric_baselines_backfill`, dry-run first (created 1 / updated 8 / unchanged 0, as expected), then for real. Verified read-only afterward: `body_battery_drain` landed with `sample_count=83`, `mean=64.6`, `median=67`, quartiles `57.5–75.0` — matching the spec's expected ~63.8 mean exactly, so Mark's flagged drain of 60 now has a real personal range to sit against.

### 2026-08-23 — Batch 217 — Facts arrive with their basis, so the coach can explain itself

295. **A basis the coach cannot say out loud is not a basis, and where none exists the model will invent one and keep it.** Built on `feat/batch-217-fact-basis` after `/batch-start 217`, closing the explainability half of Mark's 2026-08-14/15/20 challenges. **The row was corrected before any code, and the correction was larger than on any batch so far.** Two of its four covered items had already been annotated by the batches it told us to follow — Batch 212 ships `matchedText` + `basis` on the check-in cause tags, Batch 214 ships `gradeBasis` / `gradeWithheldReason` / `boundarySource` on every interval — so only the weekly-mix targets were still bare. And **217.2 asked us to establish a convention that already existed four times**: `trainingWeekSoFar.grounding` (`training_week.py:307`), 212's `basis`, 214's `gradeBasis`, and `chat_context._state_meaning`. All four are already plain readable sentences, which is the property that matters. The batch therefore *names* the convention in `coach_policy.py` — the module whose stated job is to be the one policy surface for everything the coach says — rather than adding a fifth copy of it. **`confidence` was dropped (Craig's call at `/batch-start`).** It appears zero times in project source, as the row said, but the app's honest answer to uncertainty already exists in two better forms: withhold (214's `gradeWithheldReason`) and quantify (216's `sampleCount` / `excludedSampleCount` / `reliabilityStartDate`). A categorical `"low"` beside a real sample count is a label competing with data, and Batch 214's own lesson is that a false fact in the packet is worse than a missing one. **The root cause was absent from the row and is the reason the correction mattered: a basis can be present and unquotable.** On 2026-08-20 Mark asked *"What was the basis of the 23:15 sleep protocol target as don't recall setting it?"* The stored packet for that morning **held the answer** — `knowledgeBase.sections[sleep_protocol].source = "batch_5_seed"` — and the coach replied that it would be speculating. It was reading an internal build token that `NO_PLUMBING_RULE` correctly forbids it from repeating to him. The same shape sits on 148 `planned_workouts` rows across six enums. So the convention is not *carry a basis* but **carry a sentence Mark can read**, and `INTERNAL_SOURCE_BASIS` returns `None` rather than the token when it meets a value it does not know — silence over a leak, because a `basis` field holding an enum would look answered while answering nothing. **The second missing root cause is the highest-value line in the batch: when the app cannot explain a fact, the model invents a mechanism, and the invention persists.** Challenged on the illness tag on 2026-08-14 the coach volunteered that Garmin's health snapshot had flagged a skin-temperature deviation — entirely fabricated, asserted twice, endorsing Mark's own wrong guess — and **carried it into an unrelated answer the next day**. Batch 214 recorded the same failure independently on an ERG rationalisation. `GROUNDING_RULE` forbids inventing Mark's metrics, plan, history, readiness or prescription; **nothing forbade inventing the app's own reasoning**, which is the half he was asking about both times. `no_invented_derivation` is now a `Floor`, so the conversation composes it from the registry and the morning and post-workout reads are audited for it. It is scoped to those two reads for the same reason `never_vo2_on_red` is: a walk read derives nothing and prescribes nothing. **Verified old-versus-new against the real stored production packet Mark was arguing with.** Run over the 2026-08-15 packet the registry reports all four covered facts unexplained — 22 objects, zero with a basis, `target: 2` bare and `source: batch_5_seed` on the sleep protocol; re-annotated with the new code the same real data reports nothing missing and answers his question in his own terms. Decision recorded because the sequencing rule in the row (*land after 212-215*) turned out to change what the batch should build, not merely when. **Shipped via PR #247 / squash `ba41918`.** All sixteen PR checks and post-merge `main` CI green (**1283 passed, 0 skipped** against a 1263 baseline; locally 914 / 369 expected PostgreSQL skips). Production verified on the exact merge SHA `ba41918d149ed20eb9121362e5905012128593a3` — Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health both served it, readiness `db: ok` on both, web `/` 200, protected daily-loop 401 direct and proxied. The batch smoke is a read-only introspection of the running container: the deployed `source_basis` fails closed on an unknown token, `facts_missing_basis` still reports an internal token used *as* a basis (the exact 08-20 shape), the deployed `summarize_weekly_mix` answers Mark's 08-15 question on the real 08-11 chain, and all three prompts report their new versions with the floor present and owned by exactly the morning and post-workout reads; `WeeklyMixCard-TedKX0de.js` carries the new disclosure string. **No production repair** — a stored analysis records what the coach actually said (Batch 212's principle).

### 2026-08-24 — Batch 218 — Chat prompt caching

296. **Prompt caching is explicit and chat-only, with the cache breakpoint before live app state.** Built on `feat/batch-218-chat-prompt-caching` after `/batch-start 218`, closing the latency half of repeated coach-chat turns without a migration. The shared Anthropic boundary still accepts the original bare `system_prompt: str` for every non-chat caller, but now also accepts Anthropic system text blocks so chat can place `cache_control: {"type": "ephemeral"}` on exactly the stable prefix. **Boundary choice:** cache `SYSTEM_PROMPT` plus the anchored read description/output and stored read packet, then send `Where things stand right now` as an uncached second system block. That is deliberate: the frozen read packet is the expensive repeated context, while Batch 178's live `app_state` exists specifically so a question can see plan/activity changes made after the read. Deterministic serialization (`sort_keys`) is asserted for both stored packets and current app-state JSON so insertion order cannot silently invalidate the prefix. Anthropic usage logging now includes `cache_read_input_tokens`, because a non-zero second-turn read is the only real proof the optimization is working. Re-verification corrected two stale row facts: current files live under `apps/api/src/services/...`, not `apps/api/src/coach/services/...`, and today's tree has eight non-chat `generate_anthropic_text` callers, not seven. Local gates: backend 917 passed / 369 expected PostgreSQL skips; Ruff check + format clean; mypy clean across 140 source files. Shipped via PR #248 / squash `02cd7a2`; deployed smoke confirmed a real repeated Anthropic call created 18,002 cache tokens on turn 1 and read 18,002 on turn 2 (`cache_read_input_tokens=18002`).

### 2026-08-24 — Batch 219 — Nightly environment signals

297. **A night's controllable setup belongs to its wake date as one structured object; circular weather may be stored without pretending it is a linear driver.** Built on `feat/batch-219-nightly-environment-signals` after `/batch-start 219`, before the longitudinal analyst that depends on it. Bedding, window count/aperture, blind position and local pre-cool start describe one evolving setup, so they live together in `manual_entries.sleep_setup_json` rather than becoming five sparsely populated columns. The row's `entry_date` is the morning the night ended, matching the existing sleep, weather and bedroom-driver join; a legacy client omitting the new object preserves what a newer client stored, while an explicit empty object clears it. Open-Meteo wind direction is reduced with a circular vector mean—350° plus 10° is north, not south—and persisted with mean relative humidity on `weather_daily`. Direction is deliberately excluded from the existing Pearson engine: raw compass degrees have a discontinuity at north and a scalar correlation would report mathematical artefacts as physiology. Humidity and bedroom mean/min/max temperatures are valid scalar drivers and do enter that engine. REM and awake outcomes use the stored stage durations in minutes, preserving the raw measurement for later banding rather than baking a derived percentage into the deterministic layer. The fields also enter the morning context packet and shared API schemas so Batch 220 can reason over them without another plumbing pass. No prompt/provider change and no Claude spend are introduced by this batch. **Shipped via PR #249 / squash `2496db2`; the production smoke then found the exact-north rounding edge, fixed and pinned via PR #250 / squash `2c0b3d7`.** Final PostgreSQL CI passed 1287 tests, and production on exact SHA `2c0b3d7f501e12cd8028ff84bb9a8a291cb469b1` reports Alembic `029`, all three live columns, the registered setup/drivers/outcomes, and 350° plus 10° as canonical `0.0`.
