### 2026-08-24 — Batch 220 — Monthly longitudinal analyst

298. **Longitudinal reasoning is a durable, schema-constrained batch process whose evidence policy can overrule its model output.** Built on `feat/batch-220-longitudinal-analysis` after `/batch-start 220`, using Anthropic Message Batches directly rather than widening the latency-sensitive text boundary or adding an agent loop. The packet is deterministic evidence, not a conclusion: 427 nights in a compact columnar projection plus raw nightly measurements, Mark's notes and 1°C descriptive bands. The exact production packet measures 115,541 bytes and 55,477 input tokens; its batch-priced input leg is about $0.083 and the 4,096-token output cap holds the request ceiling near $0.114. Findings arrive through `output_config.format` JSON Schema and are validated again with Pydantic after retrieval; each carries observation, confidence, evidence status, confounds, reachability and an experiment or data-quality proposal where applicable. Provider-incompatible schema constraints are removed only from the provider projection, never from local validation. **The model does not get the last word on causal evidence.** Temperature remains forced to `inconclusive` / low confidence until coverage reaches 21 bedroom nights, 21 structured-setup nights and three temperature bands with at least five nights each. Production currently has 61 bedroom nights but zero structured-setup nights, so historical prose stays contextual evidence and is never silently promoted to a controlled lever. Unreachable warm-night targets are explicitly non-actionable. The monthly paid submission has one idempotent identity; a frequent collector only polls an existing batch, persists pending/completed/failed `longitudinal_findings` analyses, and routes the temperature finding exactly once as an audited observation on `early_waking_0400`. Other structured findings remain in the analysis record rather than mutating durable knowledge silently. **Spend fails closed on a distinct human operator.** Mark can never receive the billing alert or authorize the spend path; `ADMIN_ALERT_USER_ID` must resolve to a different active profile with an active push subscription before submission. Production has the empty operator profile but not yet its device subscription, and the deployed smoke proved the gate returns `admin_billing_alert_not_ready` with zero submissions and zero analysis writes. Shipped via PR #251 / squash `9cdf7f7`; the smoke exposed an ORM-expiry-after-rollback bug, fixed and pinned against PostgreSQL via PR #252 / squash `c0f9451`. Final `main` CI passed 1306 tests and exact-SHA production freshness is green on `c0f94511ffd733954d3427259a4749eb4dbba910`.

---

### 2026-08-24 — Batch 221 — The experiment loop closes

299. **An intervention becomes evidence only when the exact issued action, the user's explicit application and the app's measured outcome join on the same night.** Built on `feat/batch-221-experiment-loop` after `/batch-start 221`. Nightly observations are forward-only, idempotent and source-owned, so the deterministic loop cannot overwrite a human entry or Batch 220's longitudinal finding. The morning packet recomputes current recommendations through the existing evaluator rather than treating an old `analyses` audit as current state; the latest audit is provenance only, and the existing human conclusion action remains the sole terminal-status write. **Application is structured user-owned evidence.** Migration `030` adds `manual_entries.rem_intervention_feedback_json`; `unknown` is not `not_applied`, missing outcomes are excluded, and prose is never mined to infer compliance. A fourth standing experiment, `rem_intervention_rotation`, owns the protocol. Its immutable weekly assignment uses stable library IDs and the exact rendered copy shown to Mark, protected by a transaction advisory lock. A first assignment begins on the actual issue date—even midweek—and ends Sunday; a Monday check-in therefore joins Sunday night to the prior assignment, not Monday's new rotation. Displaying a chronic action is itself issuance, so Daily Loop persists before showing and never fabricates a historical assignment. The REM evaluator compares applied with not-applied nights only after each action has at least three of each, reports REM and awake-time summaries plus observational confounds, and returns only a recommendation; it cannot conclude the experiment. The three original experiments keep their shipped evaluators but now accumulate per-wake collagen, recovery-block and early-waking/environment evidence. Morning prompt v34 surfaces actionable evaluations, explains underpowered ones, and forbids asking Mark to observe REM or awake-time facts already measured by Garmin; only application evidence may be requested from him. **Shipped via PR #253 / squash `f976d13`.** Local backend passed 939 tests with 376 expected PostgreSQL skips; web/shared passed 394/30, static/build and browser-accessibility gates passed; final PostgreSQL CI passed 1315 tests with zero skips. Production freshness and Railway/Vercel health are green on exact SHA `f976d1307f1f5fea7ee5460ebaca3c5ae5a39dda`. A rollback-only deployed smoke exercised migration `030`, assignment, all four nightly writes and packet evaluation, then proved row counts returned unchanged; no automatic historical backfill or smoke data was persisted.

---

### 2026-08-24 — Batch 222 — The check-in must beat the sync honestly

300. **A morning analysis is not evidence that the morning inputs synced, and a materially more complete packet is a new paid-request identity rather than an excuse to serve the old read.** Built on `fix/batch-222-checkin-sync-race` after `/batch-start 222`, closing the 2026-08-20 race. The design call is sync-then-generate in the existing Batch 97 off-request task: it reuses the idempotent morning input helper, then refuses the model call unless the wake date has a morning-phase `DailyMetric` and—before the 11:00 backstop—a `Sleep` row. The full backstop applies the same hard daily-metric gate, so even a total provider failure cannot create another authoritative no-data brief. A morning `DailyMetric` is a deliberate durable sync marker because `sync_daily` writes it on every successful full Garmin pull, even when Garmin returns no sleep session; missing sleep remains provisional before 11:00, so wake polling continues, while daily-without-sleep after the cutoff is an honest watch-not-worn/no-session result and polling terminates. `run_wake_check` therefore never consults `latest_analysis`: the real 07:19:41 first sighting, 07:26:55 check-in/empty read and 07:34:39 stable fire ordering now reaches `run_morning_sync` despite the intervening analysis. **Completeness is low-cardinality provenance, not a hash of health values.** `morning_generation_identity` adds `daily_metrics:0|1` and `sleep:0|1`; the same version is stamped in the packet. Stored pre-Batch-222 packets remain comparable by deriving those bits from their existing `dailyMetrics`/`sleep` keys. A scheduler read reuses the latest prompt-version analysis only when those bits still match; empty→complete generates a second row and leaves the first as history, matching the deliberate 2026-08-20 repair rather than rewriting it. Home and Sleep derive the same two-row readiness from the Daily Loop payload: only both present may render "your overnight data's already in"; otherwise the shared hero and morning action promise the sync the backend now performs. No migration, prompt, deterministic-verdict, plan, provider, auth or hosting change. Local gates: backend 942 passed / 380 expected PostgreSQL skips, Ruff/format/mypy clean; shared/web 30/396, typecheck/build/lint clean aside from eight pre-existing warnings; Chromium rendered-accessibility and synced/unsynced flow checks passed. The new PostgreSQL cases cover sync-before-generate, no-input refusal, empty→complete historical regeneration, exact real-time wake ordering and genuine-input polling termination.
